### [1] [PersistGS: Differentiable Physics for Object Permanence in 4D Gaussian Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03479)
*Adrian Ramlal,John S. Zelek*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: PersistGS combines 3D Gaussian Splatting with differentiable rigid-body physics so occluded objects keep moving in physically correct ways even when no camera sees them, yielding much better reconstruction than constant-velocity baselines.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: In dynamic 3DGS, when an object is fully occluded from all training cameras, photometric supervision disappears and the corresponding Gaussians receive no gradient, causing degradation. Prior solutions typically use generative priors that favor plausible appearance rather than physically correct motion. The paper aims to preserve physically consistent object states through occlusion.

Method: The method decomposes a dynamic scene into per-object Gaussians and collision meshes, estimates object friction and velocity from the visible pre-occlusion trajectory with differentiable rigid-body simulation, and then uses the simulated SE(3) trajectory to place object Gaussians during occlusion. It also introduces a centroid silhouette loss to provide cleaner positional supervision than raw photometric loss.

Result: On synthetic scenes evaluated with withheld cameras that still observe the occluded object, PersistGS reduces trajectory error by 40% relative to photometric supervision, outperforms constant-velocity extrapolation by 2.46 dB PSNR, and comes within 0.19 dB of a ground-truth-trajectory upper bound.

Conclusion: PersistGS restores object permanence for fully occluded moving objects in dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting by integrating differentiable rigid-body simulation, achieving substantially better trajectory and rendering accuracy than simple kinematic extrapolation and approaching a ground-truth upper bound.

Abstract: Dynamic 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) methods reconstruct time-varying scenes from synchronized multi-camera video using photometric supervision. When a moving object becomes fully occluded from all training cameras, this supervision vanishes: the Gaussians representing it receive no gradient signal and degrade. Existing approaches to incomplete observations in neural reconstruction rely on learned generative priors that prioritize visual plausibility over physical correctness.
  We propose $\textbf{PersistGS}$, a method that restores object permanence during occlusion by coupling differentiable rigid body simulation with 3D Gaussian Splatting. Our approach decomposes the scene into per-object Gaussians and collision meshes, estimates friction and velocity from the observed pre-occlusion trajectory via differentiable simulation, and uses the resulting SE(3) trajectory to position object Gaussians throughout the occlusion period. Because the predicted trajectory satisfies the governing equations of rigid body dynamics, it faithfully captures contact events (bounces, friction-based deceleration, direction changes) that kinematic extrapolation cannot model. We introduce a centroid silhouette loss that isolates positional gradients from appearance noise, yielding 40% lower trajectory error than photometric supervision. We evaluate using cameras withheld from training that observe the object during its occlusion. Experiments on synthetic scenes show that PersistGS outperforms constant velocity extrapolation by +2.46dB PSNR and comes within 0.19dB of a ground-truth trajectory upper bound.

</details>


### [2] [COD10K-C: Benchmarking Robustness of Camouflaged Object Detection Under Natural Image Corruptions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02603)
*Arafat Hossain Sayem*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 作者提出了 COD10K-C，用于系统评估伪装目标检测在真实图像损坏下的鲁棒性，并证明现有模型普遍脆弱；轻量模型 RobustCODLite 通过数据增强、频域先验和一致性约束取得了更强鲁棒性。 / The paper introduces COD10K-C to systematically evaluate robustness of camouflaged object detection under realistic corruptions, shows current models are broadly fragile, and demonstrates that RobustCODLite improves robustness through augmentation, frequency priors, and consistency learning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有伪装目标检测基准主要基于干净图像，未反映真实相机中常见的模糊、噪声、天气和压缩伪影，因此缺少对模型实际部署鲁棒性的系统评估。 / Existing COD benchmarks focus on clean images and fail to reflect real-world degradations such as blur, noise, weather effects, and compression artifacts, leaving deployment robustness insufficiently evaluated.

Method: 基于 COD10K 构建了 COD10K-C 鲁棒性基准，包含 8 类损坏、5 个严重度，共 40 种条件与 81,040 个评测对；在该基准上评测 SINet-v2、PFNet、ZoomNet 与轻量模型 RobustCODLite。RobustCODLite 结合了 corruption augmentation、frequency-prior branch 和 uncertainty-consistency loss。 / The paper builds COD10K-C from COD10K with 8 corruption types and 5 severity levels, yielding 40 conditions and 81,040 evaluation pairs; it evaluates SINet-v2, PFNet, ZoomNet, and a lightweight model, RobustCODLite. RobustCODLite combines corruption augmentation, a frequency-prior branch, and an uncertainty-consistency loss.

Result: 所有模型在损坏图像上都出现显著性能下降；运动模糊和高斯模糊影响最大，SINet-v2 在运动模糊下 Dice 下降 18.5 点。亮度和雾影响较小。RobustCODLite 在损坏条件下保留了 92.3% 的干净图像 Dice，优于 SINet-v2 的 87.7%、ZoomNet 的 84.8% 和 PFNet 的 84.1%；在最困难的损坏上，它可达到或超过一些在干净数据上更强的模型。 / All models suffer clear degradation on corrupted images; motion blur and Gaussian blur are the most damaging, with SINet-v2 losing 18.5 Dice points under motion blur. Brightness and fog are less harmful. RobustCODLite retains 92.3% of its clean Dice under corruption, outperforming SINet-v2 (87.7%), ZoomNet (84.8%), and PFNet (84.1%), and matches or exceeds stronger clean-image models on the hardest corruptions.

Conclusion: 现有伪装目标检测模型在图像损坏场景下鲁棒性明显不足；新提出的 RobustCODLite 在多种退化条件下具有更稳定的性能，COD10K-C 可作为该方向的标准鲁棒性评测基准。 / Existing camouflaged object detection models are insufficiently robust under image corruptions; the proposed RobustCODLite is more stable across degraded conditions, and COD10K-C can serve as a standard robustness benchmark for this area.

Abstract: Camouflaged object detection has improved substantially, but most standard benchmarks evaluate models only on clean images. This is not realistic because real cameras often capture blur, sensor noise, weather effects, and compression artifacts. We present COD10K-C, a corruption robustness benchmark based on COD10K. It includes 8 corruption types and 5 severity levels, giving 40 conditions and 81,040 evaluation pairs in total. We evaluate three popular camouflaged object detection models, SINet-v2, PFNet, and ZoomNet, as well as a lightweight model called RobustCODLite. All models show clear performance drops on corrupted images. Motion blur and Gaussian blur cause the largest drops, with SINet-v2 losing 18.5 Dice points under motion blur. Brightness and fog are less harmful. RobustCODLite uses corruption augmentation, a frequency-prior branch, and an uncertainty-consistency loss. It retains 92.3% of its clean Dice score under corruption, compared with 87.7% for SINet-v2, 84.8% for ZoomNet, and 84.1% for PFNet. On the hardest corruptions, RobustCODLite matches or outperforms models that perform better on clean data. We will release the COD10K-C GitHub repository to support future research in robust camouflaged object detection.

</details>


### [3] [AvatarMix: Identity-Preserving Cross-Avatar Composition for Outfit Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03506)
*Zhaorong Wang,Yoshihiro Kanamori,Yuki Endo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: AvatarMix transfers outfits in 3D by directly mixing a source body avatar with a target head avatar, then fixing the seam and restoring appearance when needed. This avoids clothing intersections, preserves identity better, and achieves state-of-the-art realism.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior 3D avatar outfit transfer methods either lift 2D edits into 3D and suffer identity/outfit degradation, or model body and clothing separately and produce intersection artifacts. The paper aims to preserve outfit quality and user identity while avoiding these artifacts.

Method: The method composes the head and body from two Gaussian avatars, reshapes the clothed body with a mesh-based Gaussian representation and mesh retargeting, and applies a two-tier refinement strategy: SeamFix for local hair/neck joining and FullbodyFix for optional full-body appearance restoration after retargeting.

Result: Extensive experiments show state-of-the-art performance in outfit fidelity and identity preservation, with robust handling of diverse body shapes and fewer multi-view artifacts than 2D-to-3D lifting approaches.

Conclusion: AvatarMix introduces a compositional 3D avatar outfit transfer paradigm that directly combines high-fidelity head and body Gaussian avatars, then refines the seam and optionally the full body. It achieves strong outfit fidelity and identity preservation while avoiding common degradation and intersection artifacts.

Abstract: Existing 3D avatar outfit transfer methods face distinct challenges: approaches that lift 2D edits to 3D often suffer from outfit or identity quality degradation, while those that separately model body and clothing layers are prone to intersection artifacts. We introduce AvatarMix, a compositional paradigm that bypasses these issues by directly composing the head and body from two high-fidelity Gaussian avatars. While this paradigm inherently preserves outfit quality and avoids intersections, it introduces challenges in creating a seamless join and maintaining appearance fidelity after body reshaping. To this end, we propose a two-tier refinement strategy: SeamFix, a localized diffusion module that refines hair and neck to ensure an artifact-free join, and an optional full-body refinement, FullbodyFix, that restores garment appearance when retargeting degrades the clothed body. Both operate on renders from an already 3D-consistent Gaussian avatar, which limits multi-view artifacts compared to 2D-to-3D lifting. To preserve the user's body identity, our mesh-based Gaussian representation enables the adaptation of a robust mesh retargeting technique, precisely reshaping the clothed body to the user's physique and robustly handling diverse body shapes. Extensive experiments demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art results in outfit fidelity and identity preservation, providing a new perspective for realistic 3D outfit personalization. Project page: https://larsph.github.io/avatarmix/

</details>


### [4] [AVTrack: Audio-Visual Tracking in Human-centric Complex Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02724)
*Yaoting Wang,Yun Zhou,Zipei Zhang,Henghui Ding*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper presents AVTrack, a challenging new dataset for audio-visual speaker tracking in realistic dynamic scenes, shows that current methods perform much worse on it, and offers a baseline for future work.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing audio-visual speaker tracking datasets are too simple, homogeneous, and coarsely annotated, which favors static audio-visual co-occurrence instead of testing true spatiotemporal modeling and cross-modal reasoning in complex scenes.

Method: The paper introduces AVTrack, a human-centric audio-visual instance segmentation dataset for speaker tracking, built with diverse dynamic conditions such as camera motion, occlusion, and speaker position changes, and benchmarks representative AVIS methods on it while providing a simple baseline.

Result: Representative AVIS methods show substantial performance degradation on AVTrack, demonstrating the dataset's difficulty and highlighting the need for more robust methods for complex human-centric audio-visual scene understanding.

Conclusion: AVTrack fills a key benchmark gap for audio-visual speaker tracking in realistic dynamic scenes and exposes that current AVIS methods are not robust enough under challenging real-world conditions.

Abstract: Audio-visual speaker tracking aims to localize and track active speakers by leveraging auditory and visual cues, enabling fine-grained, human-centric scene understanding. This capability is essential for real-world applications such as intelligent video editing, surveillance, and human-computer interaction. However, existing datasets are largely limited to simple or homogeneous audio-visual scenes with coarse annotations. Such oversimplified settings bias evaluation toward static audio-visual co-occurrence, rather than rigorously assessing robust spatiotemporal modeling and cross-modal reasoning in complex, dynamic scenes. To address these limitations, we introduce AVTrack, a human-centric audio-visual instance segmentation (AVIS) dataset designed for dynamic real-world scenarios. AVTrack features diverse and challenging conditions, including camera motion, visual occlusions, and position changes. Evaluations of representative AVIS methods on AVTrack reveal substantial performance degradation, establishing AVTrack as a challenging benchmark for robust human-centric audio-visual scene understanding in complex environments. We further provide a simple yet effective baseline to facilitate future research. Project website: https://FudanCVL.github.io/AVTrack/

</details>


### [5] [Qwen-Image-Flash: Beyond Objective Design](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03746)
*Tianhe Wu,Kun Yan,Zikai Zhou,Lihan Jiang,Jiahao Li,Jie Zhang,Kaiyuan Gao,Ningyuan Tang,Shengming Yin,Xiaoyue Chen,Xiao Xu,Yilei Chen,Yuxiang Chen,Yan Shu,Yixian Xu,Yanran Zhang,Zihao Liu,Zhendong Wang,Zekai Zhang,Deqing Li,Liang Peng,Yi Wang,Jingren Zhou,Chenfei Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper studies few-step distillation beyond loss design, showing that data mix, teacher guidance, and task mixture strongly affect student quality; these insights lead to Qwen-Image-Flash.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior few-step distillation research has mostly optimized distillation objectives, while the effect of the training recipe is less explored. The paper aims to understand which recipe choices critically shape student performance in advanced visual generative models.

Method: Using Qwen-Image-2.0 as a case study, the authors systematically study few-step distillation for unified text-to-image generation and instruction-guided image editing. They vary three training-recipe factors: data composition, teacher guidance, and task mixture, and analyze the resulting behaviors empirically.

Result: The experiments reveal several non-obvious behaviors across the three factors. These findings motivate the design of Qwen-Image-Flash and show that careful pipeline organization can materially improve few-step distillation.

Conclusion: Few-step distillation is not determined by the objective alone; the broader training recipe matters substantially. The work argues that principled choices over data composition, teacher guidance, and task mixture are necessary to obtain strong student models, leading to Qwen-Image-Flash.

Abstract: Few-step distillation has become an effective strategy for accelerating advanced visual generative models, yet prior work has largely focused on distillation objectives. In this work, we revisit few-step distillation from a complementary perspective, focusing on the training recipe that critically shapes student performance. Using Qwen-Image-2.0 as a representative case, we systematically investigate three factors in unified text-to-image generation and instruction-guided image editing distillation: data composition, teacher guidance, and task mixture. Our empirical analysis reveals several non-obvious behaviors, which motivate the development of Qwen-Image-Flash. Overall, our results suggest that effective few-step distillation requires not only carefully designed objectives, but also principled organization of the broader training pipeline.

</details>


### [6] [Consistent Yet Wrong: Evidence Insensitivity in Spatial Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02742)
*S Divakar Bhat,Toshihiko Yamasaki*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ViewDiag shows that many VLMs are consistently wrong on spatial distance queries: they give stable answers across views without real geometric understanding. The benchmark diagnoses this failure beyond plain accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Spatial reasoning is crucial for robotics and embodied AI, but current VLMs are unreliable on metric distance estimation. The paper investigates whether cross-view consistency truly indicates geometric grounding, a common but insufficiently tested assumption.

Method: The paper introduces ViewDiag, a controlled multi-view evaluation protocol built from Hypersim, ScanNet, and KITTI360. It evaluates 176 object-pair tracks across 80 scenes with 2–10 views per track using three diagnostics: metric accuracy, distributional concentration, and a latent feature probe to separate decision collapse from representation collapse.

Result: Across diverse VLMs, the authors find a consistent regime of high prediction stability but low metric accuracy. Models often remain confidently consistent across viewpoints even when incorrect, and latent probing helps reveal internal collapse patterns.

Conclusion: Cross-view consistency is not a reliable proxy for geometric understanding in vision-language models. VLMs can produce stable, view-invariant answers that are nevertheless wrong, suggesting prior-driven collapse rather than evidence-sensitive spatial reasoning.

Abstract: Spatial reasoning is fundamental to robotics, autonomy, and embodied AI, yet modern vision-language models (VLMs) remain unreliable on metric distance queries. A common assumption is that consistent predictions across viewpoints reflect geometric grounding. We test this assumption and find the opposite: leading VLMs often produce view-invariant and consistent answers even when those answers are incorrect, indicating weak coupling between predictions and viewpoint-specific visual evidence.
  We introduce \textbf{ViewDiag}, a controlled multi-view evaluation protocol built from Hypersim, ScanNet, and KITTI360, comprising 176 object-pair tracks across 80 scenes with 2--10 views per track. The protocol evaluates models along three axes: metric accuracy, distributional concentration, and a latent feature probe for internal collapse that distinguishes decision collapse from representation collapse. Across diverse models, we observe a consistent pattern of high prediction stability paired with substantial error, clustering in a regime characterized by strong consistency but low accuracy.
  \noindent These results challenge the common use of cross-view consistency as a proxy for geometric understanding. Instead, we show that stable predictions may reflect prior-driven collapse rather than evidence-sensitive reasoning. ViewDiag provides a controlled benchmark and diagnostic framework for evaluating spatial VLMs beyond accuracy alone. The code and data can be found \href{https://github.com/SDivakarBhat/Consistent_Yet_Wrong.git}{here}

</details>


### [7] [Plan2Map: A Multimodal Benchmark for Document-Grounded Geospatial Boundary Reconstruction from Planning Records](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02747)
*Fabian Degen,Oishi Deb,Jindong Gu,Junchi Yu,Samuele Marro,Philip Torr,Jialin Yu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Plan2Map is a new benchmark for turning planning documents into geospatial boundaries, and a tool-using multi-stage agent achieves strong IoU performance by separating evidence extraction, map alignment, and boundary reconstruction.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Planning records often describe geographic restrictions only indirectly in text, schedules, map plates, labels, and annotations, so existing systems lack a machine-readable way to reconstruct valid geospatial boundaries from source documents.

Method: The paper introduces Plan2Map, a 208-case multimodal benchmark for geospatial boundary reconstruction from planning documents, and a GeoPlanAgent pipeline that splits the task into evidence extraction, localisation, map registration, boundary segmentation, projection, and verification.

Result: GeoPlanAgent reaches 0.736 mean IoU, 0.904 median IoU, and 67.8% of predictions at or above 0.8 IoU. Direct VLM-to-GeoJSON baselines are unreliable; main errors remain in localisation and map registration, while supervised boundary segmentation improves mask quality.

Conclusion: Plan2Map provides a benchmark and GeoPlanAgent demonstrates that decomposed, tool-assisted document-to-boundary reconstruction substantially outperforms direct VLM baselines on UK planning records.

Abstract: Planning records define restrictions over geographic areas, but their source documents often provide only indirect spatial evidence rather than machine-readable boundaries. We introduce Plan2Map, a 208-case multimodal benchmark for document-grounded geospatial boundary reconstruction from UK planning records. Given only a source planning document, systems must reconstruct a valid geospatial boundary from notice text, schedules, map plates, map labels, and boundary annotations; the reference GeoJSON is held out for scoring. We propose GeoPlanAgent, a document-grounded, geospatial-tool-in-the-loop system that decomposes the task into evidence extraction, localisation, map registration, boundary segmentation, projection, and verification. On Plan2Map, GeoPlanAgent achieves 0.736 mean IoU and 0.904 median IoU, with 67.8\% of predictions at or above 0.8 IoU, substantially outperforming direct VLM-to-GeoJSON baselines. Diagnostic analysis shows that direct VLM prediction remains unreliable, while remaining errors are concentrated in localisation and map registration, and supervised boundary segmentation substantially improves pixel-level mask quality. Plan2Map provides a concrete testbed for multimodal geospatial reconstruction from public planning records. Project page: https://odeb1.github.io/Plan2Map_Project_Page/.

</details>


### [8] [MetaWorld: Scaling Multi-Agent Video World Model from Single-view Video Data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02753)
*Teng Hu,Mingchun Lu,Yating Wang,Jiangning Zhang,Jinkun Hao,Ye Pan,Ran Yi,Lizhuang Ma,Dacheng Tao*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MetaWorld turns ordinary single-view videos into training signals for multi-agent world models by reconstructing shared 3D motion and enforcing cross-view alignment during generation, producing more consistent multi-agent videos.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing video world models are limited to a single agent and single viewpoint. Extending them to multi-agent settings is difficult because paired multi-view training data is scarce and independently generated views often fail to stay physically consistent with each other.

Method: The paper proposes MetaWorld with three core components: (1) Monocular World-State Unrolling to decompose single-view video into camera ego-motion and subject trajectories in a shared 3D space, (2) a Subject-Aware World Generator conditioned on per-agent identity images for controllable appearance generation, and (3) World-State Alignment, a per-frame inter-branch cross-attention mechanism added to each video DiT transformer layer to synchronize denoising across views.

Result: Experiments show that MetaWorld outperforms prior approaches in cross-view consistency and identity preservation, supporting a scalable and physics-grounded framework for open-domain multi-agent video world modeling.

Conclusion: MetaWorld demonstrates that scalable multi-agent video world modeling can be learned from single-view videos by decomposing monocular footage into shared 3D world states and aligning multi-view generation through cross-view synchronization, yielding better cross-view consistency and identity fidelity.

Abstract: Video world models are a foundational generative technology for embodied AI and the Metaverse, yet existing approaches are inherently limited to a single agent observing from a single perspective. Extending these models to multi-agent settings introduces two critical challenges: data scarcity (coordinated multi-view recordings are prohibitively expensive to collect for general open-domain scenarios) and world state alignment (independently generated video streams cannot ensure that shared physical environments and events evolve consistently across views). To address these challenges, we propose MetaWorld, a novel framework that scales multi-agent video world models to open-domain environments directly from single-view videos. First, we introduce Monocular World-State Unrolling (MWSU) to explicitly decompose monocular footage into the camera operator's ego-motion and the visible subject's spatial trajectory. This camera-trajectory decomposition naturally extracts synchronized multi-agent motion data within a shared 3D space, completely bypassing the need for multi-camera setups. Second, for precise visual control, we develop the Subject-Aware World Generator to enable appearance-driven simulation conditioned on per-agent identity images. Finally, to ensure both views are grounded in the identical physical reality, we propose World-State Alignment, a per-frame inter-branch cross-attention mechanism inserted at every transformer layer of the video DiT. By jointly synchronizing the denoising process, WSA enforces both static geometric consistency and dynamic motion consistency, encouraging that the shared 3D environment and physical events remain well-aligned across both egocentric views. Extensive experiments demonstrate that MetaWorld achieves superior cross-view consistency and identity fidelity, establishing a highly scalable, physics-driven paradigm for multi-agent video world modeling.

</details>


### [9] [From Local Training to Large-Scale Mapping: A Comparative Assessment of Machine Learning and Deep Learning for Transferable Satellite-Derived Bathymetry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02764)
*Hsiao-Jou Hsu,Joachim Moortgat*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TL;DR：这篇论文表明，可迁移卫星测深的关键不只是换更大模型，而是训练数据的空间组织方式。保留空间连续样本、对浅水区加权、并利用多时相影像后，CNN在跨区域测深上明显优于随机森林，并在公开基准上以更少参数取得更好精度。 / TL;DR: The paper shows that transferable SDB depends less on simply using bigger models and more on how training data are structured. By preserving spatial continuity, weighting shallow depths, and exploiting multi-temporal imagery, CNNs clearly outperform Random Forest for cross-region bathymetry transfer and achieve better benchmark accuracy with fewer parameters.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机：传统基于多光谱影像的卫星测深虽然成本低，但在不同区域间难以稳定迁移，尤其是在光学性质复杂的海岸环境中。作者希望评估机器学习与深度学习模型的跨区域可迁移性，并寻找能够提升泛化性能与浅水精度的训练策略。 / Motivation: Satellite-derived bathymetry from multispectral imagery is cost-effective, but it transfers poorly across regions, especially in optically complex coastal waters. The paper aims to evaluate machine learning and deep learning approaches for cross-region transfer and identify training strategies that improve generalization and shallow-water accuracy.

Method: 方法：使用Sentinel-2多光谱影像，在Pratas Island和大堡礁部分区域上训练一个随机森林基线和四个CNN模型（ResNet-50、ResNet-101、EfficientNet-B4、ConvNeXt-Large），并在空间独立的区域内与跨区域测试集上评估。关键技术包括：1）保留连续礁体块进行训练而非随机切块；2）提出强调近表层深度的Smooth Weight Function加权RMSE损失；3）利用多时相重复影像扩展训练多样性，并在推理阶段对多次预测取中值以抑制噪声。另在MagicBathyNet公开基准上进行验证。 / Method: The study trains a Random Forest baseline and four CNNs (ResNet-50, ResNet-101, EfficientNet-B4, ConvNeXt-Large) on Sentinel-2 multispectral imagery from Pratas Island and selected Great Barrier Reef regions, then evaluates them on spatially independent intra- and cross-regional test areas. Core design choices are: (1) preserving contiguous reef blocks during training instead of random patches, (2) introducing a Smooth Weight Function weighted RMSE loss to emphasize near-surface depths, and (3) using multi-temporal repeat imagery both to diversify training data and to median-aggregate predictions at inference. The approach is also validated on the public MagicBathyNet benchmark.

Result: 结果：在0-20 m范围内，采用上述设计后，区域内测试RMSE为1.15-1.92 m，在<=3 m浅水区可低至0.26 m。随机森林跨区域性能显著下降（RMSE由1.53 m恶化到2.99-3.78 m），而深度模型更稳健（2.46-2.98 m）。在MagicBathyNet（0-16 m）上，所提网络达到0.19-0.22 m RMSE，优于U-Net基线和专用Transformer，同时参数量更少。 / Results: Over the 0-20 m depth range, the proposed design yields intra-regional RMSE of 1.15-1.92 m and as low as 0.26 m for depths <= 3 m. Random Forest deteriorates sharply under cross-regional transfer (RMSE 1.53 m to 2.99-3.78 m), while deep models remain more robust (2.46-2.98 m). On MagicBathyNet (0-16 m), the proposed networks achieve 0.19-0.22 m RMSE, outperforming a U-Net baseline and a task-specific transformer with substantially fewer parameters.

Conclusion: 结论：在可迁移卫星测深任务中，保持训练样本的空间连续性是最关键的设计选择；结合针对浅水区加权的SWF-RMSE损失、多时相影像训练与推理期中值融合，深度学习模型比随机森林具有更强的跨区域泛化能力，并可在0-20 m浅海区域实现可扩展、稳健的SDB。 / Conclusion: For transferable satellite-derived bathymetry, preserving spatial continuity in training is the most critical design choice; combined with SWF-weighted RMSE, multi-temporal training, and median aggregation at inference, deep models generalize across regions more robustly than Random Forest and enable scalable, reliable SDB in shallow waters.

Abstract: Satellite-derived bathymetry (SDB) from multispectral imagery is cost-effective but scales poorly across regions, especially in optically complex coastal environments. We evaluate machine learning and deep learning for transferable SDB over the 0-20 m depth range using Sentinel-2 imagery. A Random Forest baseline and four CNNs (ResNet-50, ResNet-101, EfficientNet-B4, ConvNeXt-Large) are trained on Pratas Island and selected Great Barrier Reef regions, then evaluated on spatially independent intra- and cross-regional test areas. Preserving spatial continuity during training, by keeping contiguous reef blocks rather than random patches, is the single most impactful design choice; we further introduce a Smooth Weight Function (SWF)-weighted RMSE loss that emphasizes near-surface depths. With these choices, intra-regional RMSE ranges from 1.15 to 1.92 m over 0-20 m and is as low as 0.26 m for depths <= 3 m. Random Forest degrades sharply under cross-regional transfer (RMSE 1.53 m -> 2.99-3.78 m), while the deep models stay more robust (2.46-2.98 m). On the public MagicBathyNet aerial-RGB benchmark (0-16 m) the proposed networks reach 0.19-0.22 m RMSE, outperforming a U-Net baseline and a task-specific transformer architecture with substantially fewer parameters. We further exploit multi-temporal repeat imagery: training on it broadens diversity, and median-aggregating predictions across passes at inference reduces noise from changing sun angles, atmospheric conditions, water properties, and tides. We release optimized architectures and pretrained weights to enable scalable transfer to new sites.

</details>


### [10] [GeoDrive-Bench: Benchmarking Region-Specific Multimodal Reasoning in Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02774)
*Yingzi Ma,Chaowei Xiao,Ming Jiang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A new benchmark, GeoDrive-Bench, shows that driving VLMs struggle with country-specific traffic rules; a distillation method improves their geo-cultural driving reasoning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although autonomous-driving VLMs show strong general performance, their ability to reason under region-specific traffic rules and geo-cultural driving conventions is insufficiently studied, creating uncertainty for deployment in diverse international settings.

Method: The paper introduces GeoDrive-Bench, a benchmark with 5,053 human-validated multiple-choice QA pairs from six countries, spanning perception, prediction, planning, and region reasoning. It also proposes a distillation algorithm to inject region-specific traffic-rule knowledge into VLM internal representations so that models better align visual understanding with local driving policies.

Result: Experiments on nine state-of-the-art VLMs reveal substantial performance variation across countries and tasks, indicating weak geo-cultural robustness. The proposed baseline models with the distillation approach improve region-specific reasoning performance across regions.

Conclusion: Current vision-language models for autonomous driving do not yet possess robust region-aware driving intelligence. GeoDrive-Bench is presented as an effective benchmark and training-oriented testbed to diagnose and improve such capabilities across different geo-cultural driving environments.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) for autonomous driving have shown promising performance, but their ability to handle region-specific traffic rules remains underexplored, raising uncertainties about their deployment across diverse global settings. We therefore introduce GeoDrive-Bench, a novel benchmark that enables the systematic investigation of VLMs' geo-culturally grounded driving reasoning. We curated 5,053 human-validated multiple-choice QA pairs across six countries covering diverse driving cultures. Specifically, we emphasize four driving tasks: perception, prediction, planning, and region reasoning. Each question requires models to infer the correct driving behavior from visual evidence and local traffic conventions without explicit country labels. Beyond evaluation, we further design a distillation algorithm that injects region-specific traffic-rule knowledge into the internal representations of VLMs, enabling models to better align visual scene understanding with local driving policies. Experiments on nine state-of-the-art VLMs show substantial performance variations across geo-driving cultures for each task, while our proposed baseline models exhibit improved geo-cultural reasoning across regions. These results suggest that current VLMs still lack robust region-aware driving intelligence and highlight GeoDrive-Bench as a diagnostic and training-oriented testbed for deployable autonomous driving foundation models.

</details>


### [11] [Diagnosis of Human Object Interaction Detectors for Real World Educational Applications](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02789)
*Divya Mereddy,Ashwin Tudur Sadashiva,Marcos Quinones-Grueiro,Gautam Biswas*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A HOI model adaptation framework diagnoses triplet-level errors and their causes in educational videos, then applies targeted fixes, boosting CCATT macro-F1 from 48.6 to 90.2.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: SOTA HOI detectors work well on benchmarks but often fail in real-world educational training settings because of domain-specific objects, occlusions, and complex visual conditions, creating a need for systematic diagnosis and adaptation.

Method: The paper proposes a diagnosis-driven framework that combines a triplet-level HOI error taxonomy with error-factor attribution analysis, then uses the diagnosed failure modes to guide targeted refinement of a pretrained HOI model for the target domain.

Result: On the CCATT mixed-reality medical training dataset, the diagnosis-informed refinement improves the macro-F1 score of a pretrained CDN model from 48.6 to 90.2.

Conclusion: Detailed diagnostic analysis combined with diagnosis-informed refinement can substantially improve pretrained HOI recognition in real-world educational environments, as shown by the large macro-F1 gain on the CCATT dataset.

Abstract: Human-object interaction (HOI) recognition is critical for automatically analyzing student behavior in complex educational environments. Although state-of-the-art (SOTA) HOI detectors perform well on benchmark datasets, their performance often degrades when deployed in real-world training environments due to domain-specific objects, occlusions, and complex visual conditions. In this paper, we introduce a diagnosis-driven framework that integrates a triplet-level HOI error taxonomy with error-factor attribution analysis for real-world educational video data. We study this problem in the context of Critical Care Air Transport Team (CCATT) mixed-reality medical training. Based on an analysis of HOI failure modes and their causes, we develop a diagnosis-informed refinement strategy for adapting pretrained HOI models to the target domain. Experiments on the CCATT dataset show that this approach improves the macro-F1 score of a pretrained CDN model from 48.6 to 90.2 through targeted refinement guided by diagnosed error factors. These results highlight the value of detailed diagnostic analysis for informing targeted adaptation of HOI models in real-world educational environments.

</details>

*Aditi,Niket Agarwal,Arslan Ali,Jon Allen,Martin Antolini,Adeline Aubame,Alisson Azzolini,Junjie Bai,Maciej Bala,Yogesh Balaji,Josh Bapst,Aarti Basant,Mukesh Beladiya,Mohammad Qazim Bhat,Zaid Pervaiz Wang,Boxiang Wang,Haoxiang Wang,Qiao Wang,Shihao Wang,Shijie Wang,Ting-Chun Wang,Yan Wang,Yu Wang,David Wehr,Fangyin Wei,Xinshuo Weng,Jay Zhangjie Wu,Kedi Wu,Hongchi Xia,Summer Xiao,Tianjun Xiao,Kevin Xie,Daguang Xu,Jiashu Xu,Mengyao Xu,Ruqing Xu,Xingqian Xu,Yao Xu,Dinghao Yang,Dong Yang,Hans Yang,Xiaodong Yang,Xuning Yang,Yichu Yang,Yurong You,Zhiding Yu,Hao Yuan,Simon Yuen,Xiaohui Zeng,Pengcuo Zeren,Cindy Zha,Haotian Zhang,Jenny Zhang,Jing Zhang,Liangkai Zhang,Paris Zhang,Shun Zhang,Xuanmeng Zhang,Zhizheng Zhang,Ann Zhao,Yilin Zhao,Yuliya Zhautouskaya,Charles Zhou,Fengzhe Zhou,Shilin Zhu,Yuke Zhu,Dima Zhylko,Artur Zolkowski*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Cosmos 3 is a unified multimodal world model for language, vision, audio, video, and actions; it outperforms prior open models across generation, understanding, and policy tasks, suggesting one backbone can cover many Physical AI capabilities.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing systems for vision-language understanding, video generation, world simulation, and action/policy modeling are often separated by modality or task. The paper is motivated by the need for a single unified model that can integrate these modalities for Physical AI and embodied agents.

Method: The paper proposes Cosmos 3, a family of omnimodal world models built with a unified mixture-of-transformers architecture that jointly processes and generates language, image, video, audio, and action sequences under flexible input-output configurations. It also uses post-training and releases curated synthetic datasets and benchmarks.

Result: Cosmos 3 sets a new state of the art on a diverse set of understanding and generation tasks. The post-trained models were ranked best open-source Text-to-Image and Image-to-Video models by Artificial Analysis and best policy model by RoboArena at the time of writing.

Conclusion: Cosmos 3 demonstrates that a unified omnimodal world model can serve as a scalable general-purpose backbone for embodied/Physical AI, achieving state-of-the-art results across understanding, generation, and policy tasks while being released openly for research and deployment.

Abstract: We introduce Cosmos 3, a family of omnimodal world models designed to jointly process and generate language, image, video, audio, and action sequences within a unified mixture-of-transformers architecture. By supporting highly flexible input-output configurations, Cosmos 3 seamlessly unifies critical modalities for Physical AI -- effectively subsuming vision-language models, video generators, world simulators, and world-action models into a single framework. Our evaluation demonstrates that Cosmos 3 establishes a new state-of-the-art across a diverse suite of understanding and generation tasks, demonstrating omnimodal world models as scalable, general-purpose backbones for embodied agents. Our post-trained Cosmos 3 models were ranked as the best open-source Text-to-Image and Image-to-Video models by Artificial Analysis, and the best policy model by RoboArena at the time the technical report was written. To accelerate open research and deployment in Physical AI, we make our code, model checkpoints, curated synthetic datasets, and evaluation benchmark available under the Linux Foundation's OpenMDW-1.1 https://openmdw.ai/license/1-1/ License at https://github.com/nvidia/cosmos}{github.com/nvidia/cosmos and https://huggingface.co/collections/nvidia/cosmos3 . The project website is available at https://research.nvidia.com/labs/cosmos-lab/cosmos3 .

</details>


### [13] [Automated Report-Derived Oncology VQA Benchmark for Evaluating Vision-Language Models on 3D Medical Imaging](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02809)
*Bo Liu,Hanxue Gu,Xiangru Li,Zheren Zhu,Jacob Ellison,Kang Wang,Janine M. Lupo,Yang Yang,Hui Lin*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: An automated agent pipeline generates private, clinically grounded medical VQA benchmarks from radiology reports and 3D cancer imaging; evaluations of six VLMs show no clear winner and reveal that some benchmark subsets truly require visual reasoning while others can be solved from textual or dataset priors alone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing medical VLM benchmarks are limited by small scale, manual annotation burden, and possible leakage into pretraining corpora. The paper aims to create a more scalable and clinically meaningful benchmark that reduces evaluation confounds and better measures true visual understanding in medical imaging.

Method: The authors design an automated pipeline that converts paired private radiology reports and 3D oncology images into multiple-choice VQA datasets. It creates two question types: schema-based RADS-style questions generated deterministically from clinician-defined reporting templates, and report-derived questions generated by an LLM from radiologist findings and then verified against the original reports. They apply the pipeline to four in-house cancer cohorts and evaluate six VLMs in zero-shot settings, including a blind ablation to test visual dependence.

Result: Using four internal cancer cohorts, the pipeline produces a private benchmark with contamination control and no per-question manual annotation. Zero-shot results on six VLMs show that no model consistently dominates and all have clear room for improvement. The blind ablation further shows that image dependence varies strongly by dataset: liver report-derived questions need visual input, while Lung CT questions can largely be solved without vision.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that an automated agent-driven pipeline can build scalable, clinically grounded, contamination-controlled medical VQA benchmarks from private radiology data without per-question human annotation, and that current VLMs still show substantial performance gaps with dataset-specific visual reliance.

Abstract: Evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) on medical images requires benchmarks that are clinically grounded, scalable, and controlled for evaluation confounds. Existing public benchmarks are limited in scale, manually annotated, or potentially leaked into VLM pretraining corpora. We present an automated agent-driven pipeline that generates multiple-choice VQA datasets directly from paired private radiology reports and 3D oncology imaging, producing two complementary question types: RADS-style questions deterministically derived from clinician-defined reporting schemas, and radiology report-derived questions generated by an LLM from radiologist findings and verified against the source report. Applied to four in-house cancer cohorts, the pipeline yields an instance-contamination-controlled benchmark without per-question human annotation. Zero-shot evaluation of six VLMs reveals no dominant model and substantial headroom across all cells. A blind ablation reveals that visual reliance is highly dataset-specific: liver Report-derived questions genuinely require the image, while Lung CT is essentially solvable without it - the leading closed model exceeds its sighted accuracy on Lung CT when blinded - indicating that even private clinical data does not guarantee a contamination-controlled read of visual capability. The pipeline is released as an open agent skill for in-house redeployment.

</details>


### [14] [Principled Reflection Separation via Nonlinear Superposition and Feature Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02831)
*Qiming Hu,Mingjia Li,Yuntong Li,Xiaojie Guo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: TL;DR：把反射分离从“线性解混”改为“非线性成像+双流交互建模”；新模型更符合真实成像过程，在真实数据上效果更好。 / TL;DR: Reframe reflection separation from linear unmixing to nonlinear formation plus dual-stream interaction modeling; the resulting method better matches real imaging and performs better on real benchmarks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于现有方法多依赖简化假设或独立建模，尤其默认 sRGB 域线性叠加，这无法刻画真实 ISP 流程带来的非线性耦合，因此在真实世界反射分离上受限。 / The motivation is that existing methods rely on simplified assumptions or separate modeling, especially the linear composition assumption in the sRGB domain, which fails to capture nonlinear coupling caused by real-world ISP pipelines and thus limits real-world reflection separation.

Method: 方法上，论文提出可学习的非线性叠加模型以替代传统 sRGB 线性合成，并在此基础上设计广义双流交互框架，通过双向特征交换显式建模 transmission 与 reflection 的依赖关系；该框架统一了激活式、门控式和注意力式交互，并兼容 CNN 与 Transformer 主干。 / Methodologically, it introduces a learnable nonlinear superposition model to replace linear sRGB composition, then builds a generalized dual-stream interactive framework with bidirectional feature exchange between transmission and reflection, unifying activation-, gating-, and attention-based interactions and supporting both CNN and Transformer backbones.

Result: 在多个真实世界基准上的大量实验表明，该方法取得了优于现有方法的性能，并表现出较强的泛化能力。 / Extensive experiments on diverse real-world benchmarks show that the proposed method outperforms prior approaches and exhibits strong generalization.

Conclusion: 该论文认为单幅图像反射分离的核心不应被视为对 sRGB 域线性混合的逆过程，而应建模真实 ISP 下的非线性成像与层间交互；所提出方法在真实场景中更有效且泛化更强。 / The paper concludes that single-image reflection separation should be treated as learning nonlinear image formation and inter-layer interaction rather than inverting a linear sRGB mixture, leading to better real-world performance and generalization.

Abstract: Single-image reflection separation is fundamentally challenged by the entanglement of transmission and reflection layers under complex image formation processes. Existing approaches largely rely on simplified assumptions or independent modeling, limiting their ability to handle real-world scenarios. In this work, we revisit the problem from a unified perspective and identify a key issue of existing approaches, i.e., the widely adopted linear composition model in the sRGB domain fails to capture the nonlinear coupling introduced by real-world image signal processing pipelines. To address this, we introduce a learnable nonlinear superposition model that more faithfully characterizes layer interactions and improves decomposition fidelity. Building upon this formulation, we propose a generalized dual-stream interactive framework that explicitly models bidirectional dependencies between transmission and reflection through feature exchange. This framework unifies activation-, gating-, and attention-based interaction mechanisms, and is compatible with both CNN and Transformer backbones. Extensive experiments on diverse real-world benchmarks demonstrate that the proposed approach achieves superior performance with strong generalization capability. More importantly, our study reveals that reflection separation is not about undoing a linear mixture, but about learning nonlinear formation and interaction}, offering new insights into the design of principled image decomposition models. Code and models are publicly available at https://mingcv.github.io/DIRS-Page.

</details>


### [15] [Pathway-Structured Privileged Distillation for Deployable Computational Pathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02877)
*Yongxin Guo,Hao Lu,Onur Koyun,Zhengjie Zhu,Muhammet Demir,Metin Gurcan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MoPE trains with both RNA and histology, then predicts from histology alone by distilling RNA pathway knowledge into specialized pathology experts, improving WSI-only performance and offering limited interpretability.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Transcriptomics plus histopathology can improve cancer risk modelling, but RNA profiling is often unavailable in routine clinical practice. Because histology only partially reflects molecular programs, the goal is to transfer useful RNA information into a model that can run on histology alone.

Method: The paper proposes Mixture of Pathway Experts (MoPE), a knowledge-distillation framework that encodes RNA-derived pathways and distills molecular supervision into pathway-indexed pathology experts through memory-usage alignment, treating multimodal learning as privileged distillation for WSI-only inference.

Result: Across multiple public benchmarks and two independent breast cancer cohorts, MoPE consistently outperformed baseline methods for whole-slide-image-only inference. Pathway-usage analysis and human-audited visual inspection also provided interpretable, bounded insight into model behavior.

Conclusion: MoPE shows that pathway-structured privileged distillation can use RNA information during training to improve histology-only cancer risk inference while keeping inference RNA-free.

Abstract: Integrating transcriptomics and histopathology can improve cancer risk modelling, yet practical use is constrained by the limited availability of RNA profiling in routine settings. Here we introduce Mixture of Pathway Experts (MoPE), a knowledge-distillation framework that reframes multimodal learning as privileged distillation for histology-only inference. MoPE is motivated by the partial observability between RNA profiles and whole-slide images: histology can capture morphology-linked consequences of certain molecular programmes, but cannot be expected to reconstruct the full transcriptomic state. MoPE encodes RNA-derived pathways and transfers the molecular supervision to pathway-indexed pathology experts through memory-usage alignment. Across diverse public benchmarks and two independent breast cancer cohorts, MoPE consistently improved WSI-only inference performance relative to baseline methods. Pathway-usage analyses and human-audited visual inspection provide bounded inspection of model behaviour and candidate morphology-linked readouts. These results support pathway-structured privileged distillation as a promising route to using molecular information during training while preserving RNA-free inference.

</details>


### [16] [Tiny Collaborative Inference for Occlusion-Robust Object Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02894)
*Chieh-Tung Cheng,Mustafa Aslanov,Eiman Kanjo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper shows that on tiny edge hardware, late fusion with WBF is more effective than feature fusion for handling occlusion in collaborative object detection, improves accuracy and frame coverage with modest communication cost, and works in host-free multi-board deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Ultra-low-end edge devices used in IoT surveillance and search-and-rescue increasingly need local vision, but they face severe memory/compute limits, communication constraints in multi-device collaboration, and accuracy degradation under occlusion. The work is motivated by finding a collaboration strategy that improves detection robustness without exceeding tight hardware budgets.

Method: The paper combines an MCUNet backbone, a YOLOv2 detection head, and TensorFlow Lite quantisation for on-device detection, then evaluates two collaborative inference strategies under occlusion: feature-level fusion of intermediate feature maps and decision-level fusion using Weighted Boxes Fusion. It further tests two-board and three-view setups, starting from a host-assisted USB relay and moving to Wi-Fi peer-to-peer execution on Coral Dev Board Micro devices.

Result: Weighted Boxes Fusion outperforms feature-level fusion under the tested occlusion settings, yielding up to +0.2736 mAP in asymmetric occlusion cases. Extending to three views increases gains up to +0.3827 mAP, with about 1.3 KB communication per exchange. On two Coral Dev Board Micro units, on-device WBF with Wi-Fi peer-to-peer communication shows low communication energy relative to inference. In a 301.9 s session over 108 frames, fused output appears on 61 frames versus 47 for Board 2 alone, a +29.8% frame-level coverage gain. A small decentralised federated learning note is included, but results remain limited under non-iid local data.

Conclusion: Decision-level fusion via Weighted Boxes Fusion is a practical and superior approach for improving occlusion-robust object detection on ultra-low-end edge devices (<1 MB SRAM), and host-free multi-board deployment is feasible with low communication energy overhead.

Abstract: Small edge devices such as IoT surveillance nodes and search-and-rescue (SAR) platforms are increasingly expected to run computer vision locally. On ultra-low-end hardware, however, object detection is limited by available memory and compute, by communication costs when several devices cooperate, and by the loss of accuracy caused by occlusion. The work evaluates occlusion-robust object detection on devices with less than 1 MB SRAM by combining an MCUNet backbone, a YOLOv2 detection head, and TensorFlow Lite quantisation. We evaluate two collaborative inference strategies: feature-level fusion, which concatenates intermediate feature maps, and decision-level fusion via Weighted Boxes Fusion (WBF). Under the tested occlusion settings, WBF outperforms feature-level fusion and gives gains of up to +0.2736 mAP in asymmetric occlusion scenarios. Extending fusion to three views improves accuracy further (up to +0.3827 mAP) while adding communication overhead (approximately 1.3 KB per exchange). The hardware experiments start with a host-assisted USB-relay baseline and then move to a Wi-Fi peer-to-peer deployment on two Coral Dev Board Micro units, where WBF runs on-device and communication energy remains small relative to inference. In a representative 301.9 s autonomous session comprising 108 frames, fused output is observed on 61 frames compared with 47 for Board 2 alone, a frame-level coverage gain of +29.8%. We also include a small exploratory decentralised federated learning (DFL) feasibility note, but do not treat it as a main result because performance remains limited under non-iid local data. The results support decision-level fusion as a viable option for improving occlusion robustness in small-scale edge object detection, including host-free multi-board operation on ultra-low-end hardware.

</details>


### [17] [Any2Poster: Any-Source Poster Generation Across Modalities and Domains](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02915)
*Amogh Vinaykumar,Aiden Li,Suozhi Huang,Shilong Liu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a benchmark and baseline agent for generating posters from many source types, and shows strong, reproducible improvements over prior paper-to-poster methods.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing automatic poster generation evaluations are hard to compare and insufficient because they are often limited to paper-only inputs, narrow domains, or superficial visual similarity metrics. A broader and more reproducible benchmark is needed to measure both information fidelity and visual communication quality across diverse source formats.

Method: The paper introduces Any2Poster Bench, which evaluates poster generation from eight input modalities and five domains using quiz-based probes for factual retention and interpretive understanding plus VLM-based assessments of visual quality, layout, readability, completeness, and logical flow. It also presents Any2Poster Agent, an end-to-end system that parses heterogeneous sources, organizes salient content, plans layouts, renders posters, and iteratively refines outputs using visual feedback.

Result: Any2Poster Agent achieves 87.25% average accuracy across input modalities and 87.28% across content domains on Any2Poster Bench. On PaperQuiz-style evaluation, it improves overall accuracy from 51.06–51.33% for PosterAgent-4o to 72.58%, and raises the density-augmented score from 116–121 to 145.16.

Conclusion: Any2Poster Bench and Any2Poster Agent together provide a reusable benchmark and a strong baseline for evaluating and advancing multimodal, domain-general automatic poster generation, with clear gains over prior paper-to-poster systems.

Abstract: Visual posters are a compact medium for communicating dense information, yet progress on automatic poster generation remains difficult to measure because existing evaluations are often restricted to paper-only inputs, narrow domains, or surface-level visual similarity. We introduce Any2Poster Bench, a benchmark for any-source poster generation that evaluates systems across eight input modalities--PDFs, URLs, PPTX, DOCX, Markdown, LaTeX, notebooks, and videos--and five content domains. Any2Poster Bench pairs each source with quiz-based probes of verbatim factual retention and interpretive understanding, together with VLM-based judgments of visual quality, layout, readability, content completeness, and logical flow, enabling reproducible assessment of both information fidelity and visual communication. To instantiate and validate this benchmark, we further present Any2Poster Agent, an end-to-end reference agent that parses heterogeneous sources, organizes salient content, plans poster layouts, renders posters, and iteratively refines them using visual feedback. On Any2Poster Bench, Any2Poster Agent achieves 87.25% average accuracy across input modalities and 87.28% across content domains. On PaperQuiz-style evaluation, where prior paper-to-poster agents are directly comparable, Any2Poster Agent improves over PosterAgent-4o from 51.06-51.33% to 72.58% overall accuracy and from 116-121 to 145.16 in density-augmented score. Together, Any2Poster Bench and Any2Poster Agent provide a reusable evaluation resource and a competitive baseline for studying multimodal, domain-general poster generation.

</details>


### [18] [Pixel Cube: Diffusion-based Portrait Video Relighting Through Realistic Lighting Reproduction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02919)
*Yufan Zhang,Yu Ji,Ayo Ajiboye,Rundi Wu,Yu Guo,Changxi Zheng,Jinwei Ye*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 作者提出了一个利用混合数据集、LED采集系统、HDR环境贴图控制和视频扩散先验的动态人像视频重光照框架，可在新环境光下生成高真实感、强时间一致性且保持身份细节的视频。 / The paper introduces a diffusion-based dynamic portrait video relighting framework using a hybrid dataset, LED-based capture, HDR environment-map control, and video diffusion priors to generate photorealistic, temporally consistent, identity-preserving relit videos under novel lighting.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有动态人像视频重光照通常难以同时兼顾照片级真实感、时间一致性、身份保持，以及对复杂新光照环境的可控性，因此需要一种更强的生成式方法与更高质量的数据采集方案。 / Existing dynamic portrait video relighting methods struggle to jointly achieve photorealism, temporal consistency, identity preservation, and controllability under complex novel lighting, motivating a stronger generative approach and higher-quality training data.

Method: 方法上，作者构建了一个由真实采集与渲染数据组成的混合训练集，并搭建LED光照系统以获取具有已知光照条件的高速动态人像数据。模型利用预训练视频扩散模型的先验，以逐帧HDR环境贴图作为光照控制，并结合合成背景图像来控制曝光和色调，从而生成身份保持且时序稳定的重光照视频。 / Methodologically, the authors build a hybrid dataset of real-captured and rendered dynamic portrait videos, supported by an LED-based lighting rig for high-speed acquisition under known lighting. The model leverages priors from pre-trained video diffusion models, uses per-frame HDR environment maps for lighting control, and incorporates synthesized background images to control exposure and color tone, enabling identity-preserving and temporally consistent relighting.

Result: 实验表明，该方法能够在野外视频和人像摄影应用中生成真实、和谐且时序一致的重光照结果，较好保留表情、肤色、皱纹和胡须等细节，并在多个维度上优于现有方法。 / Experiments show that the method produces realistic, harmonious, and temporally consistent relit videos for in-the-wild footage and portrait photography applications, while preserving expressions and fine facial details such as skin tone, wrinkles, and facial hair, outperforming prior methods across key metrics.

Conclusion: 该论文提出了一种基于视频扩散模型的动态人像视频重光照方法，在真实感、光照协调性和时间一致性方面达到SOTA，并且能较好泛化到未见主体、动作与光照条件。 / The paper presents a diffusion-based relighting method for dynamic portrait videos that achieves state-of-the-art photorealism, lighting harmony, and temporal consistency, while generalizing well to unseen subjects, motions, and lighting conditions.

Abstract: We present a diffusion-based method for relighting dynamic portrait videos with photorealism and temporal consistency. Our method is fueled by a hybrid training dataset that consists of real-captured and rendered dynamic portrait videos with diverse subject appearances, facial motions, head poses, and known lighting conditions. Specifically, we construct an LED-based lighting system for realistic lighting emulation and high-speed video relighting data acquisition. By leveraging the image priors embedded in pre-trained video diffusion models, and using per-frame high dynamic range (HDR) environment map as lighting control, we train a high-performance generative model for realistic and identity-preserving dynamic portrait video relighting. In addition to the environment map control, our model uses a synthesized background image to enable control on the camera's exposure level and color tone. Our model can produce temporally consistent relit portrait video that looks realistic and harmonious under a provided new environment and faithfully preserve the subject's expression and fine facial features, including skin tone, wrinkles, and facial hair. Our model generalizes well to unseen data, in terms of the subject appearance, motion, and lighting condition. We perform extensive experiments on relighting in-the-wild videos with various environment maps and demonstrate practical applications on portrait photography. Results show that our method achieves state-of-the-art performance in photorealism, lighting harmony, and temporal consistency.

</details>


### [19] [ATLAS: A Large-Scale Evaluation Benchmark for Adversarial LiDAR Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02924)
*Mellon M. Zhang,Siddhant Panse,Zimo Fan,Akshal Dhal,Rishit Sarkar,Glen Chou*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: ATLAS is the first large-scale benchmark for testing LiDAR perception against black-box physical attacks. It finds that top-performing models are not uniformly more robust: they handle point removal better but often fail more under point injection, partly due to common training augmentation practices.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard autonomous driving perception benchmarks mainly test clean data, but real deployment faces rare, structured, and potentially adversarial LiDAR anomalies. Existing benchmarks and prior work do not adequately evaluate how modern LiDAR perception models behave under black-box physical sensor manipulation, creating a major gap in robustness assessment.

Method: The paper introduces ATLAS, a large-scale physically grounded benchmark for evaluating LiDAR perception under black-box sensor attacks. It simulates two main attack modes, point injection and point removal, over real driving sequences, and uses this suite to evaluate a broad set of state-of-the-art LiDAR perception models. The authors then analyze robustness patterns and investigate the role of training augmentations and initial mitigation strategies.

Result: The benchmark reveals a surprising tradeoff: models with higher clean benchmark performance tend to resist point removal attacks better, but are more susceptible to point injection attacks. The study identifies standard object database sampling augmentation as a key contributor to this vulnerability and explores early mitigation directions for both attack types.

Conclusion: ATLAS shows that modern LiDAR perception models have an asymmetric robustness profile under black-box physical sensor attacks: stronger benchmark models are generally more robust to point removal but more vulnerable to point injection. The paper concludes that common training practices, especially object database sampling augmentation, can systematically induce architecture-agnostic robustness weaknesses, making black-box sensor robustness a necessary evaluation dimension for future LiDAR perception systems.

Abstract: Autonomous driving perception is typically evaluated on clean benchmark data, yet real-world deployment requires robustness to rare, structured, and potentially adversarial sensor anomalies. This gap is especially critical for LiDAR, where external actors can physically manipulate the sensing process to induce black-box perception failures without accessing the model. Existing LiDAR benchmarks provide little visibility into this failure mode. Prior adversarial LiDAR studies have largely centered on attack hardware, geometric and algorithmic defenses, and early-generation detectors, leaving the robustness of modern perception systems unexplored. To address this evaluation gap, we introduce ATLAS (Adversarial Temporal LiDAR Attack Suite), the first large-scale, physically grounded evaluation benchmark for LiDAR perception models under black-box sensor attacks, simulating the two primary attack modes -- point injection and point removal -- across real driving sequences. Evaluating a broad cross-section of current state-of-the-art LiDAR perception models, ATLAS reveals a surprising robustness asymmetry: models with stronger performance on standard benchmarks tend to better withstand removal attacks, yet are actually more vulnerable to injection attacks than weaker models. We trace this vulnerability to standard object database sampling augmentations, revealing how current training practices can induce architecture-agnostic robustness failures, and study initial directions for mitigating both attack modes. We release the ATLAS generation code to support extensible, reproducible evaluations as attack capabilities evolve, helping make black-box sensor robustness an explicit consideration in future LiDAR perception development.

</details>


### [20] [SaluNet: Enabling Total Plasticity in Normalization-Free Deep Networks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02927)
*Mourad Zaied*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 论文提出SALU，一种可学习且有界的激活函数，用它替代归一化层，构建无需BatchNorm/LayerNorm的SaluNet；实验表明该方法在CNN和Transformer上表现强劲，且对极小batch更鲁棒。 / The paper proposes SALU, a bounded learnable activation that replaces normalization layers to build SaluNet; experiments show strong CNN/Transformer performance and much better robustness at very small batch sizes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有观点认为BatchNorm和LayerNorm对稳定训练不可或缺，但作者发现它们可能破坏网络中激活函数参数的可塑性，尤其不利于完全可学习的激活机制。因此论文试图寻找一种无需批统计量或外部仿射参数、同时仍能保持训练稳定性的替代方案。 / BatchNorm and LayerNorm are widely treated as indispensable for stable optimization, but the authors argue they impair the adaptability of learnable activation parameters. This motivates a normalization-free mechanism that preserves stability without batch statistics or external affine parameters.

Method: 作者首先观察并提出“可塑性抑制效应”：标准归一化会削弱可学习激活参数的适应能力。基于此，设计了SALU激活函数 SALU(x;a,b)=ax/sqrt(1+abx^2)，利用其有界性和可学习参数实现内在信号稳定；进一步构建SaluNet，用SALU替代归一化层，并用SWALU、GALU替代常规激活，在ResNet和Transformer上进行实验验证。 / The paper identifies a 'plasticity suppression effect' caused by standard normalization, then introduces SALU, a bounded learnable activation SALU(x;a,b)=ax/sqrt(1+abx^2) for intrinsic signal stabilization. Building on this, SaluNet replaces normalization layers with SALU and standard activations with SWALU/GALU, and evaluates the design on ResNets and Transformers.

Result: 在ResNet-18上，SaluNet-C-18无归一化取得CIFAR-10 97.35%、CIFAR-100 83.25%；batch size=1时仍有93.44%和76.23%。在Transformer上，SaluNet-T相较LayerNorm-GELU从CIFAR-10 90.92%提升到91.01%，CIFAR-100从66.54%提升到68.10%。在ImageNet-1K上，SaluNet-C-50达到78.67% Top-1（224x224）和79.23%（288x288）。 / On ResNet-18, normalization-free SaluNet-C-18 reaches 97.35% on CIFAR-10 and 83.25% on CIFAR-100, and still maintains 93.44% and 76.23% at batch size 1. For Transformers, SaluNet-T improves over LayerNorm-GELU from 90.92% to 91.01% on CIFAR-10 and from 66.54% to 68.10% on CIFAR-100. On ImageNet-1K, SaluNet-C-50 attains 78.67% Top-1 at 224x224 and 79.23% at 288x288.

Conclusion: 归一化层并非深层网络稳定训练所必需；通过使用具有内在稳定性的可学习有界激活SALU及其扩展，可以在CNN和Transformer中替代BatchNorm/LayerNorm，并在多项图像分类任务上达到与或优于标准归一化架构的性能，尤其在小批量场景更稳健。 / Normalization layers are not strictly necessary for stable deep learning; a bounded learnable activation, SALU, and its variants can replace BatchNorm/LayerNorm in CNNs and Transformers while matching or exceeding normalized baselines, especially under tiny-batch training.

Abstract: Normalization layers such as BatchNorm and LayerNorm have long been considered essential for stable training in deep networks. This work demonstrates that they can be fully replaced by a single learnable activation mechanism. We identify a plasticity suppression effect induced by standard normalization: learnable activation parameters rapidly lose adaptability when paired with normalization layers. Motivated by this observation, we introduce SALU (Saturated Adaptive Linear Unit), \[ \operatorname{SALU}(x;a,b) = \frac{a x}{\sqrt{1 + a b x^2}},\quad a>0,\; b>0 \] a bounded, learnable activation that provides intrinsic signal stabilization without relying on batch statistics or external affine parameters. Building on SALU, we propose SaluNet, a paradigm grounded in total plasticity: SALU replaces normalization layers, while SWALU and GALU replace standard activations. With ResNet-18, SaluNet-C-18 achieves 97.35\% on CIFAR-10 and 83.25\% on CIFAR-100 without normalization, maintaining 93.44\% and 76.23\% at batch size 1 where normalized architectures fail. For transformers, SaluNet-T improves over LayerNorm-GELU from 90.92\% to 91.01\% on CIFAR-10 and from 66.54\% to 68.10\% on CIFAR-100. SaluNet-C-50 reaches 78.67\% Top-1 on ImageNet-1K at $224\times224$, and $79.23\%$ at $288\times288$. These results suggest normalization layers suppress total plasticity, a property biological neurons inherently possess, enabling deep networks to learn effectively.

</details>


### [21] [CAD-to-CT Registration of Cylindrical Objects via Ellipse-Based Axis Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02935)
*Aleksander Ogonowski,Mikołaj Mrozowski,Daniel Więcek,Arkadiusz Ćwiek,Konrad Klimaszewski,Rafał Możdżonek,Adam Padee,Lech Raczyński,Piotr Wasiuk,Wojciech Wiślicki,Michał Matusiak,Sławomir Wronka*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: They register cylindrical CAD models to CT scans by detecting the object's axis from ellipse fits across slices, then aligning a voxelized CAD model by overlap maximization. It avoids intensity calibration and feature matching, and reaches very high angular accuracy (<0.1°).


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing CT-to-CAD registration methods are unreliable for this setting: intensity-based methods depend on calibrated grayscale references, while point-based methods require feature correspondences that do not exist between ideal CAD geometry and noisy CT data. Large-scale machine learning workflows also need accurate object masks and ground-truth geometry.

Method: The method first detects elliptical cross-sections in CT slices, fits ellipses to edge contours, removes outliers with RANSAC, and estimates the 3D rotation axis using PCA on ellipse centers. It then voxelizes the CAD model, aligns it with the detected axis, and optimizes translation by maximizing volumetric overlap with the CT scan.

Result: The approach achieves robust registration for ionization chambers with tilt and orientation errors below 0.1 degrees, and produces aligned CAD models suitable as ground truth for object localization and automated industrial CT analysis.

Conclusion: A two-stage geometry-based method can robustly register cylindrical CAD models to CT volumes without intensity calibration or explicit feature matching, achieving sub-0.1 degree orientation accuracy and enabling reliable ground-truth geometry extraction.

Abstract: Accurate registration of CAD models to CT scans is essential for establishing ground truth geometry in volumetric imaging. Obtaining reliable object masks is of growing importance in machine learning settings; as recent architectures grow more capable, huge datasets are required to fully utilise their capabilities. Traditional intensity-based methods fail when CT grayscale values lack calibration references, while point-based algorithms (e.g., ICP, RANSAC) require feature correspondence unavailable between idealized CAD geometry and noisy volumetric CT data.
  We propose a two-stage geometric registration method for cylindrical objects (ionization chambers) that takes advantage of the distinctive geometric features of the objects. First, we estimate the 3D rotation axis by detecting elliptical cross-sections across CT slices, fitting ellipses to edge-detected contours, and performing PCA on the fitted ellipse centers after RANSAC outlier removal. Second, we voxelize the CAD model, orient it along the detected axis, and maximize volumetric overlap with the CT scan through translational adjustment.
  This approach achieves robust registration with tilt and orientation errors below $0.1^\circ$ without intensity calibration or feature matching. Once registered, the aligned CAD model provides ground truth geometry for applications including machine learning-based object localization and automated analysis in industrial CT workflows.

</details>


### [22] [The Road Ahead in Autonomous Driving: The KITScenes Multimodal Dataset](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02956)
*Richard Schwarzkopf,Fabian Immel,Alexander Blumberg,Jonas Merkert,Nils Rack,Kaiwen Wang,Fabian Konstantinidis,Julian Truetsch,Carlos Fernandez,Annika Bätz,Kevin Rösch,Marlon Steiner,Willi Poh,Yinzhe Shen,Royden Wagner,Felix Hauser,Dominik Strutz,Jaime Villa,Gleb Stepanov,Holger Caesar,Ömer Şahin Taş,Frank Bieder,Jan-Hendrik Pauls,Christoph Stiller*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KITScenes Multimodal is a new European autonomous driving dataset with better sensors, more complete 3D maps, and broader geography than prior datasets, plus four benchmarks for mapping, depth, synthesis, and driving.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing autonomous driving datasets are limited by insufficient sensor fidelity, incomplete maps, and narrow geographic diversity, which restrict progress in perception, mapping, and embodied AI tasks.

Method: The paper builds a public autonomous driving dataset using a fully synchronized sensor suite including high-resolution global-shutter cameras, long-range lidar beyond 400m, 4D imaging radar, and redundant GNSS/INS localization, and constructs highly complete reprojection-accurate 3D HD maps with full topological connectivity validated through autonomous driving trials on open-source software.

Result: The result is KITScenes Multimodal, a European multimodal dataset featuring high-fidelity sensors, very complete 3D traffic-element-aware HD maps, broader geographic diversity, and four benchmarks: online HD map construction, long-range depth estimation, novel view synthesis, and end-to-end driving.

Conclusion: KITScenes Multimodal addresses key gaps in existing autonomous driving datasets by providing a high-fidelity, geographically diverse European dataset with highly complete 3D HD maps and synchronized multimodal sensors, alongside four benchmarks that support spatial learning for embodied AI.

Abstract: Existing autonomous driving datasets have enabled major progress, but fall short in sensor fidelity, map completeness, or geographic diversity. We present KITScenes Multimodal, a European dataset built around high-fidelity sensors and maps. Our fully synchronized sensor suite combines high-resolution global-shutter cameras, long-range lidar beyond 400m, 4D imaging radar, and redundant GNSS/INS localization. Our HD maps are, to our knowledge, the most complete of any sensor dataset, validated through autonomous driving trials on open-source software. For the first time in a public dataset, all driving-relevant traffic elements, such as traffic lights, are mapped in 3D to a reprojection-accurate level with full topological connectivity. Recorded in cities with irregular street layouts and mixed traffic modes, our dataset complements existing datasets by broadening the available geographic diversity. We also introduce four benchmarks, each advancing spatial learning for embodied AI: online HD map construction, long-range depth estimation, novel view synthesis, and end-to-end driving. Project page: https://kitscenes.com/

</details>


### [23] [Hand Trajectory Fusion for Egocentric Natural Language Query Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02962)
*Enmin Zhong,Carlos R. del-Blanco,Fernando Jaureguizar,Narciso García*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper improves first-person video moment grounding by adding encoded hand-motion trajectories to video-text features, yielding notable gains on queries involving manipulation and object state changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing egocentric NLQ grounding methods rely mainly on visual appearance and text, but often ignore hand motion, even though many Ego4D NLQ queries are centered on hand-object manipulation or its outcomes.

Method: The paper introduces a hand-trajectory encoder that converts sequences of hand skeletons into semantic hand kinematic features, then fuses these with pretrained video-text features using cross-attention and adaptive gating.

Result: On the Ego4D NLQ v2 validation split, the method shows the strongest improvements on Hand-Object Interaction queries (+2.54 R1@IoU=0.3) and Quantity/State queries (+4.32 R1@IoU=0.3).

Conclusion: Incorporating hand trajectory information improves egocentric NLQ grounding beyond appearance-only video-text fusion, especially for hand-object interaction and quantity/state-related queries.

Abstract: Egocentric Natural Language Query (NLQ) grounding asks a model to localize, in a long first-person video, the temporal interval that answers a free-form text query. Existing methods fuse video appearance with the query but ignore hand motion, despite the fact that roughly 41% of Ego4D NLQ queries are answered at a moment of hand--object manipulation or their immediate outcomes.We propose a hand-trajectory encoder for converting a sequence of hand skeletons into highly-semantic hand kinematic features, which are then aligned and combined with pretrained video--text features through a cross-attention fusion strategy with adaptive gating. On the Ego4D NLQ v2 validation split, the clearest gains appear for Hand-Object Interaction queries (+2.54 R1@IoU=0.3) and Quantity/State queries (+4.32 R1@IoU=0.3), indicating that hand trajectory provides grounding cues beyond appearance alone.

</details>


### [24] [Towards Compact Autonomous Driving Perception with Balanced Learning and Multi-sensor Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02979)
*Oskar Natan,Jun Miura*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A compact multi-task autonomous driving model fuses RGB, DVS, and LiDAR to solve several perception tasks at once. With adaptive loss weighting, it outperforms heavier multi-model baselines while being faster and more memory-efficient across both simulated and real-world datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is to overcome the inefficiency of using separate models for multiple autonomous driving perception tasks and to better exploit complementary information from multiple sensor modalities. The authors also aim to address imbalanced optimization across many tasks while keeping the model compact enough for faster inference and lower GPU memory use.

Method: The method is a compact deep multi-task learning architecture for autonomous driving perception.
