It jointly performs multi-view semantic segmentation, depth estimation, LiDAR segmentation, light detection and ranging related perception, and bird's eye view projection in one model. It uses data preprocessing, intermediate sensor fusion, and multimodal inputs from RGB cameras, dynamic vision sensors, and LiDAR sensors mounted at different ego-vehicle positions. The training also includes an adaptive loss weighting algorithm to address task imbalance.

Result: The proposed model variant performs better in ablation studies and remains competitive or superior to combinations of recent models, despite having far fewer parameters. It shows faster inference, lower GPU memory utilization, and consistent results across three CARLA simulation datasets and one real-world nuScenes-lidarseg dataset.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a compact deep multi-task model can jointly solve several autonomous driving perception tasks in a single forward pass, achieving stronger overall efficiency-performance tradeoffs than combinations of recent models. The proposed training method and adaptive loss weighting improve robustness across both simulated and real-world datasets while using fewer parameters, less GPU memory, and faster inference.

Abstract: We present a novel compact deep multi-task learning model to handle various autonomous driving perception tasks in one forward pass. The model performs multiple views of semantic segmentation, depth estimation, light detection and ranging (LiDAR) segmentation, and bird's eye view projection simultaneously without being supported by other models. We also provide an adaptive loss weighting algorithm to tackle the imbalanced learning issue that occurred due to plenty of given tasks. Through data pre-processing and intermediate sensor fusion techniques, the model can process and combine multiple input modalities retrieved from RGB cameras, dynamic vision sensors (DVS), and LiDAR placed at several positions on the ego vehicle. Therefore, a better understanding of a dynamically changing environment can be achieved. Based on the ablation study, the model variant trained with our proposed method achieves a better performance. Furthermore, a comparative study is also conducted to clarify its performance and effectiveness against the combination of some recent models. As a result, our model maintains better performance even with much fewer parameters. Hence, the model can inference faster with less GPU memory utilization. Moreover, the result tends to be consistent in 3 different CARLA simulation datasets and 1 real-world nuScenes-lidarseg dataset. To support future research, we share codes and other files publicly at https://github.com/oskarnatan/compact-perception.

</details>


### [25] [MUSE: A Unified Agentic Harness for MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03005)
*Jianglin Lu,Hailing Wang,Xu Ma,Qihua Dong,Mingyuan Zhang,Yizhou Wang,Yun Fu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: MUSE shows that better scaffolding around frozen multimodal LLMs, especially verification and repair, can unlock substantial capability gains without retraining the model.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: MLLMs still fail on seemingly simple human-level multimodal tasks such as maze navigation from screenshots and puzzle-piece matching. The authors investigate whether these failures can be reduced by improving the execution scaffold around a frozen model instead of retraining the model itself.

Method: The paper proposes MUSE, a multimodal unified structured execution harness that wraps off-the-shelf frozen MLLMs with composable modules for task representation, visual processing, perception tool use, structured parsing, deterministic verification, and verifier-guided repair.

Result: Across benchmarks covering visual spatial planning, visual perception, multimodal reasoning, and fine-grained visual discrimination, MUSE consistently improves performance over bare MLLMs, with the largest gains on difficult instances.

Conclusion: Agentic multimodal execution harnesses are a critical and underexplored axis for improving frozen MLLMs; many observed failures stem from harness design rather than intrinsic model limits, and can be mitigated without retraining via verifier-guided repair.

Abstract: Despite rapid progress, multimodal large language models (MLLMs) still fail on tasks that humans solve effortlessly, such as navigating a grid maze from a screenshot or selecting the correct puzzle piece. Rather than retraining the model, we ask a complementary question: how much capability can be elicited from a frozen MLLM purely by improving the execution scaffold around it? We introduce MUSE, a multimodal unified structured execution harness that wraps any off-the-shelf MLLM with composable modules for task representation, visual processing, perception tool use, structured parsing, deterministic verification, and verifier-guided repair, without any model retraining. We evaluate MUSE across diverse benchmarks spanning visual spatial planning, visual perception, multimodal reasoning, and fine-grained visual discrimination, using multiple state-of-the-art MLLMs. MUSE delivers consistent gains over the bare model in all settings, with the largest jumps on challenging instances. Further analysis reveals that many MLLM failures arise from harness-level shortcomings rather than fundamental model deficits, and can be addressed through verifier-guided repair without touching the model. These findings highlight the agentic multimodal harness as a critical yet underexplored design dimension, offering an orthogonal avenue for improving MLLMs beyond model-centric optimization.

</details>


### [26] [FCUS-rPPG: A Fast-Converging Unsupervised Framework for Remote Photoplethysmography via Gradient Oscillation Suppression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03050)
*Jiajie Li,Yu Liu,Rencheng Song,Xun Chen,Juan Cheng*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FCUS-rPPG is a fast-converging unsupervised remote PPG framework that combines spectral feature sharing and robust optimization strategies to reach one-epoch training and state-of-the-art cross-dataset performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing unsupervised rPPG methods avoid ground-truth physiological labels but suffer from noisy and unstable gradients, slow convergence, and weak cross-domain generalization. The work is motivated by the need for a faster, more stable, and more generalizable unsupervised rPPG framework.

Method: The paper proposes FCUS-rPPG, which uses a spectrally shared backbone for BVP feature disentanglement and a unified optimization framework with three components: post-verification masking for filtering misleading gradients, perturbation-based loss landscape smoothing for flatter minima, and noise-aware null-space regularization to reduce noise-driven representation drift.

Result: Experiments on five datasets show that FCUS-rPPG converges in only one training epoch, while prior methods usually need tens to hundreds of epochs, and it consistently achieves state-of-the-art performance in cross-dataset evaluation.

Conclusion: FCUS-rPPG offers an efficient and robust unsupervised rPPG solution by improving convergence speed, optimization stability, and cross-dataset generalization, making it practical for real-world deployment.

Abstract: Remote photoplethysmography (rPPG) enables non-contact extraction of blood volume pulse (BVP) signals using consumer-grade cameras. Recent unsupervised rPPG methods learn BVP representations without requiring ground-truth physiological annotations, yet their optimization is often hindered by noisy and unstable gradients, resulting in slow convergence and limited cross-domain generalization. In this paper, we propose FCUS-rPPG, a fast-converging unsupervised rPPG framework with strong generalization capability. Motivated by the observation that BVP representations exhibit both multi-spectral covariation and low-dimensional manifold structure, we design a spectrally shared backbone that facilitates BVP feature disentanglement while improving optimization efficiency. To jointly enhance convergence stability and generalization performance, we further develop a unified optimization framework operating at the gradient, loss-landscape, and feature-representation levels. Specifically, a post-verification masking mechanism filters out misleading gradients according to the weak-amplitude physiological prior of BVP signals; a perturbation-based loss landscape smoothing strategy steers optimization toward more generalizable flat minima; and a noise-aware null-space regularization constrains feature updates to the orthogonal complement of the noise subspace, thereby mitigating noise-induced representation drift. Extensive experiments on five datasets demonstrate that FCUS-rPPG requires only one training epoch, whereas existing methods typically require tens to hundreds of epochs. Notably, FCUS-rPPG consistently achieves state-of-the-art (SOTA) performance in cross-dataset evaluations. This study provides an efficient and robust solution to the real-world deployment of unsupervised rPPG. The source code will be publicly available at https://github.com/JiaJieLee/FCUS-rPPG.

</details>


### [27] [ROBUST-WT: Robust Uncertainty-aware Segmentation Transform via Whitening and Training Enhancements](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03069)
*Aqsa Naseer,Maryam Bibi,Syeda Samiya Urooj,Muhammad Khurram Shahzad*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 论文不是改WT-PSE结构本身，而是系统修正其训练流程；通过更真实的域增强、BCE+Dice、课程式权重调度和可控消融，分割性能从Dice 0.939提升到0.956。/ The paper does not alter WT-PSE itself; it systematically improves the training pipeline with better augmentations, BCE+Dice, curriculum weighting, and controlled ablations, raising Dice from 0.939 to 0.956.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 原始WT-PSE虽然面向跨域泛化，但训练流程仍存在四个问题：增强不足、BCE对边界噪声敏感、缺少损失权重调度、以及缺乏系统消融控制，因此有必要从训练机制上改进其鲁棒性与可复现性。/ Although WT-PSE targets cross-domain generalization, its original training pipeline has four weaknesses: insufficient augmentation, BCE sensitivity to noisy boundaries, no loss-weight scheduling, and no systematic ablation controls, motivating a stronger and more reproducible training framework.

Method: 在WT-PSE框架上加入四项改进：面向域差异的增强策略（random erasing、gamma correction、salt-and-pepper noise）、混合BCE+Dice损失、基于课程学习的Dice权重调度，以及用于消融实验的命令行开关。/ The method enhances WT-PSE with four changes: domain-adaptive augmentation (random erasing, gamma correction, salt-and-pepper noise), a hybrid BCE+Dice loss, curriculum-based Dice weight scheduling, and CLI ablation switches.

Result: 在眼底视盘分割基准上，改进后的流程取得最终epoch Dice 0.956、ASD 13.31，优于基线epoch-5 Dice 0.939，表明其在跨域分割中带来一致增益。/ On the fundus optic-disc benchmark, the improved pipeline reaches a final-epoch Dice of 0.956 and ASD of 13.31, outperforming the baseline epoch-5 Dice of 0.939, showing consistent gains for cross-domain segmentation.

Conclusion: 训练层面的改进可以在不改变WT-PSE核心架构的前提下，稳定提升跨域医学图像分割性能。/ Training-level refinements can consistently improve cross-domain medical image segmentation without modifying the core WT-PSE architecture.

Abstract: Generalized segmentation of medical images prevents performance degradation when different imaging devices and clinical protocols are used across multiple domains. The Whitening Transform-based Probabilistic Shape Regularization Extractor (WT-PSE), published in IEEE Transactions on Medical Imaging in 2024, addresses this challenge by employing feature decorrelation and Wasserstein distance-based knowledge distillation to achieve robust cross-domain segmentation. This study systematically examines improvements to the WT-PSE learning framework. Four limitations in the original implementation are identified: limited training augmentations that fail to simulate real scanner variations, reliance on per-pixel binary cross-entropy loss that is sensitive to edge noise, the absence of a scheduled loss weighting strategy that may destabilize early training, and the lack of ablation switches for controlled scientific comparison. To address these issues, we propose four enhancements: (1) domain-adaptive augmentation including random erasing, gamma correction, and salt-and-pepper noise; (2) a hybrid BCE and Dice loss function for improved edge-aware segmentation under noisy conditions; (3) a curriculum-based Dice weight scheduling strategy; and (4) command-line control flags for systematic ablation studies. Experiments on the fundus optic disc segmentation benchmark demonstrate that the improved pipeline achieves a final epoch optic-disc Dice score of 0.956 and an ASD score of 13.31, outperforming the baseline epoch-5 Dice score of 0.939. These results indicate that training-level improvements can provide consistent performance gains without modifying the underlying WT-PSE architecture.

</details>


### [28] [TGV-KV: Text-Grounded KV Eviction for Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03075)
*Jizhihui Liu,Ruizi Han,Miao Zhang,Rui Shao,Xuebo Liu,Weili Guan,Yaowei Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A text-guided KV-cache pruning method for VLMs that keeps almost all accuracy while using only 5% KV budget and significantly speeds up inference.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing KV-cache eviction methods are mainly designed for language models and perform poorly on VLMs because they ignore the modality gap and the high redundancy of visual tokens. The authors aim to reduce inference memory in VLMs without the usual large accuracy drop.

Method: The paper proposes Text-Grounded KV Eviction (TGV-KV) with three components: Text-Vision Budgeting (TVB) to allocate per-layer retention budget using mutual information, Text-Weighted Ranking (TWR) to rank visual token importance via weighted text-image attention, and Text-Prioritised Retention (TPR) to always preserve crucial text KV entries.

Result: Across five VLMs of different scales and architectures, TGV-KV retains 99.2% of full-KV accuracy on VizWiz-VQA with LLaVA-NeXT and improves end-to-end throughput by 52.6% at a very small 5% retention budget.

Conclusion: TGV-KV can drastically reduce KV-cache memory in vision-language models while maintaining near-full accuracy by evicting visual KV entries under text guidance and preserving textual KV.

Abstract: Vision-Language Models (VLMs) inherit the auto-regressive generation paradigm and cache the keys and values (KV) of all previous tokens to accelerate inference, resulting in memory consumption that scales linearly with context length. This issue is particularly pronounced in VLMs due to substantial redundancy in the visual modality. Although KV cache eviction approaches can effectively reduce inference memory, they often incur significant performance degradation in VLMs, as most are designed for language models and overlook the inherent gap between text and vision. By systematically analyzing the modality gap in VLMs in this work, we argue that the importance of visual information should be grounded in textual guidance and accordingly propose a Text-Grounded KV Eviction method for VLMs (TGV-KV). TGV-KV comprises three submodules: (1) Text-Vision Budgeting (TVB) assigns budget to each layer based on the mutual information interaction. (2) Text-Weighted Ranking (TWR) assesses the priority of text and ranks vision importance based on weighted text-image attention. (3) Text-Prioritised Retention (TPR) policy strategically preserves text KV to avoid acute information loss. We evaluate TGV-KV across five models with different sizes and architectures, showing that TGV-KV preserves 99.2% full-KV accuracy on the VizWiz-VQA task with LLaVA-NeXT and boosts end-to-end throughput by 52.6% with an extreme retention budget of 5%. Code is available at https://github.com/Danielement321/TGV-KV.

</details>


### [29] [Hierarchical Federated Learning with Dynamic Clustering and Adaptive Regularization for Robust Infrastructure Inspection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03084)
*Yuhu Feng,Keisuke Maeda,Takahiro Ogawa,Miki Haseyama*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 论文提出一种面向结构健康监测的分层联邦学习方法：先在全局上按结构退化特征动态聚类客户端，再在簇内依据Non-IID程度自适应调整近端正则化，从而同时处理跨结构差异和本地数据不平衡问题。 / TL;DR: The paper presents a hierarchical FL approach for SHM that first dynamically clusters clients by degradation behavior and then adaptively regularizes local training according to Non-IID severity, addressing both cross-structure and within-client heterogeneity.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究动机在于：结构健康监测中的计算机视觉模型受限于数据孤岛与隐私法规，难以进行跨机构协同训练；虽然联邦学习提供了隐私保护方案，但在全国性基础设施网络中又受到宏观结构差异与微观本地数据非独立同分布共同构成的“双重异质性”挑战。 / The motivation is that SHM vision models are constrained by data silos and privacy regulations, making cross-organization collaboration difficult; although FL is privacy-preserving, its deployment over nationwide infrastructure is blocked by 'double heterogeneity' from both cross-structure physical divergence and local statistical Non-IID imbalance.

Method: 方法上，论文设计了一个两层优化的分层联邦学习框架：宏观层面使用基于梯度的动态聚类，根据结构退化轨迹将客户端自动划分为专家组；微观层面在簇内引入DRAPR模块，通过实时计算Non-IID强度分数，并依据标签偏斜与梯度差异自适应调节近端正则项。 / Methodologically, the paper proposes a two-tier hierarchical FL framework: a macro-level dynamic gradient-based clustering mechanism that groups clients by structural degradation trajectories, and a micro-level DRAPR module that computes a real-time Non-IID intensity score to adaptively tune proximal regularization based on label skewness and gradient divergence.

Result: 在大规模真实结构巡检数据集上的实验表明，该框架能够有效中和双层异质性，减轻客户端漂移，避免少数损伤类别的灾难性遗忘，并提升诊断模型的鲁棒性与专业化能力。 / Results on a large-scale real-world structural inspection dataset show that the framework neutralizes dual-level heterogeneity, reduces client drift, prevents catastrophic forgetting of minority damage classes, and improves robustness and specialization.

Conclusion: 该论文提出的分层联邦学习框架通过宏观动态聚类与微观自适应正则化的协同设计，有效缓解了结构健康监测中的双重异质性问题，并在真实大规模数据集上获得了更鲁棒、更专业化的诊断模型。 / The paper concludes that its hierarchical federated learning framework, combining macro-level dynamic clustering and micro-level adaptive regularization, effectively addresses double heterogeneity in SHM and produces more robust, specialized diagnostic models on large real-world data.

Abstract: The deployment of data-driven computer vision models for structural health monitoring (SHM) is heavily constrained by the data silo dilemma due to stringent privacy and security regulations. While federated learning (FL) offers a privacy-preserving collaborative alternative, its application to nationwide infrastructure networks is severely hindered by the challenge of ``double heterogeneity'': macro-level physical divergence across disparate structural types and micro-level statistical imbalances within local datasets. To overcome this challenge, this paper proposes a novel hierarchical federated learning framework. The framework orchestrates a synergistic two-tier optimization strategy. At the macro-level, a dynamic gradient-based clustering mechanism autonomously aggregates distributed clients into specialized expert groups based on their structural degradation trajectories, circumventing the need for prior geographical metadata. Concurrently, at the micro-level, an intra-cluster Dynamic Region-Adaptive Proximal Regularization (DRAPR) module computes a real-time statistical Non-IID Intensity Score for each client. By adaptively modulating a proximal penalty based on local label skewness and gradient divergence, DRAPR effectively calibrates local updates, mitigates client drift, and prevents the catastrophic forgetting of minority damage classes. Comprehensive evaluations on a large-scale, real-world structural inspection dataset demonstrate that the hierarchical integration of macro-clustering and micro-regularization successfully neutralizes dual-level heterogeneity, yielding highly robust and specialized diagnostic models for complex infrastructure inspection.

</details>


### [30] [Zero-Shot 3D Question Answering via Hierarchical View-to-Token Transportation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03100)
*Dongsheng Wang,Dawei Su,Hui Huang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KeyVT 是一种面向零样本 3D 场景理解的分层输入筛选方法：先选最有价值的视图，再用最优传输挑选最有代表性的 tokens，从而在有限上下文预算下提升 2D VLM 对 3D 任务的效果。 / KeyVT is a hierarchical input selection method for zero-shot 3D scene understanding that first chooses the most informative views and then selects representative tokens via optimal transport, improving 3D reasoning under limited context budgets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：现有基于 2D VLM 的零样本 3D 理解依赖从点云采样的多视图输入，但输入预算有限，如何尽量保留与任务相关的 3D 细节成为核心问题。 / The motivation is that zero-shot 3D understanding with 2D VLMs depends on sampled multi-view inputs, but under a limited input budget it is difficult to preserve as much task-relevant 3D detail as possible.

Method: 方法上，KeyVT 先在视图级结合像素特征与相机参数，从语义与几何位置两方面评估视图重要性；再在 token 级利用最优传输从选中视图的 patch tokens 中挑选代表性 key tokens，以减少冗余并覆盖关键特征。 / Methodologically, KeyVT first scores views by combining pixel features and camera parameters to model both semantic relevance and geometric position, then applies optimal transport at the token level to select representative key tokens from patch tokens while reducing redundancy.

Result: 实验在三个常用基准上验证了该方法的有效性，结果显示其相较现有无需调优的方法有显著提升，同时性能可与训练式方法相比。 / Experiments on three widely used benchmarks show substantial gains over existing tuning-free methods and performance comparable to training-based approaches.

Conclusion: 该论文提出了 KeyVT，通过视图级与 token 级的层次化上下文选择，在零样本 3D 场景理解中更高效地保留任务相关信息，并在多个基准上取得了显著优于免训练方法、接近训练型方法的性能。 / The paper concludes that KeyVT, a hierarchical view- and token-level context selection framework, preserves task-relevant 3D information more effectively for zero-shot 3D scene understanding and achieves strong benchmark performance, outperforming tuning-free baselines and approaching training-based methods.

Abstract: Recently, zero-shot 3D scene understanding via 2D Vision-Language Models (VLMs) has gained increasing research interest due to their promising spatial reasoning capabilities. Typically, multiple 2D views are sampled from a 3D point cloud and fed into pre-trained VLMs to answer a given question. This paradigm highlights the critical role of input context quality and raises the challenge of retaining as many task-relevant 3D details as possible under a limited input budget. We propose \texttt{KeyVT}, a hierarchical approach for input context collection at both the view and token levels. Specifically, we combine pixel features with camera parameters and assess view importance based on both semantic content and geometric position, resulting in spatially consistent and task-relevant views. Furthermore, we address redundancy among patches across selected views by identifying representative tokens under the optimal transport (OT) framework, where view tokens and key tokens are formulated as two discrete distributions in the embedding space. These key tokens are expected to cover all view features by minimizing the OT distance. We evaluate our framework on three widely used benchmarks, demonstrating significant improvements over existing tuning-free methods and performance comparable to training-based approaches.

</details>


### [31] [Inverting the Generation Process of Denoising Diffusion Implicit Models: Empirical Evaluation and a Novel Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03111)
*Yan Zeng,Masanori Suganuma,Takayuki Okatani*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: A hybrid DDIM inversion approach using gradient descent first and fixed-point updates afterward more accurately recovers the original noise latent and outperforms prior methods in reconstruction and interpolation-based evaluation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing DDIM inversion methods can reconstruct images reasonably well but often fail to accurately recover the true initial latent/noise map, limiting reliable analysis and editing of diffusion-generated images.

Method: The paper uses a hybrid inversion strategy: direct gradient-descent-based inversion for the first DDIM step, then a fixed-point method for the remaining denoising steps.

Result: Experiments on three datasets show substantially improved initial latent prediction, stronger reconstruction accuracy, and clear gains on a new self-interpolation evaluation compared with existing inversion baselines.

Conclusion: The proposed hybrid DDIM inversion method recovers the initial noise latent more accurately than prior approaches and achieves the best reconstruction and self-interpolation performance across datasets.

Abstract: This paper studies the problem of inverting the DDIM image generation process to recover latent variables, particularly the initial noise map, from a generated image. Existing methods often struggle with accuracy in this task. We propose a novel hybrid approach that combines direct inversion via gradient descent for the first step, followed by a fixed-point method for subsequent steps. Empirical evaluations across three datasets demonstrate that our method significantly improves the prediction of initial latent variables while achieving superior reconstruction accuracy. Additionally, we introduce a new evaluation, called the self-interpolation test, which assesses the quality of images generated from interpolated points between the true and predicted latent maps, offering deeper insights into performance. Our results reveal that while existing methods perform reasonably well in reconstruction, they consistently fail to accurately predict the initial latent variables, resulting in poor performance on the self-interpolation test. In contrast, our method outperforms all others across all metrics, providing valuable insights into diffusion models and enhancing their applications in image generation and editing.

</details>


### [32] [FAF-CD: Frequency-Aware Fusion for Change Detection under Imperfect Multimodal Remote Sensing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03114)
*Yufan Wang,Sokratis Makrogiannis,Chandra Kambhamettu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: FAF-CD uses spatial alignment plus frequency and wavelet cues to distinguish real changes from nuisance variation in heterogeneous remote sensing images, delivering better robustness and accuracy than strong baselines at lower computational cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Real-world remote sensing change detection depends on imperfect heterogeneous observations, where temporal asynchrony, cross-sensor gaps, illumination, seasonal changes, and modality shifts create nuisance differences that can be mistaken for true structural change, particularly in EO-SAR disaster assessment.

Method: The paper proposes FAF-CD, a frequency-aware hybrid architecture using a DINOv3-pretrained ConvNeXt encoder and a linear-complexity VMamba decoder. Its key module is a rectification-aware tri-branch fusion design that combines deformable spatial alignment with Fourier-domain and Haar-wavelet comparisons, then adaptively gates multi-scale complementary cues.

Result: On BRIGHT validation, FAF-CD outperforms NeXt2Former-CD in both clean and perturbed tc-mIoU/tc-mAP after matched heterogeneous EO-SAR adaptation. It also generalizes well to binary optical change detection, reaching 0.924 cF1 on LEVIR-CD and 0.955 cF1 on WHU-CD, and achieves the best average perturbed cIoU/cF1 on both datasets under pseudo-change-aligned stress tests. In addition, it reduces computation by about 24 GFLOPs compared with NeXt2Former-CD.

Conclusion: FAF-CD is an effective and efficient framework for heterogeneous remote sensing change detection, especially EO-SAR disaster mapping, improving robustness under nuisance variations while reducing computational cost.

Abstract: Remote sensing change detection for real-world monitoring often relies on imperfect heterogeneous observations, where pre- and post-event images may be asynchronous, cross-sensor, or affected by illumination, seasonal, and modality shifts. This setting is especially challenging for EO-SAR disaster mapping, where nuisance variation can resemble structural damage. We propose FAF-CD, a frequency-aware hybrid framework with a DINOv3-pretrained ConvNeXt encoder and a linear-complexity VMamba-based decoder. Its rectification-aware tri-branch fusion module combines deformable spatial alignment with Fourier and Haar-wavelet comparisons, using adaptive gating to aggregate complementary cues across scales. On BRIGHT validation, a matched heterogeneous EO-SAR adaptation improves clean and perturbed tc-mIoU/tc-mAP over NeXt2Former-CD. FAF-CD also generalizes to binary optical CD, achieving 0.924 cF1 on LEVIR-CD and 0.955 cF1 on WHU-CD, and obtains the best average perturbed cIoU/cF1 on both binary datasets among M-CD and NeXt2Former-CD under pseudo-change-aligned stress tests. It further reduces cost by approximately 24 GFLOPs relative to NeXt2Former-CD while maintaining or improving accuracy.

</details>


### [33] [GuidedBridge: Training-freely Improving Bridge Models with Prior Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03119)
*Zehua Chen,Yucheng Yang,Binjie Yuan,Kaiwen Zheng,Jun S. Liu,Jun Zhu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper proposes a training-free guidance method for bridge generative models: weaken the prior, compare the degraded output with the normal-prior output, and use that difference as guidance. A frequency-aware version and a CFG-based in-painting variant further improve quality, and experiments show consistent gains on image translation tasks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing guidance methods such as CFG and AG improve diffusion generation, while bridge models enable data-to-data generation using clean priors. However, prior exploitation in bridge models is still underused and not well understood. The paper is motivated by the idea that introducing controlled quality differences between denoising outputs can reveal and strengthen how bridge models use priors, yielding a practical, training-free improvement method.

Method: The method introduces a training-free guidance strategy for bridge models. It compares denoising results produced with a seen clean prior versus a deliberately weakened unseen prior, and uses their difference as guidance scaled during sampling. The authors further design Frequency-Modulated Prior Guidance (FMPG), which applies different guidance strengths to low- and high-frequency components according to bridge generation dynamics. For image in-painting, they propose a cascaded CFG-FMPG pipeline that first uses classifier-free guidance to generate a noisy hidden representation, then uses that representation as a generative prior under FMPG.

Result: Experiments show that the proposed PG methods consistently enhance pre-trained bridge models across multiple image translation tasks. The frequency-aware version FMPG better aligns guidance with the model’s low/high-frequency generative behavior, and the cascaded CFG-FMPG framework is effective for image in-painting while maintaining inference efficiency.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that Prior Guidance (PG), especially its frequency-modulated variant FMPG and the cascaded CFG-FMPG framework, can consistently improve pre-trained bridge models on diverse image translation and image in-painting tasks without additional training and without sacrificing inference efficiency.

Abstract: Guidance methods, such as classifier-free guidance (CFG) and auto-guidance (AG), have advanced noise-to-data generation in diffusion models. Recently, bridge models have introduced a data-to-data generative process that can exploit an instructive clean prior. In this work, inspired by previous methods creating quality difference between denoising results as guidance, we propose a training-free bridge guidance method, termed Prior Guidance (PG). Specifically, we introduce a weak prior, which is unseen during bridge pre-training, hindering prior exploitation and thereby degrading denoising result. Then, we contrast it with the seen prior to highlight and enhance prior exploitation via a scaling factor. Moreover, we analyze the underlying mechanism of prior exploitation in the bridge process and design frequency-modulated prior guidance (FMPG), which tailors the guidance scale to low- and high-frequency bands coherent with bridge generative dynamics. To address prior exploitation in image in-painting, we develop a cascaded framework, CFG-FMPG, which first generates a noisy hidden representation via CFG and then exploits it as a generative prior with FMPG, fulfilling their complementary strengths without compromising inference efficiency. Experiments demonstrate that our PG methods consistently improve pre-trained bridge models across diverse image translation tasks.

</details>


### [34] [KC-3DGS: Kurtosis-Constrained Gaussian Splatting for High-Fidelity View Synthesis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03120)
*Vivekjyoti Banerjee,Abhay Yadav,Rama Chellappa,Aniket Roy*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: KC-3DGS is a plug-and-play wavelet-regularized 3DGS method that reduces oversmoothing by explicitly supervising frequency structure, yielding better perceptual and quantitative results, especially for sparse-view novel view synthesis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard 3DGS relies on pixel-space losses such as L1 and SSIM, which only constrain aggregate reconstruction error and allow errors to be redistributed across frequency bands. This causes oversmoothing and structural artifacts, particularly in sparse-view scenarios where supervision is weak.

Method: The paper augments standard 3DGS training with three wavelet-based objectives: a multi-scale wavelet coefficient alignment loss for high-frequency detail recovery, a supervised kurtosis concentration loss to match heavy-tailed natural image frequency statistics, and a cross-band covariance penalty to encourage frequency specialization. It also provides a theoretical analysis of why pixel-space losses alone allow degenerate frequency-redistributed solutions.

Result: Across MipNeRF360, Tanks&Temples, MVImgNet, DeepBlending, and WRIVA-ULTRRA, KC-3DGS consistently improves perceptual quality. On WRIVA-ULTRRA it improves DreamSim by 9.48% while also improving PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. In sparse-view settings with 12 training images, it improves PSNR by up to 0.5 dB on MipNeRF360 while maintaining perceptual quality.

Conclusion: KC-3DGS shows that adding wavelet-domain regularization to 3D Gaussian Splatting improves perceptual fidelity and reconstruction quality, especially under sparse-view supervision, while remaining a plug-and-play extension to existing 3DGS pipelines.

Abstract: 3D Gaussian Splatting (3DGS) enables real-time novel view synthesis by representing scenes as collections of anisotropic Gaussians optimized via differentiable rasterization. However, standard pixel-space losses (L1, SSIM) constrain only aggregate reconstruction error, permitting the optimization to redistribute error across frequency scales. This leads to oversmoothing and structural artifacts, particularly in sparse-view settings where supervision is limited. We propose KC-3DGS, which augments 3DGS training with wavelet-domain supervision based on natural image statistics. Our method combines three components: (1) a multi-scale wavelet coefficient alignment loss that explicitly penalizes missing high-frequency detail, (2) a supervised kurtosis concentration loss that encourages rendered images to match the heavy-tailed frequency statistics of ground-truth images, and (3) a cross-band covariance penalty that promotes frequency specialization. We provide theoretical analysis showing that pixel-space losses admit a family of indistinguishable perturbations under wavelet redistribution, and that our joint objective excludes degenerate solutions. Experiments across MipNeRF360, Tanks&Temples, MVImgNet, DeepBlending, and WRIVA-ULTRRA demonstrate consistent improvements in perceptual quality. On the challenging WRIVA-ULTRRA outdoor dataset, KC-3DGS achieves a 9.48% improvement in DreamSim while also improving PSNR, SSIM, and LPIPS. In sparse-view settings with only 12 training images, our method improves PSNR by up to 0.5 dB on MipNeRF360 while maintaining perceptual quality. The approach integrates seamlessly into existing 3DGS pipelines as a plug-and-play regularization strategy.

</details>


### [35] [Disentangling Visual and Factual Correctness in LVLMs' Visualization Literacy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03142)
*Soohyun Lee,Jaeyoung Kim,Seokhyeon Park,Sihyeon Lee,Jiwon Song,Bohyoung Kim,Hyunjoo Song,Jinwook Seo*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper shows that many LVLM benchmark results overstate or distort true visual reasoning because models may answer from memorized facts instead of the chart. It proposes CVLAT and VFRI to measure whether models trust visual evidence or factual priors under conflict, finding that this arbitration behavior varies widely across models and must be evaluated explicitly.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing visualization-literacy evaluations for LVLMs confound true chart-based reasoning with factual priors learned during training. As a result, correct answers may be misattributed to visual understanding, while genuine visual interpretation may be hidden when models favor memorized facts.

Method: The paper introduces a framework that separates visual correctness from factual correctness. It evaluates 15 state-of-the-art LVLMs across three experiments using standard VLAT, randomized-data reVLAT, and the proposed Counterfactual Visualization Literacy Assessment Test (CVLAT). It also defines capability-normalized arbitration metrics and the Visual-Factual Reliance Index (VFRI), includes a human baseline on counterfactual items, and tests prompt-based interventions to shift model prioritization.

Result: Some LVLMs achieve human-level scores on standard VLAT, but this can stem from factual recall rather than visual understanding. reVLAT can also underestimate visualization literacy when factual priors override correct chart reading. CVLAT and VFRI reveal that most tested models are relatively visualization-oriented, while a minority are more fact-oriented; several models fall near zero and are harder to classify. Human participants strongly prioritized chart evidence under conflict. Prompting can alter reliance patterns, but effects vary by model and are asymmetric, and stronger chart-reading ability does not imply better prompt controllability.

Conclusion: High visualization accuracy in LVLMs does not by itself demonstrate faithful visual reasoning. Reliable deployment in visual analytics requires measuring how models arbitrate between chart evidence and memorized factual priors when they conflict.

Abstract: Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) show strong visualization interpretation, yet it is unclear whether their responses reflect genuine reasoning over visual evidence or factual priors learned during training. Current evaluations mix these two sources, obscuring when correct visual interpretation is overridden by memorized facts. We present a framework that isolates visual correctness from factual correctness, revealing validity limitations in existing visualization literacy assessments. Across three experiments with 15 state-of-the-art LVLMs: (1) several models reach human-level performance on standard tests (VLAT), but this may reflect factual recall rather than visual understanding, while randomized-data tests (reVLAT) underestimate literacy when correct visual interpretation is superseded by factual priors. (2) Using our Counterfactual Visualization Literacy Assessment Test (CVLAT) with capability-normalized arbitration metrics, we classify models by the sign of their visual-factual reliance index (VFRI), revealing a visualization-oriented majority and a factual knowledge-oriented minority, though several near-zero cases warrant caution. A human baseline (N=30) on the same counterfactual items confirms that people overwhelmingly follow the chart under conflict, providing a human reference point. (3) Prompt-based intervention can shift prioritization, but its effectiveness is highly model-dependent and direction-asymmetric, and high chart-reading capability does not predict prompt-controllability. Overall, high visualization accuracy is not sufficient evidence of faithful visual reasoning: reliable integration into visual analytics requires evaluating not only visualization literacy but also how models arbitrate between visual evidence and factual priors when the two diverge. Benchmark and code: https://github.com/JaeyoungKim-HCIL/CVLAT

</details>


### [36] [$A^2$: Smaller Self-Supervised ViTs Localize Better than Larger Ones](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03148)
*Sreehari Rammohan,Huy Ha,Carl Vondrick*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: Use a small ViT to decide where to look and a large ViT to decide what to extract; this simple pretrained-only pipeline improves robustness to spurious context and distribution shift.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Robust classification requires focusing on foreground objects instead of contextual distractors. The authors observe an inverse scaling effect: smaller self-supervised ViTs localize foreground objects better than larger ones, while larger ViTs provide richer patch representations. This motivates combining both strengths.

Method: The paper decouples localization and representation learning: a small pretrained ViT identifies attention peaks to localize foreground objects, then cropped regions around those peaks are embedded by a larger pretrained ViT for classification. The approach uses only pretrained features and avoids per-dataset attention/backbone retraining.

Result: Across five benchmarks, A^2 is competitive with backbone-matched loss-level methods such as DFR and outperforms end-to-end attention training under stronger distribution shifts.

Conclusion: A^2 demonstrates that combining a small self-supervised ViT for attention-based localization with a large ViT for feature extraction yields robust visual classification under distribution shift, without extra training or group labels.

Abstract: Robust visual classification often depends on localizing the main foreground objects in an image while ignoring contextual distractors. Surprisingly, we find that the attention maps of smaller self-supervised ViTs localize foreground objects better than those of larger ViTs. However, we still need large ViTs, because they extract richer representations from each patch. To get the best of both worlds, good localization and rich representations, we propose $A^2$, a simple method that leverages this inverse scaling finding by decoupling where to look (a small attention model) from what to extract (a large embedding model): we crop around the attention peaks of a small model and embed the crops with a larger model. $A^2$ uses entirely pretrained features, requires no group labels, and does not require per-dataset attention or backbone training. Across 5 benchmarks, $A^2$ is competitive with backbone-matched loss-level methods like DFR, and outperforms end-to-end attention training under stronger distribution shifts.

</details>


### [37] [NVIDIA OmniDreams: Real-Time Generative World Model for Closed-Loop Autonomous Vehicle Simulation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03159)
*NVIDIA,:,Aarti Basant,Amlan Kar,Despoina Paschalidou,Fangyin Wei,Francesco Ferroni,Guillermo Garcia Cobo,Haithem Turki,Huan Ling,Jaewoo Seo,James Lucas,Jay Zhangjie Wu,Jialiang Wang,Jonathan Lorraine,Jun Gao,Kai He,Katarina Tothova,Kevin Xie,Michał Tyszkiewicz,Qi Wu,Riccardo de Lutio,Ruilong Li,Sanja Fidler,Seung Wook Kim,Tianchang Shen,Tianshi Cao,Tobias Pfaff,William Lew,Xindi Wu,Xuanchi Ren,Yifan Lu,Yuxuan Zhang,Zan Gojcic,Zian Wang*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: OmniDreams is a real-time generative world model for autonomous driving simulation that produces action-conditioned sensor videos in closed loop, handles rare and novel scenarios better than reconstruction-based simulators, and also shows promise as an efficient policy backbone.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing closed-loop simulators and reconstruction-based neural simulators are limited in long-tail autonomous driving scenarios because they depend heavily on captured data and generalize poorly to novel, highly dynamic, or rare conditions such as extreme weather and unusual agent behavior. A more reactive and generalizable simulator is needed for safe policy evaluation.

Method: The paper mid-trains and post-trains a foundation diffusion model, Cosmos, on 21k hours of driving data to build OmniDreams, an autoregressive world model that generates action-conditioned videos in real time based on past frames, current simulator state, and immediate driving actions. It is deployed in a closed-loop system with Alpamayo 1 and AlpaSim, and a world-action model is further post-trained from OmniDreams for policy evaluation.

Result: OmniDreams can synthesize photorealistic and complex unobserved driving scenarios in real time within a closed-loop setup. Preliminary results also show that a world-action model derived from OmniDreams outperforms the VLA-based Alpamayo 1.5 policy on the Physical AI Autonomous Vehicles NuRec dataset while using only one-fifth of the parameters.

Conclusion: OmniDreams demonstrates that a real-time, action-conditioned generative world model can serve as a scalable and photorealistic closed-loop simulator for autonomous driving, and it may also function as a strong backbone for policy learning.

Abstract: As autonomous vehicle capabilities advance, the safe evaluation of driving policies in long-tail scenarios remains a critical bottleneck. In closed-loop simulation, the driving policy model actively interacts with the environment, where its actions dynamically update the simulator state and directly influence the next set of generated sensor observations. While recent reconstruction-based neural simulators offer photorealism, they are fundamentally constrained by their initial captured data and struggle to generalize to highly dynamic or novel scenes. To overcome these limitations, we introduce OmniDreams, a foundation generative world model mid- and post-trained from the Cosmos diffusion model to autoregressively generate action-conditioned videos in real time. By leveraging the rich visual priors of Cosmos and mid- and post-training on 21k hours of driving scenarios, OmniDreams synthesizes complex, unobserved phenomena that are hard for traditional simulators to capture, such as extreme weather and unpredictable dynamic agent behaviors. Crucially, it autoregressively conditions its photorealistic sensor generation on past frames, the current simulator state, and immediate driving actions. Deployed in a closed-loop system with the Alpamayo 1 policy model and AlpaSim orchestrator, OmniDreams acts as a highly responsive, reactive environment, providing a scalable and comprehensive solution for training and evaluating next-generation autonomous driving policies. We additionally show preliminary results indicating that a world-action model (WAM) post-trained from OmniDreams achieves strong performance on the Physical AI Autonomous Vehicles NuRec dataset, surpassing the VLA-based Alpamayo 1.5 research policy model while using only 1/5 the total parameters. These results highlight the potential for a real-time world model like OmniDreams to also serve as a backbone for policy architectures.

</details>


### [38] [SRENet: Spectral Re-Entry Network for Point Cloud Action Recognition](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03160)
*Qiuxia Wu,Jiarui Lan,Wenxiong Kang,Zhiyong Wang,Kun Hu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: SRENet把点云动作识别转化为频率建模问题：先用小波分解提取低/高频运动信息，再通过重分解修复融合后的时序频谱结构，并用从粗到细的课程学习增强判别性，最终达到SOTA。 / SRENet treats point cloud action recognition as a frequency modeling problem: it first uses wavelet decomposition to capture low/high-frequency motion cues, then re-decomposes features to repair temporal spectral structure after fusion, and finally uses coarse-to-fine curriculum learning to improve discriminability, achieving SOTA performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 点云序列的人体动作识别对自动驾驶和人机交互等3D感知应用很重要，但点云的不规则结构和时间不一致性使时空表示学习困难，尤其难以同时建模全局运动信息和细粒度时间动态。 / Human action recognition from point cloud sequences is important for 3D perception applications such as autonomous driving and human-computer interaction, but the irregular structure and temporal inconsistency of point clouds make spatio-temporal representation learning difficult, especially when jointly modeling global motion context and fine-grained temporal dynamics.

Method: 提出SRENet，包括：1）SDeBlock，在时间和空间轴上进行基于小波的频谱分解，将特征拆分为低频和高频成分，并施加频率特定注意力；2）SReBlock，对融合后失真的时间频率结构进行二次时间分解，以恢复残余动态并重对齐频率结构；3）频谱感知学习策略，结合对比损失与课程学习，使训练重点从低频逐步转向高频。 / The paper proposes SRENet with: (1) SDeBlock, which applies wavelet-based spectral decomposition along temporal and spatial axes to separate low- and high-frequency components with frequency-specific attention; (2) SReBlock, which performs secondary temporal decomposition to recover residual dynamics and realign distorted temporal frequency structures after semantic fusion; and (3) a spectral-aware learning strategy combining contrastive loss and curriculum learning that gradually shifts focus from low- to high-frequency subspaces.

Result: 在MSR-Action3D、NTU-RGBD和NTU-RGBD120上的大量实验表明，SRENet取得了最先进的性能。 / Extensive experiments on MSR-Action3D, NTU-RGBD, and NTU-RGBD120 show that SRENet achieves state-of-the-art performance.

Conclusion: SRENet通过频谱视角对点云动作序列进行建模，能够同时捕获全局运动上下文与细粒度时序动态，并在多个基准数据集上取得SOTA表现，证明了频率建模对点云动作识别的有效性。 / SRENet models point cloud action sequences from a spectral perspective, jointly capturing global motion context and fine-grained temporal dynamics, and achieves state-of-the-art results on multiple benchmarks, validating the effectiveness of frequency modeling for point cloud action recognition.

Abstract: Recognizing human actions from point cloud sequences is critical for 3D perception driven applications such as autonomous driving and human-computer interaction. However, the irregular structure and temporal inconsistency of point clouds pose unique challenges for spatio-temporal representation learning, especially in capturing both global motion context and fine-grained temporal dynamics. We propose SRENet, a spectral-aware framework designed to explicitly learn both global context and fine-grained temporal dynamics of motion from a frequency perspective for action recognition. SRENet introduces a Spectral Decomposition Block (SDeBlock) that performs wavelet-based analysis along temporal and spatial axes, disentangling features into low- and high-frequency components with frequency-specific attention. To recover residual dynamics and re-align temporal frequency structures distorted during semantic fusion, a Spectral Re-entry Block (SReBlock) performs secondary temporal decomposition. Furthermore, a spectral-aware learning strategy is devised to enhance discriminability in both frequency subspaces via contrastive loss and a curriculum schedule that gradually shifts focus from low- to high-frequency spaces in line with coarse to detailed motion patterns. Extensive experiments on MSR-Action3D, NTU-RGBD and NTU-RGBD120 demonstrate that SRENet achieves state-of-the-art performance, validating the effectiveness of frequency modeling in point cloud-based action understanding.

</details>


### [39] [JAVEDIT: Joint Audio-Visual Instruction-Guided Video Editing with Agentic Data Curation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03168)
*Yinan Chen,Chuming Lin,Zhennan Chen,Yuxiang Zeng,Junwei Zhu,Yali Bi,Xijie Huang,Chengming Xu,Donghao Luo,Zhucun Xue,Xiaobin Hu,Chengjie Wang,Yong Liu,Jiangning Zhang,Shuicheng Yan*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: The paper introduces the first major dataset and benchmark for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing and a baseline model that beats existing methods on most evaluation metrics.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that instruction-based video editing has advanced, but joint audio-visual editing remains limited because there is no dedicated large-scale dataset or benchmark for training and evaluation.

Method: The authors construct JAVEdit-100k, a dataset of about 100K instruction-based audio-visual editing triplets for human-centric videos, using four carefully designed generation pipelines and an agent-in-the-loop quality control process. They also build JAVEditBench for standardized evaluation and introduce JAVEdit as a baseline model for joint audio-visual editing.

Result: JAVEdit-100k is presented as the first large-scale, high-quality dataset for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing, JAVEditBench provides a comprehensive benchmark, and the JAVEdit baseline achieves the best performance among compared methods on five out of six evaluation metrics.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that large-scale, high-quality data and standardized evaluation are critical for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing, and that the proposed JAVEdit dataset, benchmark, and baseline model provide a strong foundation for the field. Experimental results show the baseline outperforms prior methods on five of six metrics.

Abstract: While instruction-based video editing has seen significant progress, joint audio-visual editing remains constrained by the absence of dedicated datasets and benchmarks. To bridge this gap, we present JAVEdit-100k, the first large-scale, high-quality dataset tailored for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing. Focusing on human-centric videos, JAVEdit-100k comprises approximately 100K editing triplets spanning five distinct categories, including subject editing and speech editing. This dataset is rigorously constructed via four meticulously designed generation pipelines, seamlessly paired with an agent-in-the-loop quality control mechanism. Furthermore, to address the lack of standardized evaluation within the field, we introduce JAVEditBench, a comprehensive benchmark featuring curated source videos and human-aligned instructions across all editing categories. Finally, we propose JAVEdit, a pioneering baseline model for instruction-guided joint audio-visual editing. Experiments show that \model\ outperforms all baselines on five of six evaluation metrics.

</details>


### [40] [Ask When It Pays: Cost-Aware Open-Ended Interaction for Instance Goal Navigation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03175)
*Xunyi Zhao,Sihao Lin,Gengze Zhou,Zerui Li,Shijie Li,Wei Tao,Jiajun Liu,Qi Wu*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: 中文：这篇论文研究“导航时该不该问、该问什么、问一次值不值”。核心做法是给不同类型的问题设定成本，用信息增益衡量其价值，并让导航智能体只在收益大于代价时才提问，同时用新的基准和加权成功率指标评估交互效率。英文：This paper studies when an embodied navigator should ask questions and which questions are worth asking. It assigns costs to query types, values them by information gain, lets the agent query only when the expected benefit exceeds the cost, and evaluates efficiency with a new benchmark and a weighted success metric.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中文：现有交互式IGN方法虽然允许向oracle提问，但没有区分低成本澄清与高价值路径指导，导致智能体可能通过反复提出高信息量问题来“刷”成功率，而不是高效消解歧义。现有基准也缺乏对不同提问成本和交互效率的建模。英文：Prior interactive IGN methods allow oracle queries but fail to distinguish lightweight clarification from route-level guidance, enabling agents to improve success by repeatedly asking high-information questions instead of efficiently resolving ambiguity. Existing benchmarks also do not account for query cost or interaction efficiency.

Method: 中文：作者先对现有导航语料进行信息增益分析，识别最能降低导航不确定性的线索，进而构建紧凑的问题类型集合及数据驱动权重；随后基于该分类设计新基准与加权成功率指标，并提出一个零样本多模态大语言模型导航器，在每个决策步仅当预期收益超过提问代价时才发起查询。英文：The method performs an information-gain analysis on existing navigation corpora to identify cues that most reduce navigation uncertainty, derives a compact taxonomy of question types with data-driven weights, builds a benchmark and a Weighted Success Rate metric, and proposes a zero-shot MLLM navigator that queries only when expected uncertainty reduction justifies the cost.

Result: 中文：本文产出包括：一个基于信息增益的提问类型分类及权重、一套用于诊断交互行为与效率的新基准、一个带提问惩罚的Weighted Success Rate指标，以及一个能够选择性提问的零样本MLLM导航器。摘要未提供具体数值结果。英文：The paper delivers an information-gain-based taxonomy of question types with weights, a benchmark for diagnosing interaction behavior and efficiency, a Weighted Success Rate metric that penalizes queries, and a zero-shot MLLM navigator with selective querying. The abstract does not report numerical performance results.

Conclusion: 中文：本文将交互式实例目标导航重新定义为一个成本敏感的不确定性降低问题，并提出应按“单位代价带来的不确定性下降”来决定是否提问及提什么问题。英文：The paper reframes interactive instance goal navigation as a cost-sensitive uncertainty-reduction problem, arguing that agents should choose queries based on uncertainty reduction per unit interaction cost.

Abstract: Instance Goal Navigation (IGN) requires an embodied agent to find a specific object instance among distractors from an underspecified natural-language description. Such ambiguity often cannot be resolved from perception and language alone, making interaction with an oracle a natural mechanism for disambiguation. Prior interactive methods allow oracle queries but treat lightweight clarification and route-level guidance alike, letting agents boost success rate through repeated high-information questions rather than by resolving the underlying ambiguity efficiently. We recast interactive IGN as a cost-sensitive uncertainty-reduction problem, where the agent should ask the question whose answer provides the largest reduction in navigation uncertainty relative to its penalty. To this end, we apply an information-gain analysis on existing navigation corpora to identify which cues reduce navigation uncertainty, yielding a compact set of question types and data-derived weights.However, existing interactive navigation benchmarks do not model the cost of different question types or evaluate how efficiently agents use interaction, making them unsuitable for studying cost-sensitive interaction. Based on this taxonomy, we construct a benchmark for diagnosing interaction behavior and efficiency, together with a Weighted Success Rate metric that penalizes each query by its derived cost. We further propose a zero-shot MLLM navigator that selectively queries at each decision step only when the expected uncertainty reduction justifies the interaction cost.

</details>


### [41] [GLINT: Sparsely Gated Vision-Language Alignment for Fine-Grained Radiology Representations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03180)
*Jonggwon Park,Seongeun Lee,Junhyun Park,Hannah Yun,Hyunwoong Kim,Sohyun Jeong,Hyewon Kang,Byungmu Yoon,Kyoyun Choi*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: GLINT通过稀疏门控把文本只对齐到少量相关patch，并用SSL教师保持细粒度特征，因此在放射学图文学习中显著提升零样本定位与分割能力，且可泛化到2D CXR和3D CT。 / GLINT aligns text to only a small set of relevant patches via sparse gating and preserves fine-grained features with an SSL teacher, substantially improving zero-shot localization and segmentation in radiology across both 2D CXR and 3D CT.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机是解决放射学VLM中的监督粒度失配：病灶通常只占据图像中的小区域，但训练监督只来自全局图像-报告配对，导致已有方法对所有patch进行稠密加权，难以定位与特定文本相关的稀疏区域。 / The motivation is to address the supervision granularity mismatch in radiology VLMs: findings occupy only small image regions, while supervision is provided only at the global image-report level, causing prior methods to spread attention densely over patches instead of focusing on the sparse query-relevant subset.

Method: 方法提出GLINT框架，包含两部分：1）Sparsely Gated Alignment：在独立gate嵌入空间上用sigmoid门控，只激活与文本查询相关的少量图像patch；2）Dense Feature Regularization：将可训练编码器的中间特征约束到冻结的SSL教师特征，以保留细粒度局部表征。该方案分别用于2D CXR（基于DINOv3）和3D CT（基于V-JEPA 2.1）。 / The paper proposes GLINT with two key components: (1) Sparsely Gated Alignment, which uses a sigmoid gate in a separate gate-embedding space to activate only query-relevant patches; and (2) Dense Feature Regularization, which anchors intermediate features of the trainable encoder to a frozen SSL teacher to preserve fine-grained local representations. The same recipe is applied to 2D CXR with DINOv3 and 3D CT with V-JEPA 2.1.

Result: 结果显示，GLINT在最需要局部、查询特异定位的零样本grounding和分割上收益最显著；同时在分类、报告生成和分割等下游评测中也优于SSL编码器和医学VLM基线。 / Results show that GLINT achieves the largest gains on zero-shot grounding and segmentation, where sparse query-specific localization is critical, and also surpasses SSL encoders and medical VLM baselines on classification, report generation, and segmentation.

Conclusion: GLINT通过显式稀疏对齐和特征正则化，更有效地学习放射影像中的局部-文本对应关系，在零样本分类、grounding、分割以及下游任务上优于现有SSL编码器和医学VLM；并首次在无mask监督的3D CT上展示零样本分割能力。 / GLINT improves radiology vision-language learning by explicitly modeling sparse image-text correspondence and preserving fine-grained patch features, outperforming prior SSL encoders and medical VLMs across zero-shot and downstream tasks; it is also the first to show zero-shot segmentation on 3D CT volumes without mask supervision.

Abstract: Vision-language models (VLMs) for radiology have emerged as a scalable paradigm by leveraging image-report pairs naturally produced in clinical workflows. However, this pairing reveals a mismatch in scale: each finding occupies only a small region of the image, yet supervision is provided only at the global image-report level. This poses a central challenge: prior approaches spread weight densely across all patches rather than concentrating on the sparse subset relevant to a given query. To address this, we present GLINT (Gated Language-Image alignmeNT), a framework that explicitly models this sparse correspondence. On the alignment side, we introduce Sparsely Gated Alignment, a novel architecture in which a sigmoid gate over a separate gate embedding space activates only the patches relevant to each textual query, enforcing explicit sparsity. On the representation side, we add Dense Feature Regularization, which anchors the trainable encoder's intermediate features to a frozen self-supervised learning (SSL) teacher, preserving the fine-grained patch features that the gate relies on. The same recipe applies to both 2D chest X-ray (CXR) and 3D chest computed tomography (CT), built with DINOv3 and V-JEPA 2.1, respectively. GLINT enables zero-shot classification, grounding, and segmentation from free-text queries, and to our knowledge is the first to demonstrate zero-shot segmentation on 3D CT volumes without mask supervision. Notably, the most pronounced gains arise on zero-shot grounding and segmentation, where sparse, query-specific localization is required, consistent with our design intent. In downstream evaluation, GLINT outperforms both SSL encoders and medical VLMs on classification, report generation, and segmentation.

</details>


### [42] [Reinforcement Learning from Cross-domain Videos with Video Prediction Model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03201)
*Zhao Yang,Xinrui Zu,Jacob E. Kooi,Thomas Delliaux,He Liu,Shujian Yu,Kevin Sebastian Luck,Vincent François-Lavet*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: XIPER learns rewards from visually different expert videos by predicting how the agent would look in the expert domain, enabling stronger cross-domain imitation and sim-to-real transfer.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Learning from expert videos across visually distinct domains is difficult because there are no explicit reward labels and the visual mismatch between expert and agent observations makes imitation unreliable.

Method: The paper proposes XIPER, which trains a cross-domain video prediction model to translate an agent’s observations into the expert domain and then uses the prediction likelihood as the reward signal for reinforcement learning.

Result: On the DMC Color Suite and DMC Body Suite, XIPER consistently outperforms baseline methods under domain shifts. It also yields meaningful rewards on a sim-to-real transfer dataset using only simulated expert videos.

Conclusion: XIPER demonstrates that cross-domain video prediction can provide effective reward signals for reinforcement learning from expert videos even when there are substantial visual domain gaps, including color, morphology, and sim-to-real differences.

Abstract: Reinforcement learning from expert videos across visually distinct domains is challenging due to the absence of reward signals and the presence of domain gaps. We introduce XIPER (Cross-domain Video Prediction Reward), a reward model for learning from expert videos collected in a visually different domain, where the agent's appearance differs due to factors such as color, morphology, or the sim-to-real gap. More specifically, XIPER trains a cross-domain video prediction model that maps agent observations into the expert domain and uses the prediction likelihood as a reward signal. Experiments on the DMC Color Suite (8 tasks) and DMC Body Suite (3 tasks) show that XIPER consistently outperforms baselines despite domain gaps such as differences in agent color and morphology. We further analyze XIPER on a sim-to-real transfer dataset, demonstrating that it produces meaningful reward signals for real-robot observations given only simulated expert videos. Code, pretrained models, datasets and video demonstrations can be found on our project webpage: https://sites.google.com/view/xiper

</details>


### [43] [Follow-Your-Preference++: Rethinking Preference Alignment for Image Inpainting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03216)
*Junkun Yuan,Yutao Shen,Toru Aonishi,Hideki Nakayama,Yue Ma*

Main category: cs.CV

TL;DR: This paper studies how to do preference alignment for image inpainting using DPO and reward-model-generated preference data. It finds that reward models are useful but biased, which can lead to reward hacking. Combining multiple reward models, especially with calibration, makes training more robust and delivers state-of-the-art results, including on object removal.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The work is motivated by the need to understand preference alignment for image inpainting from first principles rather than proposing another ad hoc method. The authors aim to identify the reliability, scaling behavior, and bias of reward models used to generate preference data, and to address reward hacking caused by those biases.

Method: The paper revisits image inpainting preference alignment using direct preference optimization (DPO). It constructs preference training data from publicly available reward models and performs a broad empirical study across nine reward models, two benchmarks, and two baseline inpainting models with different architectures and generative mechanisms. It also studies simple and calibrated reward-model ensembles.

Result: Most reward models can generate useful preference signals, but some are poor evaluators. Preference data shows consistent scaling trends across candidate and sample scaling. Reward models have notable biases in brightness, composition, and color scheme, which can cause reward hacking. Ensemble and calibrated ensemble methods reduce these issues, improve robustness and generalization, transfer to object removal, and outperform prior state-of-the-art methods on standard metrics, VLM evaluation, and human assessment.

Conclusion: A simple and calibrated ensemble of reward models makes preference alignment for image inpainting more robust, reduces reward hacking, transfers to object removal, and achieves state-of-the-art performance without changing model architectures or adding datasets.

Abstract: We study preference alignment for image inpainting. Rather than proposing yet another method, we revisit the problem from first principles and reassess its core challenges. We adopt the widely used direct preference optimization framework and construct preference training data with publicly available reward models. Our empirical study spans nine reward models, two benchmarks, and two baseline inpainting models that differ in architecture and generative mechanism. Our main findings are: (1) Most reward models provide valid signals for preference data construction, although some are unreliable as evaluators. (2) Across models and benchmarks, preference data exhibits consistent trends under both candidate and sample scaling. (3) Reward models display pronounced biases--particularly in brightness, composition, and color scheme--that make them prone to inducing reward hacking. (4) A simple ensemble of reward models mitigates such biases and yields robust, generalizable performance. {\color{rebuttal_blue}(5) Preference alignment is transferable to the object removal task, where the goal shifts from open-ended creative generation to coherent background completion. (6) Further analysis reveals that a calibrated ensemble method further mitigates hacking and improves robustness.} Without modifying model architectures or introducing additional datasets, our models substantially outperform prior state-of-the-art models on standard metrics, large vision-language model evaluations, and human assessments. Our code is available at: https://github.com/shenytzzz/Follow-Your-Preference.

</details>

### [44] [IdiomX A Multilingual Benchmark for Idiom Understanding, Retrieval, and Interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02584)
*Ayman Ali Sharara*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: IdiomX is a large multilingual idiom benchmark covering detection, retrieval, cross-lingual matching, and interpretation, showing that modern contextual and retrieval-based models significantly improve idiom understanding.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing idiom resources are too limited in scale, contextual diversity, and multilingual coverage, making them insufficient for modern NLP models to learn non-compositional, context-dependent idiomatic meaning across languages.

Method: The paper builds IdiomX through a reproducible multi-stage pipeline including lexical resource extraction, large-scale normalization, controlled LLM-based enrichment, and structured validation, then evaluates four benchmark tasks with contextual transformer, hybrid retrieval, and reranking models.

Result: The dataset includes 190K+ contextualized examples and 12K+ idioms with aligned English, Arabic, and French semantics, usage labels, and metadata. Experiments show strong gains in idiom detection with contextual transformers and improved mono/cross-lingual retrieval with hybrid retrieval-reranking systems; idiom interpretation is also effective as semantic retrieval.

Conclusion: IdiomX is a scalable multilingual benchmark that advances idiom research from simple detection to retrieval and semantic interpretation, and it can be extended to more languages and figurative reasoning tasks.

Abstract: Idiomatic expressions remain a persistent challenge for natural language processing because their meanings are often non-compositional, context-dependent, and difficult to align across languages. Existing idiom resources are often limited in scale, contextual diversity, or multilingual coverage, restricting their utility for modern language models. We introduce IdiomX, a large-scale multilingual benchmark for idiom understanding, retrieval, and interpretation, constructed through a reproducible multi-stage pipeline combining lexical resource extraction, large-scale normalization, controlled large language model enrichment, and structured validation. The resulting dataset contains over 190K contextualized examples spanning 12K+ idioms, with aligned English, Arabic, and French semantic representations, idiomatic and literal usage labels, and rich linguistic metadata. Building on this resource, we define a unified four-task benchmark covering idiom detection, context-to-idiom retrieval, Arabic-to-English idiom retrieval, and idiom interpretation, extending evaluation from figurative recognition to semantic grounding and explainable meaning retrieval. Experiments show that contextual transformer models substantially improve idiom detection, while hybrid retrieval and reranking architectures significantly strengthen both monolingual and cross-lingual idiom retrieval. Results further demonstrate that idiom interpretation can be effectively modeled as a semantic retrieval task, introducing interpretability as a complementary benchmark dimension. Overall, IdiomX provides a scalable benchmark for studying idiomatic language as a progression from detection to retrieval and semantic interpretation, and offers a modular framework extensible to additional languages and figurative reasoning tasks

</details>


### [45] [Greener Than Humans? Environmental Attitudes in Large Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02741)
*Stefanie Kunkel,Tilman Hartwig,Marcus Voss,Emma K. Schütt,Angelika Gellrich*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: The paper benchmarks 31 LLMs on environmental attitudes and sustainability advice, finding that many models are greener than average human respondents but also easily steered by prompting, which makes their sustainability value alignment unstable in practice.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: LLMs are increasingly used in sustainability decision support, reporting, and communication, but there is little systematic evidence about what environmental attitudes and behavioural norms are embedded in their outputs. The paper is motivated by the need to measure and compare these embedded attitudes in a structured, policy-relevant way.

Method: The authors build a benchmark for environmental cognition, affect, and behavioural recommendations using established environmental awareness survey items plus additional sustainability behaviour measures. They apply this benchmark to 31 proprietary and open-weight LLMs, compare responses across models and against human survey benchmarks from Germany, and test robustness under different prompting conditions including persona-based prompts.

Result: The study finds that many LLMs score higher than average human respondents on environmental affect and cognition and tend to recommend behaviours with substantial potential CO2 reductions. There is no systematic relationship between these sustainability-oriented responses and model origin, size, or release context. However, outputs are sensitive to prompt context and can mirror user-specified ideological positions.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that many LLMs express more environmentally progressive attitudes than average human survey respondents and often recommend high-impact carbon-reducing behaviours, but their responses are context-sensitive and can shift sycophantically with persona or ideology prompts, creating concerns about steerability and normative reliability. It proposes a reusable benchmark for evaluating sustainability-related value alignment and argues for stronger governance, transparency, and oversight.

Abstract: Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used in sustainability-related decision support, reporting, and public communication, yet little systematic evidence exists on the environmental attitudes embedded in their outputs. This paper develops a benchmark for evaluating environmental cognition, affect, and behavioural recommendations in LLMs and applies it to 31 widely used proprietary and open-weight models. Drawing on questions from established environmental awareness surveys and additional sustainability-related behavioural measures, we compare LLM responses 1) among models and 2) between models and human survey benchmarks from Germany. We assess their robustness across prompting conditions. We find that many LLMs align more closely with environmentally progressive attitudes than the average survey respondent, exhibiting higher levels of environmental affect and cognition and recommending behaviours associated with substantial potential CO2 reductions. At the same time, we observe no systematic relationship between sustainability-oriented responses and model origin, size, or release context. However, models exhibit contextual sensitivity, controlled by persona-based prompting and show sycophantic shifts mirroring user-specified ideological positions, which raises concerns about steerability and normative reliability in real-world deployments. Our findings provide a reusable evaluation framework for assessing sustainability-related value alignment in LLMs and highlight the importance of governance, transparency, and critical oversight as AI systems become increasingly embedded in sustainability transformations and public decision-making.

</details>


### [46] [On the Persistent Effects of Lexicality in Large Language Mod](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02750)
*Hammad Rizwan,Muhammad Umair Haider,Nishant Subramani,Mona T. Diab,A. B. Siddique,Hassan Sajjad*

Main category: cs.CL

TL;DR: TL;DR：论文证明LLM表征存在强而普遍的“词汇重叠偏置”，并非稳定地编码语义；中间层尤其可能同时丢失词形和语义信息，这会直接影响下游应用。/ TL;DR: LLM representations show a strong and widespread lexical-overlap bias rather than stable semantic encoding; mid layers can be especially weak for both lexical and semantic information, and this matters for downstream use.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：现有LLM表征常被用于下游任务，但其结构可能更多受表层词汇重叠驱动，而非真实语义；这一现象与语义能力之间的关系及其对应用的影响尚不清楚。/ The motivation is that LLM representations are widely used in downstream applications, yet they may be driven more by surface lexical overlap than true semantics; the relationship between this lexical bias and semantic content, and its practical impact, is still poorly understood.

Method: 作者通过分析LLM不同层的表征，量化词汇重叠相对于语义内容的影响；使用多个对抗性语义压力测试，并结合信息论视角，进一步在摘要生成和模型编辑两个下游场景中验证影响。/ The authors analyze representations across model layers to quantify lexical-overlap effects relative to semantic content, use several adversarial semantic stress tests, connect the observations to an information-theoretic view, and validate implications on summarization and model editing.

Result: 主要结果包括：词汇影响贯穿模型深度，并在不同架构、训练设置和目标函数中稳定存在，甚至包括为语义相似性训练的模型；同时发现一个中间层区域，其中词汇与语义信号同时退化，形成对表层形式和意义都较差的过渡区；这种词汇偏置还会影响摘要生成和模型编辑等任务。/ Key results: lexical influence persists throughout model depth and is robust across architectures, training settings, and objectives, including models trained for semantic similarity; the authors identify a mid-depth region where both lexical and semantic signals degrade simultaneously, creating a transitional regime poor for both form and meaning; this lexical bias also affects tasks such as summarization and model editing.

Conclusion: 该论文得出结论：LLM表征在很大程度上持续受到词汇重叠影响，而不仅仅反映语义；这种影响跨模型深度、架构、训练范式与目标函数普遍存在，并会影响下游任务。/ The paper concludes that LLM representations are persistently shaped by lexical overlap rather than purely semantics, and this effect is consistent across depth, architectures, training regimes, and objectives, with concrete downstream consequences.

Abstract: Representations extracted from large language models (LLMs) play an important role in many downstream applications. However, the structure of these representations is often influenced by lexical overlap rather than semantic content. Our understanding of the relationship between this lexical influence and semantic content, and its implications for downstream tasks, remains limited. In this work, we investigate representations to quantify the effect of lexical overlap relative to semantic content. We consider several adversarial semantic stress tests and further connect our findings to the information theory perspective. We find that lexical influence extends across the depth of models, consistently across architectures, training regimes, and objective functions, including the models trained for semantic similarity. Moreover, we observe a mid-depth region in which both lexical and semantic signals degrade simultaneously, indicating a transitional regime where representations are poor for both surface form and meaning. We further demonstrate the effect of lexical influence on downstream uses of LLMs using summarization and model editing as a case study.
