### [97] [Making Brain-Computer Interfaces More Secure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02597)
*Md Fahimul Kabir Chowdhury,Gahangir Hossain*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper studies adversarial robustness in EEG-based BCIs and shows that a lightweight custom CNN is more robust than several popular EEG CNN baselines under gradient-based attacks.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior EEG-based BCI research has focused mainly on classification accuracy, while security and robustness have received less attention. Since EEG BCIs are vulnerable to adversarial perturbations that may cause misdiagnosis, robustness evaluation is necessary for reliable deployment.

Method: The paper proposes a lightweight custom CNN architecture for EEG classification and evaluates it on two EEG datasets under gradient-based adversarial attack settings, comparing its robustness and classification performance against EEGNet, DeepConvNet, and SleepEEGNet.

Result: Experiments on two EEG datasets show that the proposed lightweight CNN consistently outperforms the compared EEG-specific CNN baselines in classification performance under adversarial perturbations, indicating stronger robustness.

Conclusion: A lightweight custom CNN can improve adversarial robustness for EEG-based BCI classification, maintaining better performance than EEGNet, DeepConvNet, and SleepEEGNet under gradient-based attacks.

Abstract: The development of brain-computer interfaces (BCIs) based on electroencephalograms (EEGs) has advanced significantly mainly to machine learning. Although the majority of earlier research has been on increasing classification accuracy, relatively little focus has been placed on security and robustness. According to recent research, EEG-based BCIs are susceptible to adversarial attacks, which can cause misdiagnosis due to minute, well-crafted disturbances. Evaluating model robustness against such perturbations is therefore critical for ensuring reliable deployment. In this study, we propose a lightweight custom Convolutional Neural Network (CNN) architecture to investigate adversarial robustness in EEG-based BCIs. The suggested method is assessed using two EEG datasets and contrasted with three novel CNN models tailored to EEG, namely EEGNet, DeepConvNet, and SleepEEGNet, under gradient-based adversarial attack scenarios. According to experimental findings, the suggested model continuously performs better in classification under adversarial perturbations compared to baseline models, indicating improved robustness. These findings highlight the potential of lightweight architectures for enhancing the reliability of EEG-based BCI systems under adversarial conditions.

</details>


### [98] [Assessing Region-Level EEG Contributions to Cognitive Workload Prediction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02598)
*Jacob Wong,Sohan Singh,Prannaya Gupta,Jin Xing Ang,Kritika Johari,U-Xuan Tan*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: 该文证明：做EEG认知负荷估计时，不必依赖全头皮电极；额叶和额-中央区域已经最稳定、最泛化，适合构建更高效、更容易部署的系统。 / TL;DR: For EEG-based cognitive workload prediction, full-scalp coverage is not necessary; frontal and fronto-central electrodes carry the most consistently useful and generalizable information, enabling more efficient systems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究动机是：虽然EEG被广泛用于认知负荷评估，但不同脑区在不同任务、数据集和被试之间的贡献是否一致仍不清楚，这限制了可泛化、低通道、可部署的工作负荷监测系统设计。 / The motivation is that EEG is widely used for workload assessment, but the consistency of region-level contributions across tasks, datasets, and subjects remains unclear, limiting the design of generalizable, low-channel, deployable workload monitoring systems.

Method: 方法上，作者提出了一个区域级EEG评估框架：按解剖学头皮区域划分电极，只用单一区域电极提取的特征训练和评估工作负荷预测模型；在4个公开EEG工作负荷数据集上进行大规模比较，并在混合被试与被试独立两种协议下，采用与模型无关、基于性能的区域重要性度量，再用基于排序的聚合策略提高跨实验配置的稳健性。 / Methodologically, the authors propose a region-level EEG evaluation framework that trains and evaluates workload prediction models using features extracted only from electrodes within anatomically defined scalp regions. They compare regions across four public EEG workload datasets under both mixed-subject and subject-independent protocols, using a model-agnostic performance-based importance measure and rank-based aggregation for robustness.

Result: 结果显示，在所有数据集和被试独立评估中，额叶电极组相对排序比全头皮基线高约15-20%，同时使用的电极更少；额-中央区域表现出最稳定的预测效用，而后部和枕部区域的贡献在不同实验条件下较不一致。 / The results show that across all datasets and subject-independent evaluations, frontal electrode groups improve relative rank position by about 15-20% over the full-scalp baseline while using substantially fewer electrodes. Fronto-central regions are the most stable predictors, whereas posterior and occipital regions are less consistent.

Conclusion: 该论文得出结论：在跨任务、跨数据集、跨被试的EEG认知负荷估计中，额叶及额-中央区域最稳定、最具泛化性的预测价值；仅使用这些区域的电极即可在更少通道下达到并超过全头皮基线表现，而后部和枕部区域贡献较不稳定。 / The paper concludes that frontal and fronto-central EEG regions provide the most stable and generalizable information for cognitive workload estimation across tasks, datasets, and subjects, outperforming full-scalp baselines with fewer electrodes, while posterior and occipital regions are less consistent.

Abstract: Accurate and generalizable estimation of cognitive workload from electroencephalography (EEG) is critical for human-centered and safety-critical systems. Although EEG is widely used for workload assessment, the consistency of region-level EEG contributions across tasks, datasets, and subjects remains unclear. This paper presents a region-level evaluation framework for EEG-based workload prediction in which models are trained and evaluated using features extracted exclusively from electrodes belonging to anatomically defined scalp regions. We perform a large-scale analysis across four publicly available EEG workload datasets spanning diverse task demands, recording hardware, and electrode montages. Region importance is quantified using a model-agnostic, performance-based approach under both mixed-subject and subject-independent evaluation protocols, with results aggregated using a rank-based strategy to ensure robustness across experimental configurations. Across all datasets and subject-independent evaluations, frontal electrode groups outperform the full-scalp baseline by approximately 15-20% in relative rank position while using substantially fewer electrodes. Fronto-central regions exhibit the most stable predictive utility, whereas posterior and occipital regions contribute less consistently across experimental conditions. These findings indicate that workload-relevant EEG information is most consistently retained within frontal and fronto-central electrode groups, supporting the design of efficient and generalizable EEG-based workload monitoring systems.

</details>


### [99] [Testing the Test: Score-Direction Instability in Class-Split Anomaly Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02601)
*Alejandro Ascarate,Leo Lebrat,Rodrigo Santa Cruz,Clinton Fookes,Olivier Salvado*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Class-split anomaly detection benchmarks can fail when anomaly and normal classes overlap in feature space; a simple diagnostic, neighborhood class leakage, predicts when score direction becomes unstable, showing these benchmarks are geometry-sensitive stress tests rather than proof of unconditional AD performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Class-split evaluation within a dataset is commonly used as a stand-in for unconditional OOD anomaly detection, but its validity is unclear when anomaly and normal classes are not well separated in representation space. The paper aims to clarify when and why this evaluation protocol becomes ill-posed.

Method: The paper analyzes the failure mode of class-split anomaly detection under representation-space overlap and introduces a simple training-free diagnostic called neighborhood class leakage. The authors evaluate score-direction instability across Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, and Imagenette in both pixel space and VAE latent space.

Result: When the held-out anomaly class overlaps with the normal mixture, anomaly scores can degrade to chance level or even reverse direction, and the preferred score direction can vary with the unknown anomaly class. Neighborhood class leakage effectively predicts this score-direction instability across multiple datasets and representation spaces.

Conclusion: Within-dataset class-split evaluation is not a reliable proxy for fully unconditional out-of-distribution anomaly detection when the held-out anomaly class overlaps with the normal data mixture in representation space. Such benchmarks should be interpreted as geometry-dependent stress tests rather than unconditional evidence of anomaly-detection capability.

Abstract: Within-dataset class-split evaluation is widely used as a proxy for fully unconditional out-of-distribution anomaly detection. We show that this protocol can become ill-posed when the held-out anomaly class overlaps the normal mixture in representation space. In this regime, anomaly scores may collapse toward chance or even invert, and the preferred score direction can depend on the unknown anomaly class. We introduce a simple training-free diagnostic, neighborhood class leakage, and show that it predicts score-direction instability across Fashion-MNIST, CIFAR-10, and Imagenette, in both pixel and VAE latent spaces. Our results suggest that class-split AD benchmarks should be treated as geometry-dependent stress tests rather than unconditional evidence of anomaly-detection ability.

</details>


### [100] [Graph Mamba Survival Analysis Based on Topology-Aware ordering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02602)
*Yuanfang Chen,Peiqiang Yan,Yuntao Shou,Qian Zhao,Xiangyong Cao*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TopoMamSurv improves WSI survival analysis by ordering graph nodes with topology awareness, using bidirectional Mamba plus GCN for efficient and spatially aware feature learning, and outperforms prior methods on five TCGA datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that WSI survival analysis requires modeling large-scale graph-structured pathology data efficiently, but Transformers are computationally expensive with O(N^2) complexity, while existing Graph Mamba approaches are highly sensitive to input order and use simplistic sorting methods that ignore graph topology. In addition, unidirectional Mamba cannot fully exploit the bidirectional spatial structure of images.

Method: The method proposes TopoMamSurv, a Graph Mamba survival analysis framework for whole slide images. It introduces a topology-aware ordering (TAO) strategy to generate node sequences that better preserve graph connectivity for Mamba, a bidirectional Mamba module to model spatial context in both directions, and integrates GCN for hierarchical learning of local aggregation and global dependency capture.

Result: The reported result is that visualization experiments show nodes produced by the topology-aware ordering strategy have higher similarity, and the overall framework demonstrates a comprehensive performance advantage across five TCGA datasets.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that TopoMamSurv, by combining topology-aware ordering, bidirectional Mamba modeling, and hierarchical GCN-based feature fusion, can better balance long-range dependency modeling, computational efficiency, and spatial structure utilization for WSI survival analysis, achieving superior overall performance on five TCGA datasets.

Abstract: In computational pathology, Whole Slide Images (WSIs) survival analysis is crucial for patient prognosis assessment, but it faces multiple technical challenges. Although the Transformer captures long-range dependencies through its self-attention mechanism, its $O(N^2)$ time complexity causes a severe computational bottleneck in large-scale WSIs graph structures. The Mamba model breaks through the Transformer's computational bottleneck with linear complexity. But, owing to Mamba's high sensitivity to the order of input data, traditional node sorting methods in Graph Mamba, such as those based on node degree or subgraph size, fail to adequately account for the topological connectivity of graph data. This inadequacy consequently restricts the performance of Mamba's sequential modeling. Moreover, its unidirectional architecture cannot leverage the bidirectional spatial structure of images. To address these challenges, this paper proposes a novel Graph Mamba survival analysis framework based on topology-aware ordering (TopoMamSurv) to adapt to the sequential sensitivity of Mamba. Our visualization experiments further confirmed that the nodes extracted through the topology-aware ordering (TAO) strategy indeed exhibit higher similarity. Furthermore, we designed a bidirectional Mamba module and integrated a Graph Convolutional Network (GCN) to achieve bidirectional spatial context modeling of images, forming a hierarchical feature learning architecture for "local aggregation - global capture." This framework effectively reconciles the contradiction between long-range dependency modeling, computational efficiency, and spatial structure utilization in WSIs analysis through its systematic design of TAO, bidirectional semantic modeling, and hierarchical feature fusion. This framework has been validated for its comprehensive performance advantage on five TCGA datasets.

</details>


### [101] [Auditable Climate Risk Intelligence from Fragmented ESG Data: Deterministic Orchestration and Imbalance-Aware Learning for Scope 1-3 Validation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02604)
*Karan Sehgal,Khawar Naveed Bhatti*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A deterministic, auditable ESG validation framework is proposed that unifies fragmented climate-reporting data, detects drift and anomalies, handles rare events, explains model decisions, and improves governance and reproducibility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that current ESG and climate risk data systems are fragmented and existing validation pipelines lack provenance-aware auditability, hidden drift detection, and reproducibility-oriented governance.

Method: The method combines single-source-of-truth orchestration, temporal anomaly and drift detection, SMOTE-based rare event handling, ensemble learning, provenance-aware governance, and TreeSHAP interpretability, evaluated through stratified five-fold cross-validation with significance testing.

Result: The reported results indicate that the proposed framework is evaluated against multiple baselines using classification, calibration, and audit-trace completeness metrics, and is presented as a stronger governance-oriented validation infrastructure.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that a deterministic climate risk intelligence framework can improve ESG validation by providing reproducibility, explainability, and operational auditability across fragmented Scope 1, 2, and 3 reporting environments.

Abstract: ESG and climate risk data remain fragmented across heterogeneous Scope 1, Scope 2, and Scope 3 reporting environments, while conventional validation pipelines lack provenance aware auditability, hidden drift detection, and reproducibility oriented governance. This paper proposes a deterministic climate risk intelligence framework integrating single source of truth orchestration, temporal anomaly detection, imbalance aware ensemble learning, and explainability oriented governance for auditable ESG validation. To support open reproducibility, we construct and release a synthetic ESG validation benchmark calibrated against publicly reported characteristics of the GHG Protocol, PCAF, and ISSB standards. The methodology incorporates temporal drift analysis, SMOTE based rare event optimization, ensemble learning, provenance aware orchestration, and TreeSHAP based interpretability for governance inspection and audit reconstruction. We evaluate the framework against statistical classifiers, anomaly detection methods, temporal forecasting baselines, and a threshold based system using classification metrics (recall, F1, ROC AUC), calibration metrics (ECE, Brier score), and a governance oriented audit trace completeness metric measuring the fraction of flagged anomalies for which a deterministic source to escalation provenance chain can be reconstructed. Results are reported as mean and standard deviation across stratified five fold cross validation with paired significance testing. The framework reframes ESG reporting toward deterministic climate risk governance infrastructure supporting reproducibility, explainability, and operational auditability.

</details>


### [102] [Cross-Modal Contrastive Learning of ECG and Angiography Representations for Severe Stenosis Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02605)
*Nikola Cenikj,Özgün Turgut,Alexander Müller,Alexander Steger,Jan Kehrer,Marcus Brugger,Daniel Rueckert,Philip Müller*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper introduces StenCE, an ECG pretraining method that learns features useful for detecting coronary artery stenosis. It beats prior methods across settings and shows that ECGs can support early, non-invasive severe stenosis screening.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Coronary angiography is the diagnostic standard for stenosis but is invasive, costly, and only used for high-risk patients, leaving some cases undiagnosed, especially asymptomatic ones. ECGs are cheap, routine, and non-invasive, but lack established stenosis-specific biomarkers, motivating a representation-learning approach to extract useful signals directly from ECGs.

Method: The paper proposes StenCE, a pretraining framework that learns ECG-derived representations for downstream coronary stenosis classification. The framework is evaluated with multiple ECG encoders, different stenosis severity thresholds, and additional ECG disease classification tasks.

Result: Across stenosis severity thresholds and extra ECG classification tasks, StenCE consistently improves performance over prior work and across encoder architectures. It is reported as the first approach to achieve high performance on severe stenosis classification from ECGs.

Conclusion: StenCE demonstrates that ECGs contain learnable signals relevant to coronary artery stenosis, and pretraining on ECG data can substantially improve stenosis risk stratification, including strong performance for severe stenosis classification.

Abstract: Coronary artery stenosis is a common cardiovascular disease, with severe, untreated cases posing significant risks of heart attack. Although coronary (X-ray) angiograms remain the standard for stenosis diagnosis, they are invasive, time- and resource-intensive, and therefore only performed on patients with a high probability of disease based on symptoms and prior clinical tests. However, a subset of patients, especially those without symptoms, may remain undiagnosed. Detecting indications of stenosis from ECGs, which are fast, cheap, non-invasive, and thus routinely acquired even in asymptomatic patients, would support early diagnosis. However, as no reliable stenosis-specific signal has been identified in ECGs, they can not currently be used for stenosis risk stratification. To address this, we introduce StenCE, a pretraining framework, allowing stratification of patients based on features derived directly from ECGs. Evaluations across varying stenosis severity thresholds and additional ECG disease classification tasks demonstrate consistent performance improvements across different ECG encoders, outperforming previous work. The obtained models successfully detect signals for stenosis diagnosis in ECGs and are the first to achieve high performance in severe stenosis classification. The source code is available at https://github.com/NikolaCenic/ecg-stenosis-cls.

</details>


### [103] [ReLoRA: Knowledge-Reusing Adaptation for Fast Rollout of Evolving LLM Services](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02606)
*Yang Xu,Zihuai Xu,Hongli Xu,Yunming Liao,Zhiwei Yao,Xitong Fu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: ReLoRA reuses knowledge from old LoRA adapters and updated base models to quickly rebuild compatible adapters, making evolving LLM services cheaper and faster to maintain.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: When base LLMs are frequently updated in real-world services, previously trained LoRA adapters may become incompatible. Retraining every adapter from scratch is too expensive, while directly reusing old adapters often degrades quality. The work aims to restore service-ready adapters efficiently under evolving backbone models.

Method: The paper proposes ReLoRA, a two-stage knowledge-reusing re-adaptation framework: (1) adaptive LoRA initialization using Bayesian optimization to build a compatibility-aware starting point from the old adapter and the updated base model; (2) fine-tuning with scheduled regularization, starting with strong regularization for rapid recovery and then relaxing it for task-specific refinement.

Result: Experiments show that ReLoRA achieves up to 8.9x faster time-to-readiness and up to 4.6% higher accuracy than baseline methods.

Conclusion: ReLoRA can efficiently re-adapt existing LoRA adapters to updated base LLMs, substantially reducing re-adaptation cost and time while maintaining or improving downstream task performance.

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) are increasingly deployed as continuously evolving services, where frequent base-model updates may invalidate previously deployed task-specific Low-Rank Adaptation (LoRA) adapters. For service providers managing numerous downstream model services, retraining each LoRA adapter from scratch for every updated base model is computationally prohibitive and delays service rollout. Meanwhile, the simpler alternative, i.e., naively applying the original LoRA adapter to the updated base model, often leads to degraded service quality due to adapter-backbone incompatibility. To address this problem, we propose ReLoRA, a knowledge-reusing re-adaptation framework that efficiently restores service-ready LoRA adapters for evolving LLM services while preserving or improving task performance. Specifically, ReLoRA comprises two key optimization steps: 1) Adaptive LoRA initialization leverages Bayesian optimization to construct a compatibility-aware starting point by fusing information from both the previously deployed task adapter and the base model's evolution; 2) Fine-tuning with scheduled regularization first rapidly steers the adapter to a high-quality region via strong regularization, followed by relaxed regularization for task-specific refinement. This design enables rapid service-quality recovery with reduced re-adaptation overhead. Extensive experiments demonstrate that ReLoRA reduces time-to-readiness by up to 8.9$\times$ and improves accuracy by up to 4.6\% compared to baselines.

</details>


### [104] [Geometry-Aware Tabular Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02607)
*David Turtora Zagardo*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: GATD adds explicit geometric supervision between table columns to diffusion models, producing better synthetic tabular data with fewer parameters; the gains generalize across MLP, GNN, and Transformer denoisers, and are mainly due to the supervision signal itself.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing tabular diffusion models depend on implicit mechanisms to learn inter-column relationships, which may be inefficient or insufficient for capturing tabular structure needed for privacy-preserving data sharing and augmentation. The work is motivated by making these relationships explicit through geometric supervision.

Method: The paper proposes Geometry-Aware Tabular Diffusion (GATD), which augments tabular diffusion denoisers with pairwise geometric features derived from column value differences, specifically angles and lengths, and trains the model using these signals both as additional inputs and as auxiliary supervision targets. The approach is instantiated with an MLP and also transferred to GNN and Transformer denoisers.

Result: The MLP-based GATD reaches state-of-the-art benchmark results with about 3.5x fewer parameters on average, and up to 25x fewer for classification tasks. Across ten datasets, it wins 8/10 on Shape, 7/10 on Trend, and 9/10 on downstream utility, reducing Shape and Trend error by 27% and 20%. With default loss weights, the method also improves Shape on 27/30 and Trend on 25/30 architecture-dataset combinations for GNN and Transformer denoisers. Ablation indicates the main gain comes from supervision rather than extra inputs or capacity.

Conclusion: Explicit relational supervision via geometry-aware signals (pairwise angles and lengths) is a portable inductive bias for tabular diffusion, improving synthetic data fidelity and downstream utility across denoiser architectures.

Abstract: Tabular synthesis is critical for privacy-preserving sharing and augmentation, yet diffusion models rely on implicit mechanisms to capture inter-column relationships. We introduce Geometry-Aware Tabular Diffusion (GATD), which augments tabular diffusion denoisers with pairwise angles and lengths computed from column value differences and used as inputs and auxiliary targets. Our MLP instantiation achieves state-of-the-art benchmark performance while using 3.5x fewer parameters on average (up to 25x for classification tasks): on ten datasets, it wins 8/10 Shape, 7/10 Trend, and 9/10 downstream utility (F1/RMSE), reducing Shape and Trend error by 27% and 20%. Default loss weights transfer to GNN and Transformer denoisers, improving Shape on 27/30 and Trend on 25/30 architecture-dataset cells. A matched ablation shows supervision (not extra inputs or capacity) drives the gain. This shows explicit relational supervision is a portable inductive bias for tabular diffusion.

</details>


### [105] [Libra: Efficient Resource Management for Agentic RL Post-Training](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03077)
*Kaiwen Chen,Xin Tan,Jingzong Li,Hong Xu*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Libra是一个面向agentic RL的动态资源管理系统，通过全局GPU重分配和基于因果信号的rollout调度，解决长尾与分布漂移问题，大幅提升训练效率与收敛速度。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有LLM后训练中的agentic RL会产生带工具调用的长尾、非平稳轨迹负载，传统资源管理假设失效，具体表现为：少量超长轨迹主导rollout时延，rollout与training在算力/显存/序列长度敏感性上强烈不对称，以及随着策略演化轨迹长度分布持续漂移，静态资源划分越来越低效。

Method: 方法包含两个核心机制：1）周期性全局资源规划器，联合优化rollout与training集群的GPU分配，并借助弹性混合池实现轻量、非阻塞的worker重分配；2）因果驱动的多级反馈队列调度器（C-MLFQ），根据工具返回结果产生的因果信号将请求路由到异构rollout桶，而非依赖脆弱的长度预测。

Result: 在48张A800 GPU上的实验表明，Libra相较基线可实现最高3.0倍吞吐提升，并在reward上达到最高2.5倍更快收敛。

Conclusion: Libra通过全局资源规划与因果驱动调度，显著提升了agentic RL中rollout与training的资源利用效率，在48张A800上实现最高3.0倍吞吐提升和2.5倍更快奖励收敛。

Abstract: Reinforcement learning (RL) has become a standard post-training paradigm for large language models (LLMs), extending beyond preference alignment to complex reasoning and multi-turn agentic behaviors. In agentic RL, the rollout stage generates trajectories while invoking tools, producing long-tailed and non-stationary workloads that challenge conventional resource-management assumptions. Three fundamental challenges arise. First, due to the long-tail distribution, a small fraction of trajectories dominates rollout makespan. Second, rollout and training exhibit strong asymmetry in compute patterns, memory demands, and sensitivity to sequence length. Third, as the RL policy evolves, the trajectory-length distribution drifts over time, rendering any static resource split progressively suboptimal.
  We present Libra, which introduces two core mechanisms. The first is a periodic global resource planner that jointly optimizes GPU allocation across rollout and training clusters. It leverages an elastic hybrid pool to enable lightweight, non-blocking worker reallocation between stages. The second is a causality-driven multi-level feedback queue (C-MLFQ) scheduler, which routes requests to heterogeneous rollout buckets based on causal signals derived from tool-return outcomes, rather than relying on fragile length predictions. Evaluated on 48 A800 GPUs, Libra achieves up to 3.0$\times$ higher throughput and converges up to 2.5$\times$ faster in reward compared to the baselines.

</details>


### [106] [Pruning Deep Neural Networks via the Marchenko--Pastur Distribution](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02608)
*Leonid Berlyand,Theo Bourdais,Houman Owhad,Yitzchak Shmalo*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TL;DR：这篇论文提出一种基于 MP 随机矩阵理论的神经网络剪枝方法，重点不是长时间再训练，而是在极短微调预算下尽量保精度。理论上，它用“被删部分对 logit 的传播扰动”来决定是否可剪，并给出样本保持不变的充分条件；实践上，它在 ImageNet 上对 ViT、ConvNeXt、ResNet 都实现了高稀疏度下的小精度损失和真实推理加速。
TL;DR: The paper proposes an MP random-matrix-based pruning method for deep networks that prioritizes accuracy retention under very short fine-tuning budgets rather than long retraining. The theory uses propagated logit perturbation of the removed component to certify safe pruning, and the experiments show small accuracy drops plus real inference speedups on ImageNet across ViT, ConvNeXt, and ResNet.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机是解决实际部署中的一个关键痛点：很多剪枝方法依赖长时间的后训练/再优化，而真实场景往往只允许极短的校准与微调预算。作者希望找到一种既有理论保证、又能在极少微调甚至零预算条件下仍保持模型精度的剪枝原则，并且能够解释哪些权重成分应被移除。
The motivation is to address a practical deployment bottleneck: many pruning methods rely on long post-pruning retraining, while real settings often permit only very short calibration and fine-tuning budgets. The authors aim to develop a pruning principle with theoretical guarantees that still preserves accuracy under minimal or even zero budget, while also explaining which weight components should be removed.

Method: 方法上，论文以 Marchenko-Pastur 随机矩阵视角分析神经网络可剪枝成分，将被移除分量 R 的传播 logit 扰动作为核心判据，给出确定性的 data-path certificate：当扰动足够小时，剪枝会降低 elastic-net 目标，并保持具有足够 dense margin 的样本预测不变。作者还讨论了零预算剪枝、prune-restore 扩展、带加性 L2 正则的训练极限分析，以及在 iid Gaussian 条件下利用拟合的 MP 边界 σ+ 作为分层预算信号。
Methodologically, the paper studies prunable components through a Marchenko-Pastur random-matrix lens. Its key criterion is the propagated logit perturbation of the removed component R, yielding deterministic data-path certificates: if this perturbation is small enough, pruning decreases an elastic-net objective and preserves predictions for samples with sufficiently large dense margins. The paper also covers the zero-budget case, a prune-restore extension, a training-limit analysis with additive L2 regularization, and a layerwise budget signal based on the fitted MP edge σ+ under iid-Gaussian conditions.

Result: 结果上，ImageNet-1k 实验显示该方法在短短 3 个蒸馏 epoch 后即可取得较强表现：ViT-B/16 在 2:4+ToMe 下达到 83.41% top-1，仅比稠密模型低 1.70 个百分点，同时稀疏执行 MAC 降低 59.81%，并带来 A40 上 1.388×、A100 无 ToMe 场景 2.705× 的观测加速。其他结果包括：ViT-B/16 6:12 达到 83.74%，ViT-L/16 8:16 dense+permutation 达到 85.33%（-0.51 pp），ConvNeXtV2-Base 12:16 达到 86.35%（-0.37 pp），ResNet50 8:16 dense+permutation 达到 75.87%（-0.26 pp），ResNet152d CAST-conv+permutation 在约 50% MAC 下达到 81.33%（-1.53 pp）并实现 1.62× A40 审计加速。
Empirically on ImageNet-1k, the method performs strongly after only three distillation epochs: ViT-B/16 with 2:4+ToMe reaches 83.41% top-1, only 1.70 percentage points below dense, with 59.81% sparse-execution MAC reduction, plus observed speedups of 1.388× on A40 and 2.705× on a no-ToMe A100 endpoint. Additional results include ViT-B/16 6:12 at 83.74%, ViT-L/16 8:16 dense+permutation at 85.33% (-0.51 pp), ConvNeXtV2-Base 12:16 at 86.35% (-0.37 pp), ResNet50 8:16 dense+permutation at 75.87% (-0.26 pp), and ResNet152d CAST-conv+permutation at 81.33% (-1.53 pp) with about 50% MAC accounting and 1.62× A40 audited speedup.

Conclusion: 该工作表明，基于 Marchenko-Pastur 随机矩阵理论的剪枝准则可以在极小后微调预算下实现可靠的精度保持，并为不同层提供可操作的剪枝预算信号；实验显示该方法在 ViT、ConvNeXt 和 ResNet 上都能在较高稀疏度/显著 MAC 降低下保持较强精度并带来实际加速。
This work concludes that an MP random-matrix-based pruning criterion can preserve accuracy under very small post-pruning fine-tuning budgets and provide actionable layerwise pruning-budget signals; experiments show strong accuracy retention and real speedups across ViT, ConvNeXt, and ResNet models under substantial sparsity and MAC reduction.

Abstract: We study a Marchenko--Pastur (MP) random-matrix approach to pruning deep neural networks with very small post-pruning fine-tuning budgets. The main practical contribution is accuracy retention under short calibration and fine-tuning schedules, rather than a long post-pruning reoptimization pipeline. The theory gives deterministic data-path certificates: if the removed component $R$ has small propagated logit effect $L_s \| R ψ_1(s) \|_\infty$, pruning decreases an elastic-net objective and preserves samples whose dense margin exceeds twice the perturbation. The zero-budget case gives perfect pruning; a prune--restore extension models weight restoration inside a fixed sparse-execution pattern; and an additive $L_2$-regularized model shows admissible random-like components vanish at the training limit, with persistent spikes stabilizing as the MP bulk collapses. Under iid-Gaussian sufficient conditions, the fitted MP edge $σ_+$ gives a high-probability layerwise budget signal.
  On ImageNet-1k, after only three distillation epochs, ViT-B/16 $2{:}4{+}$ToMe reaches $83.41\%$ top-1 ($-1.70$ pp from dense) at $59.81\%$ sparse-execution MAC reduction, with $1.388\times$ best-observed A40 native-$2{:}4$ backend speedup for the same checkpoint and ToMe graph; a separate no-ToMe A100 endpoint gives $2.705\times$. At structured sparsity, ViT-B/16 $6{:}12$ reaches $83.74\%$, ViT-L/16 $8{:}16$ dense+permutation reaches $85.33\%$ ($-0.51$ pp), and ConvNeXtV2-Base $12{:}16$ reaches $86.35\%$ ($-0.37$ pp). For CNNs, ResNet50 $8{:}16$ dense+permutation reaches $75.87\%$ ($-0.26$ pp), and ResNet152d CAST-conv+permutation reaches $81.33\%$ ($-1.53$ pp) at ${\sim}50\%$ MAC accounting with a $1.62\times$ A40 im2col$+2{:}4$ sparse-GEMM audit.

</details>


### [107] [Building Better Activation Oracles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02609)
*Jan Bauer,Celeste De Schamphelaere,Adam Karvonen,Niclas Luick,Neel Nanda*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: This paper makes Activation Oracles easier to use and evaluate by refining training and releasing a benchmark, though raw capability gains are small.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Current Activation Oracles are promising for interpreting residual stream activations, but they suffer from hallucinations, vagueness, and evaluation difficulty due to text-inversion confounds. The work is motivated by the need to make AOs more reliable, more practical, and easier to assess rigorously.

Method: The authors improve Activation Oracle training in four ways: using on-policy rollouts, refining the conversational dataset, feeding activations from more layers, and modifying the activation injection formula. They also introduce AObench, a comprehensive benchmark for evaluating AO quality.

Result: The main result is that the proposed training changes lead to marginal improvements in core capability, while producing substantial practical improvements in output quality and user experience. A further key result is the open-source release of AObench for systematic AO evaluation.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that improving the Activation Oracle training regime yields only modest capability gains but substantial usability and quality-of-life improvements, and that the released AObench evaluation suite provides a needed foundation for more rigorous progress in scalable end-to-end interpretability.

Abstract: Activation Oracles (AOs) are promising methods for interpreting residual stream activations. However, current AOs face important issues, such as hallucinations and vagueness. Additionally, text-inversion confounds make them hard to evaluate. To this end, we improve the Activation Oracle (AO) training regime in four ways: training on on-policy rollouts, improving the conversational dataset, feeding more layers and an improvement to the injection formula. The capability improvements are marginal, but quality of life improvements are quite substantial. In addition, we open source the first comprehensive evaluation suite for AO quality, which we call AObench. Overall, we hope that our work sets a foundation that helps improve AOs and other models in the paradigm of scalable, end-to-end interpretability.

</details>


### [108] [Hallucination Is Linearly Decodable from Mid-Layer Hidden States in Quantized LLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02628)
*Aizierjiang Aiersilan*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Hidden states of several open-source 7B–8B LLMs contain an almost linearly separable truthfulness signal strongest in mid layers, so a simple single-layer linear probe can detect hallucination far better than sampling-based baselines under the paper’s evaluation protocol.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper is motivated by the need to understand whether LLM truthfulness is internally encoded in a simple and detectable way, and where in the network that signal is most prominent. This matters for building efficient hallucination detectors that do not rely on expensive sampling or large hardware budgets.

Method: The authors evaluate three 7B–8B open-source instruction-tuned LLMs under 4-bit NF4 quantization across four hallucination benchmarks. They extract hidden states from every layer and compare four detection strategies: linear probes, MLP probes, INSIDE EigenScore, self-consistency, and attention entropy, using AUROC on held-out splits to measure detection quality and analyzing which layers yield the strongest truthfulness signal.

Result: A single linear probe on a mid-network layer reaches 0.904–1.000 AUROC, clearly outperforming sampling-based detectors, which stay at or below 0.541 AUROC in the reported setup. MLP probes offer little improvement over linear probes, supporting the claim that the signal is approximately linear, and the strongest layers appear in a consistent middle-depth band across model families. First-block attention entropy also performs well on HaluEval-QA with 0.866–0.941 AUROC.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that open-source instruction-tuned LLMs contain a strongly linearly separable truthfulness signal in mid-layer hidden states, making simple linear probes highly effective for hallucination detection. It also finds that first-layer attention entropy can provide a useful complementary signal in some knowledge-grounded tasks, while weaker performance from sampling-based methods is largely due to evaluation mismatch rather than intrinsic failure.

Abstract: We investigate whether open-source LLMs encode a linearly separable truthfulness signal in their hidden states, and at which network depth this signal is strongest. Across three $7$B--$8$B instruction-tuned models (Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B, Qwen2.5-7B) loaded in $4$-bit NF4 quantization, we extract per-layer hidden states on four hallucination benchmarks (TruthfulQA, HaluEval-QA, FEVER, and a controlled synthetic set) and compare four detection approaches: linear and MLP probes, INSIDE EigenScore, self-consistency, and attention entropy. A linear probe on a single mid-network layer achieves $0.904$--$1.000$ AUROC on held-out splits, while sampling-based detectors do not exceed $0.541$ AUROC under the same protocol. The truthfulness signal is approximately linear: MLP probes rarely surpass linear probes by more than $0.01$ AUROC. Peak probing layers fall in a consistent band across model families on natural-language benchmarks -- blocks~$13$--$18$ of~$32$ for Llama and Mistral, and blocks~$19$--$25$ of~$28$ for Qwen. First-block attention entropy provides a complementary signal in knowledge-grounded settings ($0.866$--$0.941$ AUROC on HaluEval-QA) at no additional inference cost. The low discriminability of sampling methods under this protocol reflects a structural mismatch between paired-label evaluation and the information these methods access, rather than an inherent limitation of those methods. Code and data are released for full reproducibility on a single $8$\,GB GPU.

</details>


### [109] [Regime-Arrival Uncertainty in Generalization Bounds under Distribution Shift](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02657)
*Prince Poudel*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: A new regime-aware generalization theory shows that when train and test periods have different calm/crisis mixes, error comes from both changed regime proportions and model sensitivity to regimes; the bound works for Markov-switching and beta-mixing data, matches synthetic and equity-index evidence, but does not forecast future regime changes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Standard generalization bounds assume identical or static train-test distributions and therefore miss errors caused by changes in the proportions of latent regimes such as calm versus crisis states. The paper is motivated by the need to quantify extra risk under regime composition mismatch in switching environments.

Method: The method develops a regime-aware generalization framework for Markov-switching distribution shift, derives an exact risk decomposition, extends the bound to beta-mixing time series using an effective sample size adjusted by the spectral gap, and establishes a minimax lower bound; it is evaluated on synthetic data and 25 years of global equity index data.

Result: The main results are an exact decomposition of excess risk under regime composition mismatch, a beta-mixing extension with spectral-gap-corrected effective sample size, and empirical evidence that the proposed penalty correlates with realized out-of-sample generalization gaps, whereas a training-only estimator does not. The analysis also shows that crisis feature geometry is detectable but timing of regime arrival is not forecastable within this framework.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that in Markov-switching environments, generalization error can be decomposed exactly into regime composition mismatch and model regime sensitivity, and that the proposed regime-aware penalty explains ex post realized generalization gaps while not enabling reliable forecasting of future regime arrivals.

Abstract: The standard generalization bounds assume that the training and deployment distributions are the same, or are static, and don't consider regime switching environments where the ratio of calm vs crisis states is different. This paper proposes a framework that generalizes regime-aware models by quantifying the extra risk due to regime composition mismatch, when distribution shifts are Markov-switching. We obtain an exact decomposition, separating regime mismatch from regime sensitivity; we extend the bound to beta-mixing data using the effective sample size corrected for the spectral gap; and we show a minimax lower bound for synthetic data and on 25 years of global equity indices. The proposed penalty is an ex post realized generalization gap, whereas the training-only estimator does not show significant correlation: the feature geometry of crises can be detected, but not the temporal arrival. Thus, the framework is not a forecast machine. Forecasting the composition of the future regime is an open question in the rare cases of regime change.

</details>


### [110] [CL-DMDF:Dynamic Multimodal Data Fusion Model Based on Contrastive Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02659)
*Dong Li,Lingling Zhang,Binghao Han,Linlin Ding,Yue Kou*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: CL-DMDF is a contrastive-learning-based multimodal fusion framework for missing or uncertain modalities, using cross-dimension attention, centroid contrastive learning, and adaptive fusion to achieve better performance on three datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing multimodal fusion methods are often task-specific, assume complete modalities, and can overfocus on local interactions in missing-modality settings while neglecting global complementary information. The work is motivated by the need for a more robust and dynamic fusion approach under uncertain or missing modality inputs.

Method: The paper proposes CL-DMDF, which uses a dual-dimension attention mechanism over features and modalities to estimate importance scores, an entity-centroid contrastive learning module to create centroid-based positive pairs for better representation learning, and an adaptive fusion module to dynamically fuse available modalities.

Result: Experiments on three datasets show that CL-DMDF outperforms or demonstrates strong effectiveness across diverse multimodal fusion tasks, particularly in settings with incomplete or uncertain modalities.

Conclusion: CL-DMDF effectively handles multimodal fusion with uncertain or missing modalities by combining cross-feature/modality attention, entity-centroid contrastive learning, and adaptive fusion, and it shows strong performance across multiple datasets.

Abstract: Multimodal data fusion involves integrating and analyzing information from multiple modalities to uncover latent correlations and complementary patterns, thereby enhancing data processing and decision-making. While existing methods for structured multimodal inputs are typically designed around specific tasks and assume fully observed modalities, real-world applications often suffer from uncertain or missing modality inputs due to various factors. Some traditional models overly emphasize local interactions within missing modalities, neglecting the global complementary cues embedded in multimodal representations. To overcome these limitations, we propose a Dynamic Multimodal Data Fusion model based on Contrastive Learning (CL-DMDF). CL-DMDF introduces a novel attention mechanism that operates across both feature and modality dimensions to compute reliable attention scores, effectively reflecting importance at each level. The CL-DMDF further incorporates an entity-centroid contrastive learning module that constructs centroid-based positive samples from entity features to enhance discriminative learning. Additionally, an adaptive fusion module is employed to improve the efficiency and accuracy of dynamic fusion strategies. Extensive experiments conducted on three datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of the CL-DMDF across diverse multimodal fusion tasks.

</details>


### [111] [Improvise, Adapt, Overcome: An On-The-Fly Multifidelity Algorithm for Efficient Machine Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02662)
*Vivin Vinod,Peter Zaspel*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: An adaptive multifidelity ML method for quantum chemistry automatically chooses how much low- and high-fidelity data to acquire, cutting training data cost substantially while preserving high predictive accuracy.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that generating high-fidelity quantum chemistry training data is prohibitively expensive, and standard MFML methods still waste resources because they depend on predefined scaling factors that can produce redundant multifidelity datasets.

Method: The method introduces an adaptive multifidelity machine learning algorithm that dynamically queries training samples at each fidelity level, saturates predictive performance at lower-cost fidelities first, and then escalates to more expensive high-fidelity reference calculations only when needed.

Result: The reported results show that the adaptive-MFML framework performs well across diverse chemical properties, including coupled cluster energies and excitation energies, reducing data generation cost by up to 30x versus single-fidelity methods and by up to 5x versus standard MFML.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity machine learning framework can automatically optimize the composition of training data across fidelity levels, reducing redundant data generation while maintaining high accuracy for quantum chemistry tasks.

Abstract: Machine learning has accelerated quantum chemistry but is hindered by the prohibitive cost of generating high fidelity training data. Multifidelity machine learning (MFML) mitigates this overhead by systematically combining abundant low fidelity data with sparse high fidelity data. In spite of its success, standard MFML schemes rely on pre-defined scaling factors to determine sparse data ratio across fidelities, often generating redundant multifidelity data resulting in a loss of efficiency. Here, we introduce an adaptive on-the-fly multifidelity framework for machine learning that autonomously determines training dataset composition. By dynamically querying training samples at each fidelity, the algorithm saturates model accuracy at lower fidelities before moving up to more expensive reference calculations. We benchmark the novel adaptive-MFML across diverse chemical properties including the computational chemistry gold standard coupled cluster energies, and the more chemically challenging excitation energies. In our numerical experiments we show that our adaptive algorithm reduces data generation costs by up to a factor of 30 compared to single fidelity methods and improves upon standard MFML by up to a factor of 5. The mitigation of data redundancy establishes a high-accuracy low-cost pathway for sustainable cost-aware machine learning in quantum chemistry.

</details>


### [112] [AdaWeather: Adaptively Mixing Probabilistic Weather Forecasts with Logarithmic Regret](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02663)
*Saptarishi Dhanuka,Sarvesh Iyer,Manmeet Singh,Mihir More,Rushil Gupta,Dhruman Gupta,Parthasarathi Mukhopadhyay,Sandeep Juneja*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: AdaWeather adaptively combines multiple probabilistic weather forecasts using machine learning and mixture-of-experts, improving temperature forecasts and offering logarithmic regret guarantees versus the best fixed expert mixture.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing probabilistic weather forecasting models, including strong machine learning and numerical weather prediction systems, do not consistently outperform each other across all spatial and temporal contexts. Because performance is context-dependent, there is a need for an adaptive combination method that improves robustness and accuracy.

Method: The paper proposes AdaWeather, which integrates machine learning with a mixture-of-experts approach to combine multiple probabilistic weather forecasts adaptively. It also extends the regret analysis from comparison with the best single expert to comparison with the best static mixture of experts in hindsight.

Result: The method shows empirical improvements for temperature forecasting over existing approaches. Theoretically, it achieves logarithmic regret relative to the best static mixture of experts in hindsight.

Conclusion: AdaWeather provides a unified adaptive framework that combines probabilistic weather forecasts and achieves both empirical improvement and theoretical guarantees, including logarithmic regret against the best static mixture of experts in hindsight.

Abstract: Recent advances in machine learning have produced probabilistic weather forecasting models comparable to state-of-the-art numerical weather predictors. But no model consistently dominates spatio-temporally, and relative performance is highly context-dependent. This motivates adaptive methods for combining multiple forecasts to obtain improvements and robustness. While combined forecasts have been proposed in the literature, these are achieved either through supervised learning or through prediction with expert advice methods. We introduce AdaWeather, an adaptive framework that combines many probabilistic forecasts using both machine learning as well as mixture of experts to arrive at a unified improved probabilistic forecast. While traditional expert methods develop the regret bounds with respect to the best single expert in hindsight, we extend the algorithm and analysis to show our method has logarithmic regret compared to the best static mixture of experts in hindsight. Empirically, we focus on forecasting temperature, and observe improvements over existing methods.

</details>


### [113] [Anomalies in Multivariate Time Series Benchmarks Are Mostly Univariate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02670)
*Marc Pinet,Julien Cumin,Samuel Berlemont,Dominique Vaufreydaz*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Public MT-SAD benchmarks mostly reward per-channel anomaly detection rather than true cross-channel reasoning, so they are poor tests for cross-channel modeling.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Many MT-SAD methods assume anomalies are distributed across channels and therefore require cross-channel modeling, but this assumption had not been systematically tested on common benchmarks.

Method: The paper proposes a per-segment diagnostic framework to classify each anomaly segment by whether it contains univariate deviation, cross-channel correlation rupture, or both, evaluates this on eight public benchmarks, introduces a complementary timestep-level metric, validates the framework on synthetic cross-channel-only anomalies, and compares channel-independent versus channel-dependent detectors.

Result: Across eight benchmarks, the study finds no pure cross-channel rupture without accompanying univariate deviation under reasonable thresholds. On six datasets, at least half of anomaly segments show univariate deviation on 79% to 100% of timesteps. On synthetic data, the framework correctly detects cross-channel-only anomalies and channel-dependent models outperform channel-independent ones; on real benchmarks, cross-channel modeling yields no measurable gain.

Conclusion: Current public MT-SAD benchmarks do not meaningfully validate cross-channel modeling because labeled anomalies almost always include detectable univariate deviations; therefore, structurally richer benchmarks are needed.

Abstract: Many recent multivariate time series anomaly detection (MT-SAD) models incorporate cross-channel modeling, under the implicit assumption that the structure of anomalies may be spread across multiple channels. We evaluate this assumption on eight widely used public benchmarks by introducing a per-segment diagnostic framework that flags, for each labeled anomaly, whether at least one channel deviates individually from its normal history, whether the cross-channel correlation structure changes, or both. The framework shows that no crosschannel rupture occurs without an accompanying univariate deviation across a range of reasonable thresholds. A complementary metric also reveals that on six of the eight benchmarks, at least half of the labeled anomaly segments deviate univariately on 79% to 100% of their timesteps, reaching 100% on three of these datasets. To verify that our framework captures cross-channel structure when present, we construct synthetic data of phase-shifted sinusoidal channels with shared noise. Each anomalous segment is altered through one of two channelwise corruptions that preserve the per-channel marginal distribution while breaking cross-channel structure, and our framework correctly characterizes these segments as cross-channel-only. On these data, channel-dependent (CD) models successfully exploit the cross-channel signal whereas channel-independent (CI) ones fail. The CI/CD comparison of a recent SOTA detector on real benchmarks further confirms that CD modeling brings no measurable gain. We conclude that current MTSAD benchmarks are unsuitable for validating cross-channel modeling capabilities, and we call for the development of more structurally diverse evaluation sets. The code for this study is publicly available.

</details>


### [114] [Aligning Data-Driven Predictors with Allocation: A Decision-Focused Approach to Survival Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02671)
*Itai Zilberstein,Ioannis Anagnostides,Tuomas Sandholm*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Standard survival metrics can fail badly for organ allocation. This paper reframes survival modeling as a decision-focused ranking problem, optimizes NDCG with censoring-aware evaluation, and shows major empirical gains in transplant allocation utility.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: There is a mismatch between how survival prediction models are usually trained and evaluated and how they are actually used in high-stakes allocation decisions; strong predictive accuracy on standard statistical metrics does not necessarily imply good decision quality.

Method: The paper develops a decision-focused learning framework for survival analysis that optimizes normalized discounted cumulative gain (NDCG) for allocation tasks, introduces a bootstrapping procedure to improve existing survival models under this objective, and handles right-censored data when evaluating ranking performance.

Result: The authors prove that standard-metric-optimized survival predictors can lead to arbitrarily poor allocation outcomes, while NDCG provides guarantees for allocation performance. On US historical heart transplant data, their approach improves baseline model NDCG by 50-100% and is estimated to add tens of thousands of life years annually.

Conclusion: Optimizing survival predictors for task-aligned ranking quality, specifically NDCG rather than standard metrics like the C-index, can substantially improve downstream organ allocation utility and yield large real-world gains in patient life years.

Abstract: Machine learning predictors have become essential tools for guiding automated decision making. However, a major misalignment persists: predictive models are typically optimized in terms of standard statistical metrics in isolation from the algorithmic tasks they inform. We highlight this incongruity in the high-stakes domain of organ allocation by demonstrating that any algorithm relying on (even highly accurate) survival predictors optimized for standard metrics -- such as the Concordance index (C-index) -- can yield arbitrarily poor outcomes when used for allocation, failing to guarantee utility better than a uniform random selection. To bridge the gap between survival analysis and policy optimization, we introduce a decision-focused learning approach based on optimizing normalized discounted cumulative gain (NDCG), a mainstay metric in information retrieval. We establish the utility of NDCG in survival analysis by proving that it translates to guarantees on the performance of allocation. Empirically, we propose a bootstrapping approach to optimize the NDCG of existing survival models. Unlike prior work, we also address the challenge of right censorship when evaluating ranking. On historical heart transplant data from the US, our method dramatically boosts the NDCG of baseline models by 50-100%, which translates to tens of thousands of additional life years gained annually when deployed for transplant allocation. We anticipate that our framework will find broader applications in decision making with predictions.

</details>


### [115] [Before Fusion, Ask What to Keep: Contextual Calibration of Multimodal Signals](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02679)
*Jiyuan Liu,Liangwei Nathan Zheng,Wei Emma Zhang,Xinpei Wang,Weitong Chen*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: TL;DR: 这篇论文提出一种融合前的多模态校准插件，先判断各模态之间是互相支持还是冲突，再按样本和特征维度重新加权原始模态特征，从而减少不可靠模态的干扰，并在多类多模态任务上稳定提升性能。 / TL;DR: The paper proposes a plug-in pre-fusion multimodal calibration module that detects cross-modal support/conflict and reweights raw modality features at both instance and feature-dimension levels, reducing interference from unreliable modalities and consistently improving multimodal performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机是多模态信息并非总是互补：某一模态在部分样本上可能有用，在另一些样本上则会成为干扰；同一模态内部的局部特征也可能与其他模态证据冲突。因此需要在融合前识别并抑制误导性成分，同时保留微弱但有价值的证据。 / The motivation is that multimodal inputs are not always complementary: a modality helpful for one instance may distract on another, and local responses within a modality may conflict with evidence from other modalities. Therefore, representations should be calibrated before fusion to suppress misleading components while preserving weak but useful evidence.

Method: 方法上，作者设计了一个紧凑的预融合校准模块：先在摘要/全局层面比较各模态，提取跨源支持与冲突线索，再生成实例级与维度级的调制信号，直接作用于原始模态特征，而不是作用于已融合表示；该模块可无缝接入不同的序列式或卷积式融合骨干。 / Methodologically, the authors introduce a compact pre-fusion calibration module that compares modalities at a summary/global level, extracts cross-source support and conflict cues, and converts them into instance-wise and dimension-wise modulation signals applied directly to unimodal features rather than fused representations; it plugs into both sequence-based and convolutional fusion backbones.

Result: 结果显示，该方法在情感理解、动作识别、音视频事件检测和音视频情绪分类等五个基准上均带来性能提升；在模态移除、合成扰动、训练动态和特征可视化分析中，也表现出更强的抗干扰能力和更稳定的训练过程。 / Results show consistent gains on five benchmarks spanning sentiment understanding, action recognition, audio-visual event detection, and audio-visual emotion classification. Additional studies on modality removal, synthetic corruption, training dynamics, and feature visualization indicate stronger interference resistance and more stable training.

Conclusion: 提出一种可插拔的融合前校准模块，在多模态特征融合前根据跨模态支持与冲突对各模态进行实例级和维度级调制，可在多种融合骨干与五个基准任务上稳定提升性能，并增强对不可靠模态的鲁棒性与优化稳定性。 / The paper concludes that a plug-in pre-fusion calibration module can modulate modality features using cross-modal support/conflict cues before fusion, consistently improving performance across multiple backbones and five benchmarks while improving robustness to unreliable modalities and optimization stability.

Abstract: Multimodal systems often benefit from combining information across language, sound, and visual streams, but this benefit is not guaranteed. A modality that is useful for one input may become distracting for another, and local feature responses within the same modality can disagree with evidence from other sources. This work investigates how to adjust multimodal representations before they are merged by a downstream predictor. We develop a compact calibration module that compares each modality with the others at the summary level, extracts cues of cross-source support and conflict, and converts these cues into instance-wise and dimension-wise modulation signals. The calibration is applied to the original modality features rather than to already fused representations, enabling the model to suppress misleading components, preserve weak but useful evidence, and emphasize responses that are better supported by the current multimodal context. The module is designed as a plug-in component and can be attached to different fusion backbones without changing their prediction heads. Across five benchmarks covering sentiment understanding, action recognition, audio-visual event detection, and audio-visual emotion classification, the proposed pre-combination calibration strategy improves performance under both sequence-based and convolutional fusion settings. Additional analyses under modality removal, synthetic corruption, training dynamics, and feature-level visualization show that calibrating signals before fusion can reduce interference from unreliable modalities and produce more stable multimodal optimization.

</details>


### [116] [Locality Does Not Imply Reachability: Boundary Repair in Block-Sparse Causal Attention](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02680)
*Zhibo Yang*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: This paper shows that fixed block sparse causal attention can break information flow at block boundaries even between neighboring tokens. It provides theory, diagnostics, and a minimal zero-parameter boundary repair that improves the predicted failure cases.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Sparse causal attention is usually justified by sequence locality, but sequence distance does not reliably reflect reachability in the induced attention graph. The paper is motivated by the need to explain why fixed block sparse attention can fail even for nearby tokens and to provide a theory-guided way to diagnose and repair this mismatch.

Method: The paper formalizes dependency limits using structural dependency sets under fixed block causal masks and positionwise operations, proves a boundary-copy separation on a constructed K-way task with accuracy and cross-entropy bounds, derives phase-conditioned coverage functions based on source-target distance and target offset within a block, and evaluates a constructive repair, Boundary Bridge Attention, in controlled 1024-token experiments plus an external-validity probe on Qwen2.5-7B at 8K context.

Result: The theory shows that with fixed block causal attention, a target can only depend on tokens in its own block prefix, creating a boundary artifact and proving an upper bound of 1/K top-1 accuracy and a lower bound of log K expected cross-entropy on a K-way boundary-copy distribution. The derived coverage laws predict failure modes and show that sliding-window attention and boundary repair are not equivalent. Experiments confirm that Boundary Bridge Attention improves performance mainly on diagnostics aligned with coverage predictions, and an 8K-token probe on Qwen2.5-7B shows the same coverage-incomparability pattern.

Conclusion: Fixed block causal attention has an architecture-level locality-reachability mismatch: even adjacent tokens can be unreachable across block boundaries when the same block mask is reused at every layer. Phase-conditioned coverage explains these failures, and a minimal repair called Boundary Bridge Attention can restore critical reachability with zero additional parameters and targeted empirical gains.

Abstract: Sparse causal attention is usually described by sequence locality: nearby tokens should remain easy to access, while distant tokens may be dropped to reduce cost. This paper studies a mismatch between sequence locality and attention-graph reachability. In fixed block causal attention, two adjacent tokens can be disconnected in the attention graph at every depth. We formalize this boundary artifact through structural dependency sets: if every attention layer uses the same fixed block causal mask and all remaining operations are positionwise, a target representation can depend only on tokens in its own block prefix. This yields an architecture-level boundary-copy separation for a constructed K-way boundary-copy distribution, with top-1 accuracy upper bound 1/K and expected cross-entropy lower bound log K. We then derive phase-conditioned coverage functions showing that reachability depends on both source-target distance and the target's offset within its block. These coverage laws predict when a sparse pattern should fail, when a repair can help, and why sliding-window attention and boundary repair are not interchangeable. Boundary Bridge Attention is treated as a constructive witness: it preserves the fixed block path and adds zero-additional-parameter auxiliary causal edges near block boundaries using shared projections. Controlled 1024-token experiments show that gains concentrate in coverage-aligned diagnostics. As secondary external-validity evidence, a fixed-checkpoint 8K-token Qwen2.5-7B probe shows the same coverage-incomparability pattern. The contribution is a theory-guided diagnostic framework for locality-reachability mismatch in block-sparse causal attention, together with phase-conditioned coverage analysis and a minimal constructive repair.

</details>


### [117] [Filter, Then Reweight: Rethinking Optimization Granularity in On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02684)
*Yuying Li,Leqi Zheng,Yongzi Yu,Wenrui Zhou,Xuchang Zhong,Xing Hu,Jing Jin,Huangjie Yuan,Tao Feng*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: FiRe-OPD first removes bad trajectories, then softly upweights useful tokens, giving more stable and effective on-policy distillation than hard token selection.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Recent OPD research has moved away from uniform full-trace KL supervision toward selective supervision over trajectories, tokens, and signals. The paper is motivated by the need to optimize OPD at a finer granularity while avoiding the information loss and instability caused by hard token selection.

Method: FiRe-OPD uses a two-stage optimization strategy: (1) filter out low-quality rollout trajectories, and (2) softly reweight tokens within the retained trajectories so that more informative tokens receive larger training emphasis while preserving more information than hard token selection.

Result: The method is validated in strong-to-weak, single-teacher, and multi-teacher settings, and reports gains over recent token-level OPD methods, including +6.25 on AIME 2024 in strong-to-weak and +18.81 on Miner in multi-teacher setups.

Conclusion: FiRe-OPD improves on-policy distillation by combining trajectory-level filtering with token-level soft reweighting, yielding finer-grained and more stable optimization than hard token selection and outperforming recent OPD baselines across multiple teacher-student settings.

Abstract: On-Policy distillation (OPD) in large language models is shifting from full-trace KL supervision toward more selective training paradigms. Recent OPD methods increasingly focus on selecting which trajectories to learn from, which tokens are most informative, and which supervision signals are most reliable. Motivated by this trend, we rethink optimization granularity of OPD and propose \fireicon\ FiRe-OPD (Filter, then Reweight), which jointly adjusts supervision signals at both trajectory and token levels. In details, FiRe-OPD first filters trajectories to remove low-quality rollout samples, and then applies soft reweighting within the retained trajectories to emphasize informative tokens. Compared with hard token selection, FiRe-OPD leverages a soft-weighting mechanism to effectively mitigate information loss and enhance optimization stability, thereby achieving finer-grained OPD optimization. We validate the effectiveness of FiRe-OPD across strong-to-weak, single-teacher, and multi-teacher settings, and demonstrate its superiority over recent token-level OPD methods ( (e.g., +6.25 on AIME 2024 in strong-to-weak, +18.81 on Miner in multi-teacher). Our code is available at https://github.com/YuYingLi0/FiRe-OPD.

</details>


### [118] [$Ψ$-Bench: Evaluating Persona-Sensitive Influencing in Persuasive Dialogues](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02754)
*Peixuan Han,Hongyi Du,Jiayu Liu,Yihang Sun,Yutong Liu,Jiaxuan You*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: Psi-Bench measures whether LLMs can proactively persuade personalized users in realistic conversations. Testing 10 top models shows persuasion is still hard, but user profiles boost results by 18.24%.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing personalized language agents are mostly evaluated as passive responders to user preferences, which misses an important real-world capability: proactively guiding, suggesting, and influencing users in realistic conversations.

Method: The paper proposes Psi-Bench, a benchmark for evaluating proactive personalization via conversational influence. It defines three realistic persuasion scenarios, constructs simulated clients with explicit personal profiles derived from dialogue histories, and evaluates 10 frontier LLMs with and without profile access.

Result: Across Psi-Bench, most tested LLMs generate coherent and reasonable arguments, but even the strongest models still show substantial limitations in persuasion. Providing client profiles improves average performance by 18.24%.

Conclusion: Persona-sensitive proactive personalization remains challenging for current frontier LLMs; access to user-specific profiles significantly improves persuasive performance, making profile-aware influence evaluation both practical and necessary.

Abstract: Personalization is a crucial capability of modern language agents. However, current research primarily positions personalized agents as passive responders to user preferences, limiting their ability to interact with users and provide suggestions or guidance proactively. To systematically evaluate such proactive personalization in realistic interactions, we propose $Ψ$-Bench, a benchmark for assessing LLMs' ability to influence realistic users through conversation. We design three real-world interaction scenarios that involve persuasion in $Ψ$-Bench, and endow simulated clients with personal characteristics through explicit user profiles derived from dialogue histories. We evaluate 10 frontier LLMs on $Ψ$-Bench and find that while most models can produce coherent and reasonable arguments, even state-of-the-art models still leave considerable room for improvement in persuasion. We also find that providing access to client profiles yields an average performance gain of 18.24\%, highlighting the importance of user-specific information for effective persuasion. Overall, our work highlights persona-sensitive influencing as a challenging yet practical direction for evaluating and developing more proactive personalized LLM agents. Codes are available at: https://github.com/Hanpx20/Psi-Bench.

</details>


### [119] [Binary Road Surface Classification Using Machine Learning on Production Vehicle Signals During Cruising](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02762)
*Vishal Hariharan,Salar Basiri,Kanwar Bharat Singh*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper uses vehicle-signal-based machine learning to detect whether the road is high-grip or slippery, especially in normal cruising where traditional friction estimation does not work well.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional dynamics-based recursive friction estimators depend on measurable tire slip and are ineffective during cruising because production sensors cannot reliably measure or estimate micro slip. Therefore, the authors seek to uncover correlations between commonly available vehicle signals and road surface condition using machine learning.

Method: The paper compares a feature-based framework and an end-to-end data-driven framework for binary road-state classification. It uses a sliding-window of vehicle signals including wheel speeds, wheel torques, longitudinal acceleration, steering angle, and yaw rate as input to machine learning models to classify surfaces into grip (dry/damp) versus slip (snow/ice).

Result: Validation on public-road data shows that the data-driven method can correctly identify road surface condition, including in cruising scenarios where traditional methods are weak.

Conclusion: Data-driven machine learning methods can identify road surface grip state even during cruising conditions where traditional slip-slope friction estimators fail, indicating strong potential for practical friction-related state estimation in vehicle dynamics applications.

Abstract: Knowledge of real-time road slipperiness, or even better, a refined estimate of peak grip potential, is a critical input for vehicle warning and intervention control systems. Typically, friction is estimated through dynamics-based recursive estimators by calculating the slip slope; however, its efficacy is heavily constrained by the vehicle dynamic scenario. When the vehicle is cruising and there is little to no slip, these methods become ineffective due to the inability of present-day production-grade sensors, such as wheel speed sensors, and methods to either measure or accurately estimate micro slip, which is crucial for distinguishing different surfaces. To address this challenge, the correlation between vehicle signals and road surface condition during cruising needs to be uncovered using machine learning. In this paper, a feature-based framework and an end-to-end data-driven framework are used to correlate the statistics of vehicle dynamics behavior with the condition of the road surface and perform binary classification into grip, dry or damp, and slip, snow or ice, conditions. A sliding-window approach is adopted to batch a short buffered window of wheel speeds, wheel torques, longitudinal acceleration, steering angle, and yaw rate, which are fed into a machine learning module for predicting the road state. Validation results on public-road data show scenarios where the data-driven method identifies the road surface correctly even during cruising, showing promise for accurate data-driven friction-related state estimators in the field of tire and vehicle dynamics.

</details>


### [120] [Representational Capacity: Geometric Limits on Feature Representation in Transformer Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02765)
*Alexander Guha*

Main category: cs.LG

TL;DR: The paper measures how strictly transformer embeddings preserve near-orthogonality, uses that to estimate how many distinct feature directions a model can represent, and proposes a better capacity formula than the standard JL bound; the main takeaway is that capacity depends strongly on ε, and larger models appear to prefer tighter geometric organization over maximum raw directional count.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that model dimension d_model is a core transformer hyperparameter, but its geometric role in limiting feature representation is poorly understood. Under the Linear Representation and Superposition Hypotheses, features are encoded as near-orthogonal directions, so the paper seeks a practical way to estimate how many such directions a model can actually support.

Method: The authors use the embedding matrix as an observable proxy for latent-space geometry, estimate the tolerated deviation ε from orthogonality by locating the boundary between meaningful and incidental pairwise cosine similarities, evaluate this across many open-source models, compare empirical packing behavior against the standard Johnson-Lindenstrauss lemma, and derive an adjusted capacity formula where near-orthogonal packing depends on the vector-to-dimension ratio k/d.

Result: The study finds two empirical classes of models: those with high ε and weak near-orthogonal embedding structure, and those with low ε and strong near-orthogonal structure. It also shows that the standard Johnson-Lindenstrauss lemma severely underestimates trained representation packing efficiency, while the adjusted formula reduces prediction error by roughly two orders of magnitude without adding parameters.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that transformer representational capacity can be quantified as an upper bound on the number of distinguishable near-orthogonal directions in latent space, that this capacity is exponentially sensitive to the tolerated orthogonality deviation ε, and that larger models tend to enforce stricter near-orthogonality rather than simply maximize raw capacity.

Abstract: Model dimension ($d_{model}$) is a fundamental hyperparameter in transformer language models, yet its role in setting the geometric limits of feature representation remains under-explored. Grounded in the Linear Representation and Superposition Hypotheses - which propose that models encode features as near-orthogonal directions in latent space - we develop a framework for estimating how many such directions a model can support. We first establish the embedding matrix as a measurable proxy for near-orthogonality constraints across the latent space: the boundary between meaningful token relationships and incidental similarity in the pairwise cosine similarity distribution gives a concrete estimate of the model's accepted deviation $\varepsilon$ from perfect orthogonality. Applying this metric across dozens of open-source models reveals two classes: models with high $\varepsilon$ whose embeddings lack near-orthogonal structure, and models with low $\varepsilon$ that maintain it.
