In ClinicalMC, patients in the English dataset undergo an average of 5.11 clinical courses, whereas those in the Chinese dataset undergo 3.42. To assess LLM performance, we construct a multi-agent evaluation framework that includes patient, examiner, and doctor agents. Based on the benchmark and framework, we design two experimental settings -- a single-turn static setting and a multi-turn dynamic setting -- and assess three categories of LLMs: 1) closed-source LLMs like GPT5-mini; 2) open-source LLMs like DeepSeek-V3.2; and 3) medical LLMs like HuatuoGPT-o1. Through extensive evaluation, we aim to better understand LLM performance in the medical domain and support its effective deployment in healthcare.

</details>


### [169] [MedCUA-Bench: A Screenshot-Only Benchmark for Clinical Computer-Use Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03203)
*Jia Yu,Zilong Wang,Xinyang Jiang,Dongsheng Li,Shuo Wang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: MedCUA-Bench 是一个专为临床 GUI 操作设计的新 benchmark，用真实风格医疗界面和安全维度评估 computer-use agents；实验表明当前最强模型离可靠临床应用仍有明显距离。 / MedCUA-Bench is a new benchmark for evaluating computer-use agents on clinical GUIs with realistic medical interfaces and safety-aware metrics; results show current agents are still far from reliable clinical deployment.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 现有 benchmark 主要面向通用网页或桌面任务，无法充分覆盖医疗软件的特点：需要医学领域知识、界面设计与主流应用差异大、缺乏公开测试环境，并且除任务完成率外还需要安全验证。因此需要一个专门针对临床软件使用的标准化评测基准。 / Existing benchmarks mainly target general web or desktop tasks and underrepresent medical software, which requires domain knowledge, has very different UI patterns, lacks public test environments, and needs safety validation beyond simple task completion. Hence, a dedicated benchmark for clinical software use is needed.

Method: 作者提出了 MedCUA-Bench，一个面向临床 computer-use agents 的交互式基准。该基准覆盖 10 个医学领域中的 18 个临床场景，基于真实产品手册与开源医疗系统重建任务环境；每个任务包含 intent-level 与 step-level 双层目标，用于区分临床推理与界面执行；评测由确定性检查器完成，指标包括任务完成情况及 5 个临床安全维度。 / The paper introduces MedCUA-Bench, an interactive benchmark for clinical computer-use agents. It spans 18 clinical scenarios across 10 medical domains, reconstructed from real product manuals and open-source medical systems. Each task includes paired intent-level and step-level goals to separate clinical reasoning from UI execution, and evaluation is done by a deterministic checker over task completion plus five clinical safety dimensions.

Result: 在 23 个智能体上评测显示：最佳闭源模型的 strict success 为 54.2%，但在真实 OpenEMR 环境中所有模型都低于 9%；开源智能体平均仅 2.5%，最佳为 16.2%。这些结果表明现有模型在临床软件使用上的泛化与可靠性都很有限。 / Evaluation over 23 agents shows the best closed-source model achieves 54.2% strict success, yet all models remain below 9% on real OpenEMR. Open-source agents average only 2.5%, with the best at 16.2%, indicating limited generalization and reliability in clinical software use.

Conclusion: 当前计算机使用智能体在临床图形界面中的可靠性明显不足；MedCUA-Bench 揭示了其与安全、可靠临床软件操作之间的显著差距，并提供了一个可复现的评测平台以推动后续研究。 / Current computer-use agents are far from reliable in clinical GUIs; MedCUA-Bench exposes this gap and offers a reproducible benchmark for future research.

Abstract: Computer-use agents could automate repetitive screen-based clinical work, but their reliability in medical graphical user interfaces remains largely unvalidated. Existing benchmarks focus on general web or desktop tasks and underrepresent medical software, which requires domain knowledge, exhibits markedly different UI design from mainstream applications, lacks public testing environments, and demands safety validation beyond task completion. We introduce MedCUA-Bench, an interactive benchmark for clinical computer-use agents. It covers 18 clinical scenarios across 10 medical domains, reconstructed from real product manuals and open-source medical systems to capture authentic clinical interfaces while avoiding licensing and privacy constraints. Each task ships with paired intent- and step-level goals to disentangle clinical reasoning from UI execution, and is evaluated by a deterministic checker over task completion and five clinical safety dimensions. Across 23 agents, the best closed-source model reaches 54.2% strict success, while all models remain below 9% on the real OpenEMR. Open-source agents average only 2.5%, with the best reaching 16.2%. MedCUA-Bench exposes the gap between current agents and reliable clinical software use, providing a reproducible testbed for future research.

</details>


### [170] [Effect of Demographic Bias on Skin Lesion Classification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03214)
*Ralf Raumanns,Gerard Schouten,Veronika Cheplygina,Josien P. W. Pluim*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: The paper tests ResNet skin-lesion classifiers on datasets with controlled sex and age balance. Sex bias mostly comes from data imbalance and can sometimes be reduced with multi-task or adversarial training, but age bias is more persistent and consistently benefits younger patients. Domain shift also changes both accuracy and fairness outcomes.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The paper is motivated by the need to understand how demographic bias in medical imaging datasets, especially patient sex and age imbalance, affects skin lesion classification performance and whether specific training strategies can mitigate such bias.

Method: The study evaluates ResNet-based skin lesion classifiers under controlled demographic compositions generated with linear programming. It compares three learning strategies: a single-task baseline, a reinforcing multi-task model, and an adversarial learning scheme, across sex- and age-stratified settings, with additional cross-dataset validation on two external datasets.

Result: Sex-specific training data improved performance, especially for male subgroups when male samples were included. Reinforcing and adversarial learning reduced or removed sex bias in balanced and female-majority datasets but were less effective in male-majority settings. For age, all three approaches had similar baseline behavior, and performance consistently declined with increasing age, with younger groups performing best regardless of training distribution. External validation showed that domain shift substantially altered performance and demographic bias patterns.

Conclusion: Sex bias in skin lesion classification is driven mainly by training-data imbalance, while age bias consistently favors younger patients regardless of distribution; therefore, these two bias types require different mitigation strategies, and external domain shift further changes both overall performance and observed bias patterns.

Abstract: In this study, we evaluate the performance of skin lesion classification using ResNet-based convolutional models, focusing on the impact of demographic bias in training data, particularly variations in patient sex and age. We use linear programming to generate datasets with controlled demographic characteristics, allowing systematic investigation of bias effects. Three learning strategies are evaluated: a single-task model, a reinforcing multi-task model, and an adversarial learning scheme. Our sex-based analysis indicates that sex-specific training datasets optimise model performance. Notably, including male patients in the training data improved performance for the male subgroup, even in female-majority cases. Reinforcing and adversarial learning schemes narrowed or eliminated bias gaps in balanced and female-majority datasets. However, these strategies proved less effective in male-majority settings, where models continued to perform better for males than females. The two learning schemes showed marginal bias reduction compared to the baseline model in predominantly male patient populations. Age-based analysis demonstrates comparable baseline performance across the three model approaches, with performance declining across age categories. Younger groups consistently achieve the highest performance, regardless of training data distribution. Although balanced training yields optimal results for the youngest age category, performance decreases in older categories. We find that sex biases arise mainly from data imbalances, while age biases consistently favour younger groups regardless of distribution. These distinct mechanisms require targeted mitigation strategies. Additionally, cross-dataset validation on two external datasets revealed that domain shifts notably affect performance and patterns of demographic bias.

</details>


### [171] [Perceive Before Reasoning: A Pre-Reasoning Perception Framework for Efficient and Reliable Proactive Mobile Agents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03236)
*Zhijie Ding,Weinan Hong,Zicheng Zhu,Lei Li,Dezhi Kong,Hao Wang,Peng Zhou,Xuchu Jiang,Jiaming Xu*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 中文：这篇工作把主动助手拆成“先判断要不要介入，再决定怎么帮”，因此更少误报、更高成功率、也更省算力。 English: The paper splits proactive assistance into first deciding whether to intervene, then deciding how to help, yielding fewer false alarms, better task success, and lower compute cost.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中文：现有主动移动助手通常把“何时干预”和“如何帮助”合并到同一个MLLM流水线中，导致目标不一致：一方面干预过滤需要保守，另一方面帮助生成需要全面；同时在不应发声时仍会产生冗余推理开销。 English: Existing proactive mobile agents often unify deciding when to intervene and how to assist in a single MLLM pipeline, causing objective misalignment between conservative intervention filtering and comprehensive assistance generation, along with unnecessary inference when the agent should stay silent.

Method: 中文：方法由两阶段组成。第一阶段使用轻量级的Multimodal Proactive Perceptor (MPP)进行主动干预门控与上下文压缩；第二阶段仅在需要干预时激活Proactive Agent Reasoner (PAR)生成帮助策略。 English: The method uses a two-stage framework: a lightweight Multimodal Proactive Perceptor (MPP) handles intervention gating and context compression, and a Proactive Agent Reasoner (PAR) is invoked only when intervention is necessary.

Result: 中文：在ProactiveMobile基准上，PRPF相较基线显著降低FTR，提升SR，并改善推理效率。 English: On the ProactiveMobile benchmark, PRPF substantially lowers false trigger rate (FTR), improves success rate (SR), and increases inference efficiency compared with the ProactiveMobile baseline.

Conclusion: 中文：PRPF通过“先感知、后推理”的两阶段设计，将是否干预与如何帮助解耦，在ProactiveMobile上同时降低误触发率、提升成功率，并提高推理效率。 English: PRPF decouples intervention timing from assistance generation via a two-stage perceive-before-reason design, reducing false triggers while improving success rate and inference efficiency on ProactiveMobile.

Abstract: Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have substantially advanced mobile agents, yet proactive mobile assistance remains challenging because agents must decide \emph{when} to intervene before determining \emph{how} to assist. Existing systems often implement these two decisions within a unified MLLM-based pipeline, leading to goal misalignment between conservative intervention filtering and comprehensive assistance generation, as well as redundant inference when the agent should remain silent. To address these limitations, we propose the \textbf{Pre-Reasoning Perception Framework (PRPF)}, a two-stage framework built on perceiving before reasoning. PRPF introduces a lightweight Multimodal Proactive Perceptor (MPP) for intervention gating and context compression, and activates the Proactive Agent Reasoner (PAR) only when intervention is warranted. Experiments on the ProactiveMobile benchmark show that PRPF substantially reduces false trigger rates (FTR) while improving success rates (SR) and inference efficiency over the ProactiveMobile baseline.

</details>


### [172] [Solipsistic Superintelligence is Unlikely to be Cooperative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03237)
*Rakshit S Trivedi,Natasha Jaques,Logan Cross,Alexander Sasha Vezhnevets,Joel Z Leibo*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: AI should not be built only as a stronger optimizer acting on a fixed world; because deployment changes the world itself, future AI must be designed for cooperation, adaptive interaction, institutional context, and retained human control.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The authors are motivated by the concern that increasing AI capability alone does not ensure safe or cooperative coexistence. Once deployed, AI systems change the environment and the incentives of other actors, so traditional assumptions of a stationary external world break down.

Method: The paper is a conceptual/theoretical analysis. It critiques the dominant unilateral-optimization paradigm, introduces the idea of endogenous non-stationarity and the train-test-deploy gap, and proposes a cooperation-centered design framework with dynamic testbeds, institutions as primitives, and human agency as a structural constraint.

Result: The paper formulates the self-undermining property of unilateral optimization, argues that superintelligent task-solvers trained under solipsistic assumptions are unlikely to be cooperative, and identifies key research directions for building AI systems that can participate in equilibrium selection under interdependence.

Conclusion: Current solipsistic AI design is likely to produce highly capable but non-cooperative systems; AI research should shift toward a non-solipsistic paradigm centered on cooperation, institutional design, adaptive evaluation, and preservation of human agency.

Abstract: AI's central challenge is shifting from capability to coexistence. The dominant paradigm in AI research focuses on developing powerful agents that treat the world as an exogenous and stationary source of feedback. We contend that superintelligence, an extremely capable task solver, born out of such a solipsistic approach to AI design, is unlikely to be cooperative. Deploying AI systems induces endogenous non-stationarity, resulting in a train-test-deploy gap where historical distributions diverge from the deployment context. We refer to this as the self-undermining property of unilateral optimization. Closing this gap requires AI that participates in cooperation: the equilibrium-selection process through which multiple actors navigate their interdependence. We call for a non-solipsistic research paradigm that treats this interdependence as a core design principle rather than approaching cooperation as a task to solve. This entails building dynamic evaluation testbeds involving adaptive counterparties, treating institutions as design primitives, and preserving human agency as a structural feature of the systems we build.

</details>


### [173] [Do Real-World Datasets Contain Natural Experiments? An Empirical Study Using Causal Feature Selection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03251)
*Gautam Gare,John Galeotti,Michael Mozer,Deva Ramanan,Nan Rosemary Ke*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: This paper asks whether existing datasets hide natural experiments. Using causal discovery plus causally guided feature selection, the authors test whether modeling parts of the data as interventions improves prediction. Synthetic and real-world experiments suggest the answer is yes: natural experiments do exist in many datasets, and leveraging them can improve performance.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: The motivation is that many real-world datasets may already embed implicit interventions, where some individuals or groups are affected by events while others are not. If such natural experiments exist but are ignored, models may miss exploitable causal structure and underperform.

Method: The authors detect natural experiments by applying causal discovery to recover an underlying causal graph, performing feature selection based on causal links, and then comparing downstream performance when the data are treated as interventional versus observational. They validate the approach on synthetic datasets and then evaluate it systematically across many real-world datasets.

Result: The results show that the proposed procedure can distinguish datasets with and without simulated natural experiments, and across a broad set of real-world datasets, treating detected natural experiments appropriately improves model performance.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that real-world datasets often contain natural experiments, and explicitly treating affected subsets as interventional rather than purely observational can improve downstream model performance through causal inference.

Abstract: In nature, events that affect some individuals or groups but not others constitute an implicit intervention and are known as natural experiments. For example, the COVID-19 pandemic was an intervention by the coronavirus on the sub-population infected with COVID. We ask, do natural experiments occur in existing real-world datasets? If yes, how should we treat them? To detect natural experiments in data, we use causal discovery to recover the underlying causal graph and perform feature selection based on causal links. If downstream performance improves by treating the data as interventional rather than observational, we argue that this suggests the dataset contains natural experiments. We first validate this hypothesis by simulating datasets with and without natural experiments using synthetic graphs. We then perform a systematic empirical evaluation on a large suite of real-world datasets. Our results indicate that real-world datasets do contain natural experiments and we can take advantage of those natural experiments to improve model performance using causal inference. Our work represents the initial foray into this area, offering a preliminary exploration within a limited scope.

</details>


### [174] [Distilling Answer-Set Programming Rules from LLMs for Neurosymbolic Visual Question Answering](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03269)
*Thomas Eiter,Nelson Higuera Ruiz,Johannes Oetsch*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: 论文提出一种让LLM为VQA自动扩展ASP逻辑规则的方法，借助少量样例和求解器反馈完成规则生成与纠错，在多个数据集上有效。 / The paper proposes using LLMs to automatically extend ASP-based logical rules for VQA, with a few examples and solver feedback enabling rule generation and correction effectively across multiple datasets.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：基于逻辑的模块化VQA方法具有可解释性优势，但当任务需求变化时，手工适配或扩展规则表示会给开发者带来较大负担，因此需要一种更高效的规则获取与更新方式。 / The motivation is that logic-based modular VQA systems are interpretable, but manually adapting or extending their rule representations when task requirements change imposes substantial developer burden, creating a need for a more efficient way to obtain and update rules.

Method: 方法上，作者以答案集程序（ASP）形式给出一个初始VQA推理理论，并提示LLM根据新任务需求扩展该理论；同时使用VQA数据集示例进行引导、验证，并结合ASP求解器反馈修正规则。 / Methodologically, the authors start with an initial VQA reasoning theory expressed as an answer-set program (ASP), then prompt an LLM to extend it for new task requirements, using VQA examples for guidance and validation and ASP-solver feedback to correct erroneous rules.

Result: 结果表明，该方法在多种VQA数据集上都有效，并且只需少量示例就能诱导出正确规则；实验还显示该方法具有较强实用性。 / The results show that the approach is effective across diverse VQA datasets, and that only a small number of examples are needed to elicit correct rules, indicating strong practical utility.

Conclusion: 该论文得出结论：利用大语言模型从少量VQA示例中蒸馏逻辑规则是可行且有效的，可作为传统数据驱动规则学习的有前景替代方案。 / The paper concludes that distilling logical rules from LLMs using only a few VQA examples is feasible and effective, and is a promising alternative to traditional data-driven rule learning.

Abstract: Visual Question Answering (VQA) is the task of answering questions about images, requiring the integration of multimodal input and reasoning. Modular approaches that incorporate logic-based representations into the reasoning component offer clear advantages over end-to-end trained systems, particularly in terms of interpretability. However, adapting or extending these representations when task requirements change can place a significant burden on developers. To address this challenge, we present an approach for distilling rules from Large Language Models (LLMs). Our method prompts an LLM to extend an initial VQA reasoning theory, expressed as an answer-set program, to meet new requirements of the task. Examples from VQA datasets guide the LLM, validate the results, and help correct erroneous rules by leveraging feedback from the ASP solver. We demonstrate that our approach is effective across diverse VQA datasets. Notably, only a few examples are needed to elicit correct rules from LLMs. Our experiments suggest that rule distillation from LLMs is a promising alternative to traditional data-driven rule learning approaches. Under consideration in Theory and Practice of Logic Programming (TPLP).

</details>


### [175] [A Negative Result on Cross-Model Activation Transfer in a Pythia Multi-Hop Setting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03280)
*Peiyan Zhang*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: Strong hidden-state alignment does not translate into effective inference-time communication: translated activations look aligned offline but fail to help, and often hurt, the receiving model’s reasoning.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Prior work showed that language models can transmit behavioral traits indirectly through hidden signals in generated training data. This paper asks a stricter question: whether one model can directly pass useful intermediate reasoning information to another at inference time through hidden activations instead of natural-language text.

Method: The paper trains a linear translation layer to map sender model hidden states into the receiver model’s normalized hidden-state space, then injects the translated activations into the receiver during inference using additive and replacement-style interventions, with and without norm rescaling.

Result: The translation layer achieves strong offline alignment, with normalized cosine similarity around 0.97 across seeds. However, additive injection does not improve downstream answer accuracy beyond baseline, replacement-style injection harms performance, and norm rescaling does not recover usefulness.

Conclusion: In this controlled Pythia-160M to Pythia-410M multi-hop reasoning setup, representational alignment alone is not enough to enable useful causal communication between language models via hidden-state translation and injection at inference time.

Abstract: Recent work shows that language models can transmit behavioural traits through hidden signals in generated data during training. We ask whether a more direct and stricter channel is also viable: can one language model communicate useful intermediate reasoning state to another at inference time by translating and injecting hidden activations, rather than by passing natural-language text? We test this question in a controlled Pythia-160M to Pythia-410M multi-hop reasoning setting. A linear translation layer learns a strong normalized-space map between sender and receiver hidden states, with normalized cosine similarity near 0.97 across seeds. However, when the translated activations are injected into the receiver at inference time, they do not improve downstream answering. Low-strength additive injection remains near the no-injection baseline, with confidence intervals that cross zero. Replacement-style injection is consistently destructive, and rescaling translated vectors to the receiver hidden-state norm does not rescue performance. The result is therefore a scoped negative result: in this setting, offline representational alignment is not sufficient for useful causal communication inside the receiver.

</details>


### [176] [LEAP: Supercharging LLMs for Formal Mathematics with Agentic Frameworks](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03303)
*Po-Nien Kung,Linfeng Song,Dawsen Hwang,Jinsung Yoon,Chun-Liang Li,Simone Severini,Mirek Olšák,Edward Lockhart,Quoc V Le,Burak Gokturk,Thang Luong,Tomas Pfister,Nanyun Peng*

Main category: cs.AI

TL;DR: LEAP turns strong general LLMs into effective formal theorem provers in Lean by stepwise decomposition and compiler-guided self-refinement, delivering SOTA results on new hard benchmarks and some research-grade problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although LLMs are good at informal mathematical reasoning, they remain weak at generating formally verifiable proofs in Lean. The work aims to close this gap and provide a harder benchmark than saturated existing datasets.

Method: The paper proposes LEAP, an agentic framework that decomposes hard theorem-proving tasks into smaller steps, connects informal reasoning with formal Lean proof construction, and iteratively refines candidate proofs through continuous compiler feedback.

Result: LEAP solves all 12 problems from the 2025 Putnam Competition, raises one-shot formal solve rate on Lean-IMO-Bench from under 10% to 70% for general-purpose LLMs, exceeds a prior 48% specialized IMO-level system, and formalizes a verified proof for a key open combinatorial subproblem.

Conclusion: LEAP enables general-purpose foundation models to produce state-of-the-art mechanically verified Lean proofs, achieving strong results on both competition-level and research-level formal theorem proving.

Abstract: Large Language Models (LLMs) exhibit strong informal mathematical reasoning but struggle to generate mechanically verifiable proofs in formal languages like Lean. We present LEAP, an agentic framework that enables general-purpose foundation models to achieve state-of-the-art performance on automated formal theorem proving. LEAP leverages foundation model capabilities, such as informal reasoning, instruction following, and iterative self-refinement. By decomposing complex problems into smaller units, the system bridges formal proof construction with informal blueprints through continuous interaction with the Lean compiler. To provide a rigorous evaluation beyond increasingly saturated benchmarks, we introduce Lean-IMO-Bench, a benchmark of IMO-style problems formalized in Lean, with short statements yet highly non-routine and multi-step proofs across a wide range of difficulty levels. Empirically, on the latest 2025 Putnam Competition, an annual mathematics competition for undergraduate students in North America, LEAP solves all 12 problems, matching recent breakthroughs by frontier formal mathematical models. On Lean-IMO-Bench, LEAP boosts the one-shot formal solve rate of general-purpose LLMs from below 10% to 70%, notably surpassing the 48% benchmark set by a specialized, gold-medal-caliber IMO system. Furthermore, we demonstrate LEAP's research-level utility by autonomously formalizing complex proofs for open combinatorial challenges, including a verified proof for a key subproblem in Knuth's Hamiltonian decomposition of even-order Cayley graphs.

</details>


<div id='cs.CE'></div>

# cs.CE [[Back]](#toc)

### [177] [Streami: An MPI Data-Parallel Library to Compute Field Lines on GPUs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02627)
*Stefan Zellmann,Milan Jaros,Andrea Paris,Ingo Wald,Tatiana von Landesberger*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: Streami is an open-source GPU-accelerated HPC library for fast, extensible field-line computation in fluid flows, designed to plug into MPI codes for both in-situ and post-processing analysis.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Computing field lines in fluid flows at high performance is important for scientific analysis, but existing workflows need a flexible, high-performance, and easily integrable solution that works with modern GPU-enabled HPC and MPI-based simulation codes.

Method: The paper presents the design and API of Streami as a thin software layer, explains performance-oriented and extensibility-oriented design choices, extends support to multiple fluid-flow field representations, and demonstrates usage through a rapid-prototyping application with interactive seed-point placement.

Result: The work delivers Streami as an open-source, permissively licensed library with GPU acceleration, MPI interoperability, support for post-hoc and in-situ analysis, extensibility to different flow-field representations, and a sample interactive application.

Conclusion: Streami is a practical and extensible GPU-accelerated library that enables efficient field-line computation for fluid-flow analysis on HPC systems, supporting both post-hoc and in-situ workflows while interfacing with MPI applications.

Abstract: We present Streami, an extensible GPU-accelerated library for the computation of field lines in fluid flows on high-performance computers. Streami acts as a thin layer used for both post-hoc or in-situ analysis and can interface with existing MPI applications. We discuss Streami's application programming interface, key design decisions that led to Streami's high performance and extensibility, as well as extensions to support different fluid flow field representations. We also present a sample application for rapid prototyping and interactive seed point placement. Streami is released under a permissive open-source software license.

</details>


### [178] [Samudra 2: Scaling Ocean Emulators across Resolutions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02610)
*Yuan Yuan,Jesse Rusak,Alexander Merose,Adam Subel,Pavel Perezhogin,Alistair Adcroft,Carlos Fernandez-Granda,Laure Zanna*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: Samudra 2 is a faster and more accurate neural emulator for global ocean circulation. It fixes key long-horizon failures of the earlier Samudra model, improves deep-ocean prediction substantially, and extends autoregressive rollouts to higher resolutions, enabling cheaper climate ensemble experiments.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional OGCMs are computationally expensive, which restricts ensemble size and scenario coverage in climate studies. Existing neural ocean emulators have not simultaneously achieved fine spatial resolution and stable multi-year autoregressive rollouts, while the prior Samudra model suffered from variance collapse and imprinting artifacts over long horizons.

Method: The paper proposes Samudra 2, an autoregressive neural ocean emulator built with a wider U-Net backbone, modified ConvNeXt-style blocks, a reduced internal expansion factor, and a dynamic loss that adaptively reweights output channels based on prediction errors to strengthen learning for slow-evolving deep-ocean variables.

Result: At 1 degree resolution, Samudra 2 improves upper-ocean global-mean temperature R^2 from 0.56 to 0.87 and cuts deep-ocean temperature error by about seven times. The same architecture also works at 1/2 degree and 1/4 degree resolutions over roughly 8-year autoregressive rollouts, capturing mesoscale eddies and sharp western boundary currents on a single GPU.

Conclusion: Samudra 2 significantly improves long-horizon neural ocean emulation over the original Samudra by increasing accuracy, reducing deep-ocean errors, and scaling autoregressive rollouts to finer spatial resolutions, making practical large-ensemble climate studies more feasible on modest hardware.

Abstract: Ocean general circulation models (OGCMs) are essential to climate science but computationally expensive, limiting ensemble size and forcing scenarios. Neural emulators promise orders-of-magnitude speedups, yet existing ocean emulators have not combined fine spatial resolution with multi-year autoregressive rollouts. Samudra, the first autoregressive neural ocean emulator to produce multi-decade global rollouts, is limited to $1^\circ$ resolution and exhibits two long-horizon failure modes: \emph{variance collapse}, the loss of temporal variability, and \emph{imprinting artifacts}, in which velocity patterns leak into deep-ocean fields. We present Samudra 2, which introduces a wider U-Net backbone with modified ConvNeXt-style blocks and a reduced block-internal expansion factor, together with a dynamic loss that reweights output channels according to their prediction errors, strengthening gradients for slow-evolving deep-ocean fields. At $1^\circ$, Samudra 2 increases upper-ocean global-mean temperature $R^2$ from 0.56 to 0.87 and reduces deep-ocean temperature error by roughly sevenfold. The same architecture scales to $1/2^\circ$ and $1/4^\circ$ over approximately 8-year autoregressive rollouts, recovering mesoscale eddies and sharp western boundary currents. Running on a single GPU, Samudra 2 enables larger ensembles for sea-level projections, ocean heat uptake, and climate variability studies. We provide code, documentation, and benchmark resources at https://openathena.ai/Ocean_Emulator/.

</details>


### [179] [Applying Two-Grid Preconditioner for Subsurface Flow Simulation using Attention-enhanced Hybrid Network to Accelerate Multiscale Discretization in High-contrast Media](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02582)
*Peiqi Li,Jie Chen,Shubin Fu*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: Use learning to predict mixed GMsFEM multiscale basis functions, then keep the standard two-grid preconditioned global solve. This cuts offline basis-construction cost while maintaining stable and accurate pressure solutions for high-contrast heterogeneous Darcy problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Traditional mixed GMsFEM is accurate for heterogeneous high-contrast Darcy problems but suffers from expensive repeated local basis construction in the offline stage. Pure learning-based solvers may improve speed but can sacrifice stability or accuracy. The paper aims to reduce basis-generation cost without discarding the reliable numerical global solver.

Method: The paper proposes a hybrid framework for Darcy equations in strongly heterogeneous media: a learning model predicts multiscale basis functions for mixed GMsFEM to avoid repeated offline local computations, after which the global system is assembled and solved using a two-grid preconditioned solver to compute the pressure field.

Result: Numerical experiments on 2D heterogeneous Darcy problems show that the proposed hybrid method achieves better final pressure reconstruction than several representative learning-based methods, remains stable under strong heterogeneity and high-contrast permeability, and improves efficiency mainly by accelerating the basis-generation stage relative to traditional mixed GMsFEM.

Conclusion: Accelerating multiscale basis construction with learning, while preserving a robust multiscale discretization and two-grid preconditioned global solver, is an effective and stable strategy for high-contrast heterogeneous Darcy simulations.

Abstract: In this paper, we study the efficient numerical solution of Darcy equations in strongly heterogeneous media with high-contrast permeability and propose a hybrid framework that combines learning with multiscale numerical methods. The learning component is used for the prediction of multiscale basis functions in the mixed generalized multiscale finite element method (mixed GMsFEM), with the goal of reducing the repeated local computations required in the offline stage. Once these basis functions are predicted, the global system is assembled and the pressure field is computed by a two-grid preconditioned solver. The resulting method accelerates the costly local basis-construction stage while retaining the multiscale discretization and preconditioned iterative structure of the underlying solver. Numerical experiments on two-dimensional heterogeneous Darcy problems show that the proposed framework yields more accurate final pressure reconstruction than several representative learning-based methods and remains stable under strong heterogeneity and high-contrast coefficients. In comparison with the traditional mixed GMsFEM, its main advantage lies in the efficiency of the basis-generation stage, while the quality of the global solve is still ensured by the two-grid preconditioner. These results indicate that accelerating multiscale basis construction through learning, while preserving a mature numerical solver for the global problem, provides a viable approach for high-resolution Darcy-type simulations.

</details>


### [180] [A complete simulation framework for stone degradation on 3D real geometries](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02583)
*Silvia Preda,Gabriella Bretti,Francesco Freddi,Bruno Nazarena,Matteo Semplice*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: 利用摄影测量+水平集几何重建+PDE数值模拟，论文构建了一个可预测艺术品石材劣化过程的完整工作流。 / The paper builds a full workflow for predicting stone degradation in artworks by combining photogrammetry, level-set geometry reconstruction, and PDE-based numerical simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机在于：艺术品石材劣化预测既需要真实、准确的复杂几何信息，也需要能在该几何上稳定求解的数学模型，而这两部分通常难以有效衔接。 / The motivation is that predicting degradation in stone artworks requires both accurate acquisition of complex real-world geometry and a robust way to solve the governing mathematical model on that geometry, a combination that is typically difficult to achieve.

Method: 方法上，作者先用摄影测量生成物体表面点云，再进行后处理得到三维几何的水平集表示，并将该表示嵌入劣化过程PDE系统的数值离散中；同时结合合适的时间推进与预条件技术完成高效模拟。 / Methodologically, the workflow starts with photogrammetric surface point-cloud acquisition, followed by post-processing into a 3D level-set representation, which is then incorporated into the numerical discretization of the degradation PDE system, together with suitable time-stepping and preconditioning strategies.

Result: 结果表明，该框架能够在不同情景下预测劣化演化，例如大理石上石膏结壳厚度的增长。 / The resulting framework can simulate degradation evolution under different scenarios, for example predicting the growth of gypsum crust thickness on marble.

Conclusion: 该论文提出了一套完整流程，将摄影测量得到的点云、基于水平集的三维几何重建以及PDE数值模拟整合起来，可用于预测艺术品石材劣化（如大理石硫酸盐化）的时空演化。 / The paper concludes that an end-to-end pipeline combining photogrammetric point clouds, level-set geometry reconstruction, and PDE-based simulation can effectively predict stone degradation in artworks, such as marble sulfation.

Abstract: We present a complete workflow for predicting stone degradation phenomena, such as marble sulfation, in works of art. The main challenge is to accurately acquire the geometry of the artwork and then use it to perform simulations based on a mathematical model of the degradation process, typically formulated as a system of partial differential equations (PDEs).
  To address this, we generate a point cloud of the object surface using photogrammetric techniques and subsequently post-process it to obtain a level-set description of the three-dimensional geometry. This representation is then incorporated into the numerical discretization of the PDE system. Combined with suitable time-stepping and preconditioning strategies, the resulting framework enables the prediction of degradation evolution, such as the growth of gypsum crust thickness on marble, under different scenarios.

</details>


### [181] [An improved PINN framework integrating localized collocation scheme and PIKF](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02585)
*Qiang Xi,Wenzhi Xu,Mario Cvetkovic,Dragan Poljak,Timon Rabczuk,Zhuojia Fu*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: 中文：这篇论文提出 LPIKFNN，用“局部配点 + 物理信息核函数”替代 PINN 中昂贵的自动微分，使 PDE 训练在复杂、高振荡和异质问题上更快、更准。 English: The paper proposes LPIKFNN, which replaces expensive automatic differentiation in PINNs with localized collocation and physics-informed kernel functions, making training faster and more accurate for complex, oscillatory, and heterogeneous PDE problems.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 中文：传统 PINN 在高阶导数和高波数问题中计算代价高、训练困难，PIKFNN 在异质问题中又受制于全局 PIKF 难以获得，因此需要一种兼具高效、准确且适用于异质场景的新框架。 English: Conventional PINNs struggle with costly derivative evaluation and poor training behavior in high-order and high-wavenumber settings, while PIKFNN is bottlenecked in heterogeneous problems due to dependence on global PIKFs. The work is motivated by the need for a more efficient and broadly applicable framework.

Method: 中文：方法上，作者用局部配点方案在局部区域离散物理量，并将物理场表示为 PIKF 的线性组合；再训练多层感知机迭代学习物理量。损失函数由 PIKF 与局部配点构建，而不是通过自动微分显式计算 governing equations 所需导数。 English: The method discretizes physical quantities in local subdomains with a localized collocation scheme, represents the field as a linear combination of PIKFs, and trains an MLP to iteratively learn the solution. The loss is built from PIKFs and localized collocation instead of enforcing PDEs via automatic differentiation.

Result: 中文：基准实验覆盖高波数、高阶导数、非线性、异质问题以及基于势函数的逆向肌电图任务；LPIKFNN 的数值结果与解析解和实验测量高度一致，显示出优异的可行性与精度。 English: Across benchmarks including high-wavenumber, higher-order derivative, nonlinear, heterogeneous, and potential-based inverse electromyography problems, LPIKFNN matches analytical solutions and experimental measurements closely, demonstrating strong feasibility and accuracy.

Conclusion: 中文：LPIKFNN 通过局部配点与物理信息核函数构造损失，避免高代价自动微分，在高波数、高阶导数、异质与非线性问题上同时提升效率、精度与适用性。 English: LPIKFNN improves PINN by combining localized collocation with physics-informed kernel functions, removing expensive automatic differentiation and achieving better efficiency, accuracy, and robustness on high-wavenumber, high-order, heterogeneous, and nonlinear problems.

Abstract: We propose a localized physics-informed kernel function neural network (LPIKFNN), which is an improved physics-informed neural network (PINN) based on physics-informed kernel function (PIKF). In the LPIKFNN framework, the localized collocation scheme discretizes the physical quantities within the local domain, where the physical field is represented as a linear combination of PIKFs. Based on this representation, the multilayer perceptron is trained to iteratively learn the physical quantities. To overcome the computational challenges of conventional PINN in higher-order derivative and high wavenumber problems, the LPIKFNN constructs the loss function using the PIKF and a localized collocation scheme rather than relying on automatic differentiation. As a result, the costly derivative evaluations required to enforce governing equations during iterative training are eliminated, leading to significantly improved computational efficiency and training performance. Moreover, incorporating PIKFs into the loss function enables the proposed LPIKFNN to significantly improve computational accuracy in high-wavenumber problems characterized by highly oscillatory physical fields. To overcome the computational bottleneck of the physics-informed kernel function neural network (PIKFNN) in heterogeneous problems, the LPIKFNN introduces a localized collocation scheme that removes reliance on global PIKFs, enabling accurate predictions where global PIKFs are unavailable. The feasibility and accuracy of the proposed LPIKFNN are demonstrated through a series of benchmark studies, including high wavenumber problems, higher-order derivative problems, nonlinear problems, heterogeneous problems, and potential-based inverse electromyography. The numerical predictions obtained by LPIKFNN show excellent agreement with available analytical solutions and experimental measurements.

</details>


### [182] [Margin Play: A Multi-Agent System For Public Policy Analysis In The Brazilian Equatorial Margin](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.02614)
*Antonio de Sousa Leitão Filho,Fabrício Saul Lima,Selby Mykael Lima dos Santos,Rejani Bandeira Vieira Sousa,Luís Jorge Mesquita de Jesus,Dennys Correia da Silva,Allan Kardec Duailibe Barros Filho*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: 这篇论文用多智能体强化学习模拟巴西赤道边缘海域开发中的政府、企业、监管者与社区博弈，发现决定Maranhao是否受益的关键不是“开不开发”，而是“以什么制度开发”。 / This paper uses multi-agent reinforcement learning to simulate strategic interactions among government, firms, regulators, and communities in BEM oil exploration, showing that the key issue is not simply whether to develop, but under what policy regime development occurs.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 研究动机来自巴西赤道边缘海域即将成为新的海上油气前沿，但其财政和领土收益主要关联到Maranhao这一HDI较低州，因此核心问题是：在多方利益冲突与监管目标不一致的条件下，勘探如何真正转化为地方净福利。 / The motivation is that the Brazilian Equatorial Margin is becoming a new offshore oil frontier, while its fiscal and territorial linkages fall mainly on Maranhao, a low-HDI state; the key question is how exploration can translate into net local welfare under conflicting stakeholder incentives and regulatory mandates.

Method: 论文提出Margin Play，一个基于CTDE范式的多智能体强化学习系统，设置六个代理人，并使用BRO-MARL进行训练；模型按巴西经验数据与经典经济学文献进行校准，在六种情景下运行60,000个训练回合进行比较。 / The paper introduces Margin Play, a multi-agent reinforcement learning system under the CTDE paradigm, with six agents trained via BRO-MARL; it is calibrated using Brazilian empirical data and classical economic literature, and evaluated over 60,000 episodes across six scenarios.

Result: 60,000回合、六种情景的结果表明：在基准制度下，福利增益很有限（Waval约1.68）；而在MA-Prospero情景下，福利提升17.5%，社区收益提升21.3%，且环境负债更低（Eamb 0.048 对比 0.076）。 / Results over 60,000 episodes and six scenarios show that under the reference baseline the welfare gain is only marginal (Waval about 1.68); under the MA-Prospero regime, welfare increases by 17.5%, community revenue by 21.3%, and environmental liability is lower (Eamb 0.048 vs. 0.076).

Conclusion: BEM勘探是否能为Maranhao带来净正外部性，并不取决于单纯的产量提升，而主要取决于与勘探绑定的制度与公共政策安排；在更优制度配置下，可以同时提升福利与社区收益并降低环境负债。 / Whether BEM exploration generates net positive externalities for Maranhao depends less on production itself and more on the institutional and public-policy regime attached to exploration; under a better regime, welfare and community returns can rise while environmental liability falls.

Abstract: The Brazilian Equatorial Margin (BEM) is Brazil's next offshore oil frontier, with operations expected to begin in 2026 in the Foz do Amazonas basin. Its assets are fiscally and territorially linked primarily to Maranhao -- the state with the lowest HDI in the Federation (0.676, IBGE 2022). This raises the central policy question: under what conditions does BEM exploration generate net positive externalities for Maranhao? The problem is intrinsically multi-agent: the Federal Government seeks revenue and energy security; the state seeks regional welfare under constitutional royalty earmarking; the operator maximizes profit under risk; ANP and IBAMA hold conflicting mandates; and Amazonian communities prioritize territorial and environmental vectors over monetary income. We present Margin Play, a Multi-Agent Reinforcement Learning (MARL) system simulating these tensions under Brazilian empirical calibration and classical economic literature. It implements six agents under the CTDE paradigm, trained with BRO-MARL. Results from 60,000 episodes across six scenarios indicate the answer is conditional on the institutional regime: under the reference baseline, the welfare gain is marginal (Waval approx. 1.68), whereas the MA-Prospero configuration yields Delta W = +17.5% and Delta Rcom = +21.3%, with a lower environmental liability (Eamb = 0.048 vs. 0.076). The fundamental problem is not a trade-off between production and welfare, but the choice of public policy regime linked to exploration.

</details>


### [183] [Critical evaluation of PINN for FWD inverse analysis and differentiable FEM as an alternative](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03210)
*Yongjin Choi,Hyeonbin Moon,Seunghwa Ryu*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: For multilayer pavement FWD backcalculation, PINNs struggle with discontinuous layer properties, and even XPINN is fragile under tuning and noise. DiffFEM performs better overall, making it the preferred option when a differentiable FEM forward solver is available.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Although automatic-differentiation-based inverse methods such as PINNs and differentiable programming are promising for gradient accuracy and convergence, their suitability for FWD pavement backcalculation had not been systematically examined, especially for layered systems with discontinuous material properties.

Method: The paper evaluates inverse analysis methods for multilayer pavement FWD backcalculation using a synthetic benchmark. It compares standard PINN, XPINN with domain decomposition, and differentiable finite element method (DiffFEM) in terms of their ability to recover layer moduli under discontinuities and measurement noise.

Result: Standard PINN fails to recover layer moduli accurately because of sharp discontinuities across pavement layers. XPINN improves performance on discontinuous domains, but remains sensitive to loss weighting, network design, and noise. DiffFEM consistently provides more accurate, stable, and efficient inversion results across the benchmark.

Conclusion: DiffFEM is a more practical and reliable approach than PINN-based methods for FWD backcalculation in multilayer pavements, because it enforces physics as a hard constraint and therefore delivers better accuracy, robustness to noise, and computational efficiency.

Abstract: Automatic-differentiation-based inverse analysis methods, including physics-informed neural networks (PINNs) and differentiable programming, have recently shown great promise due to their ability to compute accurate gradients and convergence efficiency. However, their applicability to falling weight deflectometer (FWD) backcalculation remains unexplored. This study critically evaluates PINN-based inverse analysis for a multilayer pavement system and investigates differentiable finite element method (DiffFEM) as an alternative based on a synthetic benchmark. The standard PINN does not recover layer moduli because of the sharp domain discontinuities inherent to layered pavement systems. Although we use an extended PINN with domain decomposition (XPINN), which shows better performance on discontinuous domains, its performance remains highly sensitive to loss weighting and network architecture, and degrades under measurement noise. By contrast, DiffFEM consistently achieves more accurate, stable, and computationally efficient inversion results. These results indicate that DiffFEM, which enforces the governing physics as a hard constraint, yields better accuracy, robustness, and computational efficiency than PINN-based approaches, in which the governing physics is imposed as a soft constraint through the loss function. More broadly, the findings suggest that the choice between PINN- and DiffFEM-based inverse analysis needs careful consideration, with DiffFEM offering practical advantages when an efficient and robust differentiable forward solver is available.

</details>


### [184] [GROSS: German Rail Open-Source SUMO Scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03282)
*Juri Penell,Damian Dailisan*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: GROSS is an open pipeline that turns OSM and GTFS data into large-scale SUMO rail simulations more reliably than existing methods. Its topology-aware station mapping and routing repair greatly reduce teleportation artefacts and improve delay realism, enabling Germany-scale open rail simulation.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Existing open SUMO scenarios and toolchains focus mainly on road traffic, while rail is underrepresented despite its importance in public transport and its vulnerability to network-wide disruptions. Existing rail conversion pipelines also suffer from geometry-only stop-to-track matching and inconsistent assignments, causing routing anomalies and unstable simulations with teleportation artefacts.

Method: The paper builds the German Rail Open-Source Scenario (GROSS), combining OpenStreetMap railway infrastructure with GTFS schedules to generate SUMO rail scenarios. Its core method is topology-aware stop mapping via a hierarchical station model, followed by station-level routing, validation, and targeted repair to fix inconsistent platform/track assignments and routing issues.

Result: Across several German regions, GROSS reduces average teleportations per vehicle by 1.7x to 76.8x and shortens delays relative to the vanilla SUMO pipeline. It also enables end-to-end generation of a Germany-wide scenario with 35,925 trips for comparison against operator-reported delay statistics.

Conclusion: GROSS is an open, scalable pipeline for generating national-scale rail scenarios in SUMO from OpenStreetMap and GTFS. By using topology-aware stop mapping and station-level routing repair, it substantially improves simulation stability and supports country-scale delay propagation studies, though timetable metadata and dispatch modeling remain limiting factors.

Abstract: Microscopic simulation enables reproducible evaluation in intelligent transportation systems, yet most open SUMO scenarios and toolchains remain road-traffic centric, leaving rail underrepresented despite its importance for public transport and its sensitivity to network-wide disruptions. We present the German Rail Open-Source Scenario (GROSS), an open pipeline that combines OpenStreetMap railway infrastructure with GTFS schedules to generate nation-scale rail scenarios for SUMO (Simulation of Urban MObility). Existing conversions often rely on geometry-only stop-to-track matching and inconsistent platform/track assignments, which can create routing anomalies and unstable simulations dominated by teleportation artefacts. GROSS addresses this with topology-aware stop mapping via a hierarchical station model, followed by station-level routing with validation and targeted repair. Across multiple German regions, GROSS reduces average teleportations per vehicle by a factor of 1.7--76.8$\times$, shortens delays compared to the vanilla SUMO pipeline, and it enables end-to-end generation of a Germany-wide scenario with 35\,925 trips for comparisons with operator-reported delay statistics. While the remaining long delays highlight limitations in available timetable metadata and rail dispatch modeling, GROSS lowers the barrier to building scalable, fully open rail simulations and to studying delay propagation at country scale.

</details>


### [185] [Cost of Manipulation in AMM-Based Oracles](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03548)
*Sebastian Müller,Nordine Moumeni,Adel Messaoudi*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: TL;DR: 这篇论文研究如何让AMM链上预言机更难被操纵。核心指标是“把预言机价格推偏一定幅度，攻击者最少要亏多少钱”。结论是：多数情况下按流动性给各池子加权最稳健，尤其是加权中位数；加权均值在小幅操纵下还行，但大幅操纵时更脆弱。若套利者能迅速跨池纠偏，操纵难度主要取决于系统总流动性。论文还把结果扩展到多资产结构，并考虑时间窗口和限速等现实机制。


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: 动机/Motivation: AMM链上价格预言机容易受到策略性交易操纵，而协议设计者需要可量化、可比较的标准来选择聚合规则和参数，以提高预言机抗攻击能力。

Method: 方法/Method: 论文采用EMH视角，将操纵成本定义为攻击者为使预言机偏离目标倍数所需承担的最小盯市损失；在独立CPMM中推导单池闭式操纵公式，并求解攻击者与预言机设计者在加权均值、加权中位数下的博弈；进一步扩展到带跨池套利的无摩擦CPMM模型、多资产星型架构，并加入dwell time与rate limit进行实践化分析。

Result: 结果/Result: 论文给出了独立CPMM下的闭式操纵成本表达；证明了加权中位数采用流动性权重可最大化最小操纵成本，加权均值在小扭曲时也是如此，但大扭曲时更脆弱且最优权重依赖目标扭曲；在跨池套利下，操纵成本与总流动性深度一致并在对称聚合器间相同；多资产星型结构下结论延续；加入停留时间和速率限制后，可将理论指标转化为实际预言机容量设计准则。

Conclusion: 结论/Conclusion: 在AMM链上预言机的操纵-设计博弈中，流动性加权通常最优地提高操纵成本；对加权中位数，这一结论对任意目标扭曲都成立；对加权均值，仅在小扭曲局部成立，而大扭曲下不存在对所有扭曲水平都一致最优的单一权重。存在跨池套利时，操纵成本主要由总报价深度决定。

Abstract: We study the robustness of AMM-based on-chain price oracles to strategic manipulation. An attacker trades against constant product automated market makers (CPMMs) to distort an on-chain oracle, arbitrageurs restore cross-pool and cross-venue consistency, and an oracle designer chooses how to aggregate pool quotes.
  Taking an efficient-market-hypothesis (EMH) view of the off-chain "true" price, we define the \emph{cost of manipulation} as the minimal mark-to-market loss that an attacker must incur to move the oracle by a given multiplicative factor. For independent CPMMs, we derive closed-form single-pool manipulation formulas and solve the attacker-designer game for weighted means and weighted medians, showing that liquidity weights maximize the minimum cost of manipulation within these classes for weighted medians (for any distortion level) and, for weighted means, locally as the distortion tends to zero. For larger distortions, weighted means become more fragile: optimal weights can depend on the target distortion and no single choice is uniformly optimal across distortion levels. In a frictionless CPMM model with cross-pool arbitrage, the manipulation cost depends only on the total quote depth and coincides across symmetric aggregators.
  We extend this framework to multi-asset star architectures, confirming that liquidity weights remain optimal in the same sense. Finally, we bridge theory and practice by incorporating dwell times and rate limits, providing a quantitative yardstick to size oracles against the explicit economic costs of attack.

</details>


### [186] [Physics-Informed Single Atom Matching Pursuit: Guided-Waves Wavenumbers and Propagation Distance Estimation for Damage Localization in Structural Health Monitoring](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.03933)
*Sebastian Rodriguez,Borja Ferrandiz,Francisco Chinesta,Nazih Mechbal,Marc Rébillat*

Main category: cs.CE

TL;DR: PISAMP uses wave physics to separate complex guided-wave SHM signals into meaningful components, making damage-related features easier to identify and enabling interpretable, efficient damage localization.


<details>
  <summary>Details</summary>
Motivation: Conventional guided-wave SHM with bonded PZT transducers excites multiple dispersive modes simultaneously, making signals difficult to interpret and limiting reliable damage monitoring and localization. A more interpretable and computationally efficient method is needed.

Method: The method is PISAMP, a physics-informed signal decomposition framework for guided-wave structural health monitoring. It embeds wave-propagation constraints into a low-dimensional matching-pursuit style representation to identify physically meaningful atoms and recover modal wavenumber functions and actuator-damage-sensor propagation distances.

Result: The proposed method directly extracts key physical features from GW signals, especially source-damage-sensor distances, and these features can then be used with established elliptical localization methods to locate damage in thin-walled structures.

Conclusion: The paper concludes that Physics-Informed Single Atom Matching Pursuit (PISAMP) can decompose guided-wave SHM signals into a compact, physically interpretable representation, enabling extraction of modal properties and propagation distances that support practical damage localization via elliptical localization.

Abstract: Structural Health Monitoring (SHM) aims at the real-time monitoring of the integrity of engineering structures, with Guided-waves (GWs) providing high sensitivity to damage presence and to ageing effects for thin-walled components. In conventional GW-based SHM, a bonded piezoelectric transducer (PZT) emits a short tone burst that produces an Initial Wave Packet (IWP) propagating through the structure. As this packet interacts with boundaries and potential damages, additional scattered wave packets are produced. A major limitation of such approaches lies in the simultaneous excitation of multiple dispersive GW modes by a single PZT, which significantly complicates signal interpretation and damage monitoring. In this context, this work proposes the Physics-Informed Single Atom Matching Pursuit (PISAMP) method, a signal decomposition method grounded in the physical principles governing wave propagation. In contrast with purely data-driven or numerically intensive techniques, the proposed approach embeds strong physical constraints into a low-dimensional and computationally efficient signal representation. This formulation enables the direct identification of key physically meaningful features, including modal wavenumber functions and propagation distances between actuator, damage and sensors. These extracted features, especially source-damage-sensor distances, allows to subsequently perform damage location using well established Elliptical Localization techniques. The principal novelty of this study lies in integrating wave propagation physics into a compact signal decomposition framework and developing an interpretable damage localization methodology for GW-SHM applications.

</details>
